=== [1:12] 어제 내 생파를 했어? 진짜? ===
meaning: I had my birthday party yesterday? Really?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly surprised.
--- word ---
term: 어제
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: yesterday
notes: A common adverb indicating past time. Used casually here.
--- word ---
term: 내
type: determiner/pronoun
breakdown: 나 (I) + 의 (possessive particle)
meaning: my
notes: Contraction of 나의 (na-ui). Used informally. '나' is the informal first-person pronoun.
--- word ---
term: 생파를
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 생파 (birthday party) + 를 (object particle)
meaning: birthday party
notes: 생파 (saengpa) is a common colloquial contraction of 생일 파티 (saengil pati), meaning 'birthday party'. The object particle 를 (reul) indicates that 'birthday party' is the direct object of the verb.
--- word ---
term: 했어?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 하다 (to do) + -았어? (past tense informal interrogative ending)
meaning: did (you/I) do?
notes: This is the informal, casual past tense interrogative form of 하다 (hada, to do). The subject is often omitted in Korean when clear from context. Here, Hyejin is likely asking herself or confirming with fans if 'she' had her party yesterday, perhaps playfully feigning forgetfulness.
--- word ---
term: 진짜?
type: adverb/interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: really?
notes: A common informal expression used to express surprise, disbelief, or to confirm something. Can also be used as an adverb meaning 'really' or 'truly'.

=== [1:20] 점심시간에.. 지금 점심시간이구나 ===
meaning: At lunchtime... it's lunchtime now, isn't it?
tone: Informal, reflective, self-realizing.
--- word ---
term: 점심시간에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 점심시간 (lunchtime) + 에 (locative particle)
meaning: at lunchtime
notes: 점심시간 (jeomsim-sigan) means 'lunchtime'. 에 (e) is a locative particle indicating time or place, here meaning 'at'.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: Indicates the present moment. Often contrasted with 이제 (ije), which means 'from now on' or 'by now'. 지금 refers to the immediate present.
--- word ---
term: 점심시간이구나
type: noun + verb ending
breakdown: 점심시간 (lunchtime) + 이다 (to be) + -구나 (exclamatory ending)
meaning: it's lunchtime, I see / oh, it's lunchtime
notes: 이다 (ida) is the informal copula 'to be'. -구나 (-guna) is an informal exclamatory ending used when the speaker realizes something new or expresses mild surprise/realization. It conveys a sense of 'Oh, I see!' or 'So that's how it is!'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)구나
meaning: An informal exclamatory ending used to express realization, discovery, or mild surprise about a fact. It's often used when the speaker observes or confirms something for the first time.
example: 맛있구나! (It's delicious, I see!)
level: Beginner

=== [1:29] 어쩐지 지금 얼른 하고 싶더라 ===
meaning: No wonder I wanted to do it quickly now.
tone: Informal, reflective, connecting thoughts.
--- word ---
term: 어쩐지
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: no wonder, somehow, for some reason
notes: Used when the speaker realizes the reason behind a previous feeling or event. It connects a current understanding to a past observation or intuition.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: Refers to the immediate present. See notes for Segment 1.
--- word ---
term: 얼른
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: quickly, soon, promptly
notes: Indicates a desire for speed or promptness. Often used with verbs of action.
--- word ---
term: 하고
type: verb stem + connective ending
breakdown: 하다 (to do) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: to do (and)
notes: -고 (-go) is a connective ending that links verbs or adjectives. Here, it connects 'to do' with 'want to'.
--- word ---
term: 싶더라
type: verb ending
breakdown: 싶다 (to want to) + -더라 (retrospective ending)
meaning: I wanted to (I recall)
notes: 싶다 (sipta) means 'to want to'. -더라 (-deora) is an informal retrospective ending used to recall a past observation or feeling that the speaker experienced directly. It implies a sense of 'I remember feeling/seeing that...' or 'It turned out that...'. It's often used when reflecting on one's own past thoughts or actions.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the desire to do something. Attached to the verb stem.
example: 밥을 먹고 싶어요. (I want to eat rice.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -더라
meaning: An informal retrospective ending used to recall a past observation or experience that the speaker personally witnessed or felt. It implies a sense of 'I remember that...' or 'It turned out that...'. It's often used when reflecting on one's own past thoughts or actions or describing something one observed.
example: 날씨가 좋더라. (The weather was good, I remember.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:32] 지금 딱 일어나자마자 커피 한 잔을 화장실 한번.. ===
meaning: Just now, as soon as I woke up, a cup of coffee, then the bathroom once...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: Refers to the immediate present. See notes for Segment 1.
--- word ---
term: 딱
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: exactly, precisely, just
notes: An adverb used to emphasize exactness or suddenness. Here, it emphasizes 'just' as in 'just now'.
--- word ---
term: 일어나자마자
type: verb ending
breakdown: 일어나다 (to wake up/get up) + -자마자 (as soon as)
meaning: as soon as I woke up/got up
notes: 일어나다 (ireonada) means 'to wake up' or 'to get up'. -자마자 (-jamaja) is a grammatical pattern meaning 'as soon as' or 'right after'. It indicates that the second action happens immediately after the first.
--- word ---
term: 커피
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: coffee
notes: A loanword from English.
--- word ---
term: 한
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: one
notes: The native Korean number for 'one' used before a counter or noun.
--- word ---
term: 잔을
type: counter + particle
breakdown: 잔 (cup) + 을 (object particle)
meaning: a cup (object)
notes: 잔 (jan) is a counter for cups or glasses. 을 (eul) is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 화장실
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: bathroom, restroom
notes: A common noun for 'bathroom'.
--- word ---
term: 한번
type: adverb/noun
breakdown: 한 (one) + 번 (time/instance)
meaning: once, one time
notes: 한번 (hanbeon) can be an adverb meaning 'once' or 'one time', or a noun meaning 'one instance'. Here, it implies 'going to the bathroom once'. The sentence trails off, indicating an unfinished thought or a natural pause in speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -자마자
meaning: Means 'as soon as' or 'right after'. It indicates that the second action or event occurs immediately after the first one.
example: 집에 오자마자 잤어요. (As soon as I came home, I slept.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:50] 어제 몇 차까지 갔냐고? ===
meaning: You're asking how many rounds I went to yesterday?
tone: Informal, responsive, slightly playful.
--- word ---
term: 어제
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: yesterday
notes: See notes for Segment 0.
--- word ---
term: 몇
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: how many
notes: Used before a noun or counter to ask about quantity.
--- word ---
term: 차까지
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 차 (round/place) + 까지 (until/up to)
meaning: up to how many rounds/places
notes: 차 (cha) here refers to 'rounds' or 'venues' for a social gathering, as explained in the STORY BIBLE (몇 차). 까지 (kkaji) is a particle meaning 'until' or 'up to', indicating a limit or extent.
--- word ---
term: 갔냐고?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 가다 (to go) + -았냐고? (past tense informal indirect question ending)
meaning: did (I) go, you ask?
notes: 가다 (gada) means 'to go'. -았냐고? (-annyago?) is a contraction of -았느냐고? (-anneunyago?), which is the informal, casual form of the indirect question ending -(으)냐고 하다 (to ask if/whether...). Here, Hyejin is repeating a fan's question, implying 'You're asking if I went...?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)냐고 하다
meaning: Used to report or ask an indirect question in informal speech. It translates to 'to ask if/whether...' or 'to ask what/where/when...'. The form -냐고 is a common contraction.
example: 뭐 하냐고 물어봤어. (I asked what he was doing.)
level: Intermediate

=== [1:53] 어제.. 어제 원래 그냥 혼자 있고 싶어가지고 ===
meaning: Yesterday... yesterday, I originally just wanted to be alone, so...
tone: Informal, explanatory, slightly wistful.
--- word ---
term: 어제
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: yesterday
notes: See notes for Segment 0.
--- word ---
term: 원래
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: originally, usually, by nature
notes: Indicates a prior intention, state, or habit.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, simply, without any particular reason
notes: A versatile adverb used to express simplicity, lack of specific purpose, or a casual attitude.
--- word ---
term: 혼자
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: alone, by oneself
notes: Describes an action performed without others.
--- word ---
term: 있고
type: verb stem + connective ending
breakdown: 있다 (to be/exist) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: to be (and)
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to be' or 'to exist'. -고 (-go) connects it to the following verb '싶어가지고'.
--- word ---
term: 싶어가지고
type: verb ending
breakdown: 싶다 (to want to) + -어가지고 (because/so)
meaning: wanted to, so/because
notes: This is a colloquial contraction of -고 싶어 가지고 (-go sipeo gajigo). -고 싶다 (-go sipta) means 'to want to'. -아/어가지고 (-a/eogajigo) is an informal connective ending indicating a reason or cause, similar to -아서/어서 (-aseo/eoseo) or -기 때문에 (-gi ttaemune). It means 'because (I wanted to)' or 'so (I wanted to)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the desire to do something. Attached to the verb stem.
example: 여행 가고 싶어요. (I want to travel.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어가지고
meaning: An informal and colloquial connective ending that expresses cause or reason, similar to -아/어서 or -기 때문에. It indicates that the preceding clause is the reason for the following clause.
example: 배고파가지고 밥 먹었어. (I was hungry, so I ate.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:03] 좀 조용히 이렇게 집에 있고 싶은 거야 ===
meaning: I wanted to be quietly at home like this.
tone: Informal, explanatory, personal.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little, a bit
notes: A common adverb used to soften a statement or indicate a small degree. Colloquial shortening of 조금 (jogeum).
--- word ---
term: 조용히
type: adverb
breakdown: 조용하다 (to be quiet) + -히 (adverbial suffix)
meaning: quietly
notes: Formed by adding the adverbial suffix -히 (-hi) to the adjective stem 조용하- (joyongha-). It describes the manner of being.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 (to be like this) + -게 (adverbial suffix)
meaning: like this, in this way
notes: Describes the manner or way of doing something. 이렇다 (ireota) means 'to be like this'.
--- word ---
term: 집에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 집 (home/house) + 에 (locative particle)
meaning: at home
notes: 집 (jip) means 'home' or 'house'. 에 (e) is a locative particle indicating 'at' or 'in'.
--- word ---
term: 있고
type: verb stem + connective ending
breakdown: 있다 (to be/exist) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: to be (and)
notes: See notes for Segment 5.
--- word ---
term: 싶은
type: verb modifier
breakdown: 싶다 (to want to) + -(으)ㄴ (adjective-forming ending)
meaning: wanting to
notes: 싶다 (sipta) means 'to want to'. -(으)ㄴ (-(eu)n) is an adjectival ending that modifies the following noun. Here, it modifies '거야' (geoya), making it 'the thing that I want to be'.
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 것 (thing) + 이다 (to be) + -야 (informal ending)
meaning: it's that (thing)
notes: A colloquial contraction of 것이다 (geot-ida). This pattern, -(으)ㄴ/는 거야 (-(eu)n/neun geoya), is used to explain or emphasize a fact, reason, or intention. It adds a nuance of 'the thing is that...' or 'it's that...'. It's very common in informal speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the desire to do something. Attached to the verb stem.
example: 한국어를 배우고 싶어요. (I want to learn Korean.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ/는 거야
meaning: This pattern is used to explain or emphasize a fact, reason, or intention. It adds a nuance of 'the thing is that...' or 'it's that...'. It's very common in informal speech.
example: 지금 가는 거야. (The thing is, I'm going now.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:07] 그래서 그냥 뭐 약속 아무것도 안 잡고 ===
meaning: So, I just didn't make any plans or anything.
tone: Informal, explanatory, straightforward.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: A common conjunction used to connect a cause or reason to its result or consequence.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, simply
notes: See notes for Segment 5.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: interjection/filler
breakdown: 
meaning: well, what (filler)
notes: Used as a filler word in conversation, similar to 'well' or 'you know'. Can also mean 'what'.
--- word ---
term: 약속
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: appointment, plan, promise
notes: Refers to a pre-arranged meeting or commitment.
--- word ---
term: 아무것도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 아무것 (anything) + 도 (also/even)
meaning: anything (with negation)
notes: 아무것 (amugeot) means 'anything'. When combined with the particle 도 (do, also/even) and a negative verb, it means 'nothing' or 'not anything'. For example, 아무것도 안 하다 means 'to do nothing'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: A short-form negation adverb placed directly before a verb or adjective.
--- word ---
term: 잡고
type: verb stem + connective ending
breakdown: 잡다 (to catch/make/hold) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: made (and)
notes: 잡다 (japda) has various meanings, including 'to catch', 'to hold', 'to grab'. When used with 약속 (yakssok, appointment/plan), it means 'to make an appointment' or 'to set a plan'. The -고 (-go) ending connects this action to an implied subsequent action or state (e.g., 'so I didn't make any plans and just stayed home').
--- grammar ---
pattern: 아무것도 + negative verb
meaning: Means 'nothing' or 'not anything'. '아무것' means 'anything', and '도' means 'even/also'. When used with a negative verb, it negates 'anything' to 'nothing'.
example: 아무것도 안 먹었어요. (I didn't eat anything / I ate nothing.)
level: Beginner

=== [2:12] 일 끝나자마자 운동이랑 하고 ===
meaning: As soon as work finished, I exercised and...
tone: Informal, narrative, slightly trailing.
--- word ---
term: 일
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: work
notes: 일 (il) means 'work' or 'day'. In this context, it refers to one's job or tasks.
--- word ---
term: 끝나자마자
type: verb ending
breakdown: 끝나다 + -자마자
meaning: as soon as it finished
notes: -자마자 (-jamaja) is a grammatical ending attached to the stem of a verb, meaning 'as soon as' or 'right after' the action of the preceding verb. It indicates immediate succession of two actions. It is commonly used in informal and formal contexts.
--- word ---
term: 운동이랑
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 운동 + -이랑
meaning: exercise and
notes: 운동 (undong) means 'exercise' or 'sport'. -이랑 (-irang) is an informal particle meaning 'and' or 'with'. It is used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant. If the noun ends in a vowel, -랑 (-rang) is used. The more formal equivalent is -과/와 (-gwa/wa).
--- word ---
term: 하고
type: verb + conjunction
breakdown: 하다 + -고
meaning: did and
notes: 하다 (hada) is the verb 'to do'. -고 (-go) is a connective ending used to link two or more actions or states in chronological order or simply to list them. It can be translated as 'and' or 'and then'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-자마자
meaning: Used to express that an action or event occurs immediately after another action or event. 'As soon as V-ed'.
example: 집에 오자마자 밥을 먹었어요. (As soon as I came home, I ate.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-(이)랑
meaning: An informal particle meaning 'and' or 'with'. -이랑 is used after a noun ending in a consonant, and -랑 after a noun ending in a vowel.
example: 친구랑 영화 봤어. (I watched a movie with a friend.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고
meaning: Connects two or more verbs or adjectives, indicating a sequence of actions or a listing of states. 'V and (then)...'
example: 밥을 먹고 커피를 마셨어요. (I ate and then drank coffee.)
level: Beginner

=== [2:19] 또 집에 가는 길에 친구한테 전화가 오더라고 ===
meaning: Also, on my way home, a friend called me.
tone: Informal, narrative, slightly reflective.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again, also
notes: 또 (tto) means 'again,' 'also,' or 'in addition.' It indicates repetition or an additional item/action.
--- word ---
term: 집에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 집 + -에
meaning: to home
notes: 집 (jip) means 'home' or 'house.' -에 (-e) is a particle indicating direction ('to'), location ('at'), or time ('at'). Here, it indicates direction towards home.
--- word ---
term: 가는
type: verb + adjective form
breakdown: 가다 + -는
meaning: going
notes: 가다 (gada) is the verb 'to go.' -는 (-neun) is a present participle suffix that turns a verb into an adjective modifying a following noun. So, 가는 길 (ganeun gil) means 'the road/way that is going' or 'on the way to go'.
--- word ---
term: 길에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 길 + -에
meaning: on the way
notes: 길 (gil) means 'road' or 'way.' Combined with -에, 길에 means 'on the road' or 'on the way.' The phrase V-는 길에 is a common expression meaning 'on the way to V-ing'.
--- word ---
term: 친구한테
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 친구 + -한테
meaning: to a friend
notes: 친구 (chingu) means 'friend.' -한테 (-hante) is an informal particle used to indicate the recipient of an action, meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. The more formal equivalent is -에게 (-ege).
--- word ---
term: 전화가
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 전화 + -가
meaning: a call
notes: 전화 (jeonhwa) means 'phone call' or 'telephone.' -가 (-ga) is a subject particle, indicating that '전화' is the subject of the clause.
--- word ---
term: 오더라고
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 오다 + -더라고
meaning: came (I recall)
notes: 오다 (oda) means 'to come.' -더라고 (-deorago) is a recollective ending used to express the speaker's recollection of something they personally observed or experienced in the past. It often carries a nuance of surprise or realization at the time of observation. It is an informal ending.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 길에
meaning: Used to indicate that an action is taking place while one is on the way to or from a certain place. 'On the way to V-ing'.
example: 학교 가는 길에 친구를 만났어요. (I met a friend on the way to school.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-한테
meaning: An informal particle used to indicate the recipient of an action (to N) or the source of an action (from N). It is used with people or animals.
example: 엄마한테 편지를 썼어요. (I wrote a letter to Mom.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-더라고
meaning: A recollective ending used to express the speaker's past observation or experience, often with a nuance of realization or discovery at that time. It is an informal ending.
example: 그 영화 정말 재미있더라고요. (That movie was really interesting, I found.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:25] 나는 정말 집에서 편하게 쉴 생각으로 ===
meaning: I, truly, with the thought of comfortably resting at home,
tone: Informal, narrative, setting the scene.
--- word ---
term: 나는
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + -는
meaning: I (as for me)
notes: 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I'. -는 (-neun) is a topic particle, indicating that '나' is the topic of the sentence. It can also imply contrast.
--- word ---
term: 정말
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: 정말 (jeongmal) is an adverb meaning 'really,' 'truly,' or 'indeed.' It emphasizes the truth or intensity of a statement.
--- word ---
term: 집에서
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 집 + -에서
meaning: at home
notes: 집 (jip) means 'home' or 'house.' -에서 (-eseo) is a particle indicating the location where an action takes place. Here, it means 'at home' as the place of resting.
--- word ---
term: 편하게
type: adjective + adverbial suffix
breakdown: 편하다 + -게
meaning: comfortably
notes: 편하다 (pyeonhada) means 'to be comfortable.' -게 (-ge) is an adverbial suffix that turns an adjective into an adverb, meaning 'comfortably' or 'in a comfortable way.' It can also be attached to verb stems to mean 'so that...'
--- word ---
term: 쉴
type: verb + future/prospective modifier
breakdown: 쉬다 + -ㄹ
meaning: to rest
notes: 쉬다 (swida) means 'to rest.' -ㄹ (-l) is a future or prospective noun modifier (adjective form) attached to verb stems ending in a vowel. It indicates an action that is intended, expected, or will happen. Here, it modifies '생각' (thought).
--- word ---
term: 생각으로
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 생각 + -으로
meaning: with the thought
notes: 생각 (saenggak) means 'thought' or 'idea.' -으로/로 (-euro/ro) is a particle indicating means, method, or direction. Here, V-ㄹ 생각으로 means 'with the thought of V-ing' or 'intending to V'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A-게
meaning: Attached to an adjective stem to form an adverb, meaning 'in a (adjective) way' or 'adjectively'.
example: 예쁘게 입으세요. (Please dress beautifully.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄹ/을 생각으로
meaning: Used to express the intention or plan to do something. 'With the thought of V-ing' or 'intending to V'.
example: 내일 일찍 일어날 생각으로 일찍 잤어요. (I went to bed early with the thought of waking up early tomorrow.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:29] 그래 너무 좋다 이렇게 가고 있었는데 ===
meaning: Yes, this is so good, I was going along like this, but then...
tone: Informal, narrative, transitional, implying an interruption.
--- word ---
term: 그래
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: yes, okay
notes: 그래 (geurae) is an informal interjection meaning 'yes,' 'okay,' or 'that's right.' It's a casual form of 그렇다 (geureota, to be so).
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: 너무 (neomu) means 'too much' or 'excessively,' but colloquially it is often used simply to mean 'very' or 'so' without a negative connotation, especially in informal speech like Hyejin's.
--- word ---
term: 좋다
type: adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: good
notes: 좋다 (jota) means 'to be good' or 'to be nice.' It's the base form of the adjective.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this, this way
notes: 이렇다 (ireota) means 'to be like this.' -게 (-ge) is an adverbial suffix. Thus, 이렇게 means 'like this' or 'in this way,' describing the manner of an action.
--- word ---
term: 가고
type: verb + conjunction
breakdown: 가다 + -고
meaning: going and
notes: 가다 (gada) means 'to go.' -고 (-go) is a connective ending. Here, it's part of the progressive tense V-고 있다, meaning 'to be V-ing'.
--- word ---
term: 있었는데
type: verb + past tense + connective ending
breakdown: 있다 + -었- + -는데
meaning: was (doing something) but
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to be' or 'to exist.' -었- (-eot-) is the past tense suffix. -는데 (-neunde) is a connective ending that provides background information, sets the scene, or introduces a contrast or a subsequent event. The full construction V-고 있었는데 means 'I was V-ing, but (something happened/will happen).'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 있다
meaning: Used to express an ongoing action or a continuous state. 'To be V-ing'.
example: 지금 책을 읽고 있어요. (I am reading a book now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-는데/은데/ㄴ데
meaning: A versatile connective ending that provides background information, sets the scene, or introduces a contrast or a subsequent event. For verbs and 있다/없다, use -는데. For adjectives ending in a consonant, use -은데; for adjectives ending in a vowel, use -ㄴ데.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Intermediate

=== [2:32] 친구는 또 걱정이 됐나 봐 ===
meaning: My friend, again, seemed to have gotten worried.
tone: Informal, narrative, speculative.
--- word ---
term: 친구는
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 친구 + -는
meaning: friend (as for the friend)
notes: 친구 (chingu) means 'friend.' -는 (-neun) is a topic particle, indicating that '친구' is the topic of this part of the sentence.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again, also
notes: 또 (tto) means 'again' or 'also.' Here, it implies a repeated or additional instance of worry.
--- word ---
term: 걱정이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 걱정 + -이
meaning: worry
notes: 걱정 (geokjeong) means 'worry' or 'concern.' -이 (-i) is a subject particle. The phrase 걱정되다 (geokjeongdoeda) means 'to become worried' or 'to be worried'.
--- word ---
term: 됐나
type: verb + past tense + interrogative ending
breakdown: 되다 + -었- + -나
meaning: became, perhaps
notes: 되다 (doeda) means 'to become.' -었- (-eot-) is the past tense suffix. -나 (-na) is an informal interrogative ending, often used to express speculation or wonder, especially when combined with 보다 (boda) in the V-나 보다 pattern. So, 됐나 means 'became, perhaps?'
--- word ---
term: 봐
type: auxiliary verb
breakdown: 보다
meaning: seems, looks like
notes: 보다 (boda) means 'to see' or 'to look.' When used as an auxiliary verb after V-나, it forms the pattern V-나 보다, meaning 'it seems that V' or 'it looks like V.' It expresses the speaker's conjecture based on observation or inference. This is an informal usage.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-나 보다
meaning: Used to express the speaker's conjecture or inference based on observation or a situation. 'It seems that V' or 'It looks like V'.
example: 비가 오나 봐요. (It seems like it's raining.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: 걱정되다
meaning: A common verb meaning 'to become worried' or 'to be worried'. It is formed by combining the noun 걱정 (worry) with the verb 되다 (to become).
example: 시험 때문에 걱정돼요. (I'm worried because of the exam.)
level: Beginner

=== [2:37] 그래도 혼자 있는 건 아닌 거 같은데 ===
meaning: Still, it seems like being alone isn't the case, so...
tone: Informal, narrative, slightly explanatory, trailing.
--- word ---
term: 그래도
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: still, however
notes: 그래도 (geuraedo) means 'still,' 'yet,' or 'however.' It indicates a continuation despite a preceding condition or a slight contrast.
--- word ---
term: 혼자
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: alone
notes: 혼자 (honja) is an adverb meaning 'alone' or 'by oneself.' It describes the state of being solitary.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: verb + adjective form
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: being, existing
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to be' or 'to exist.' -는 (-neun) is a present participle suffix, turning '있다' into an adjective modifying '건' (thing).
--- word ---
term: 건
type: noun + particle (contraction)
breakdown: 것 + -은
meaning: the thing (topic)
notes: 건 (geon) is a common contraction of 것 (geot, 'thing' or 'fact') + -은 (-eun, topic particle). It refers to 'the fact of being' or 'the thing that is'.
--- word ---
term: 아닌
type: adjective + adjective form
breakdown: 아니다 + -ㄴ
meaning: not being
notes: 아니다 (anida) means 'to not be.' -ㄴ (-n) is an adjective-forming suffix for adjectives ending in a vowel (or for 아니다). So, 아닌 means 'that is not' or 'not being'.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing
notes: 거 (geo) is a common informal contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact.' It is frequently used in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 같은데
type: adjective + connective ending
breakdown: 같다 + -은데
meaning: seems like, but
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be similar' or 'to seem.' -은데 (-eunde) is a connective ending used after adjectives ending in a consonant, providing background information or setting up a contrast. The full pattern V-는 것 같은데 means 'it seems that V, but...' or 'it seems that V, so...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 것 같다
meaning: Used to express the speaker's opinion, guess, or observation about a situation. 'It seems that V' or 'I think that V'.
example: 그 사람이 거짓말을 하는 것 같아요. (It seems like that person is lying.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: 것 -> 거 (contraction)
meaning: The noun 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact', is very commonly contracted to 거 (geo) in informal spoken Korean.
example: 이거 뭐야? (What is this thing?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: 것 + 은 -> 건 (contraction)
meaning: The combination of the noun 것 (geot) and the topic particle -은 (-eun) is frequently contracted to 건 (geon) in informal speech.
example: 그건 내 책이야. (That's my book.)
level: Beginner

=== [2:47] 집에서 최고의 친구지 ===

type: noun + particle

type: adjective
breakdown: 최고 + -의
meaning: best, greatest
notes: 최고 (choego) means 'best' or 'greatest.' -의 (-ui) is a possessive particle ('of'), but it is often omitted or used to form an adjectival phrase. 최고의 means 'the best' or 'of the best quality'.

'right?' or 'you know?'. It's an informal ending, often used to seek confirmation or express a shared understanding.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-지
meaning: Attached to a noun (or verb/adjective) to confirm a fact that the speaker assumes the listener already knows or agrees with. It can also express the speaker's own realization or rhetorical question. 'N, isn't it?'
example: 이거 맛있지? (This is delicious, isn't it?)

meaning: did that and stuff, you know?

The past tense form is 그랬다 (geuraetda). -거든 (-geodeun) is an informal conversational ending used to provide background information, explain a reason, or seek confirmation, often with a nuance of 'you know' or 'as you know'. It implies the speaker expects the listener to understand or agree. This ending is casual and frequently used in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb stem + -고
meaning: Connects two or more verbs or clauses, indicating a sequence of actions ('and then') or a simple listing of actions/states ('and').

meaning: An informal conversational ending used to provide context, background information, or a reason for a statement. It often implies a shared understanding or seeks confirmation, similar to 'you know' or 'as you see'.
example: 나 배고프거든. (Na baegopeugeodeun.) - I'm hungry, you know. (as a reason for something)

=== [3:04] 얘가 내 친구들을 데리고 왔다는 거야 ===
meaning: She said she brought my friends.
tone: Informal, recounting, slightly surprised/annoyed.
--- word ---
term: 얘가
type: Pronoun + Subject particle
breakdown: 얘 (this child/person) + -가 (subject particle)
meaning: she
notes: 얘 (yae) is a colloquial contraction of 이 아이 (i ai - this child/person). It's used informally to refer to someone, often younger or in a familiar context. Here, Hyejin is referring to 'my friend' from the previous sentence. -가 (-ga) is the subject particle.
--- word ---
term: 내
type: Possessive pronoun
breakdown: 나 (I) + -의 (possessive particle)
meaning: my
notes: 내 (nae) is the contracted form of 나의 (naui), meaning 'my'. It's commonly used in informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 친구들을
type: Noun + Plural marker + Object particle
breakdown: 친구 (friend) + -들 (plural marker) + -을 (object particle)
meaning: friends
notes: 친구 (chingu) means 'friend'. -들 (-deul) is the plural marker. -을 (-eul) is the object particle, used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant (here, the 'ㄹ' sound from -들).
--- word ---
term: 데리고
type: Verb stem + Connective ending
breakdown: 데리다 (to bring/take a person/animal) + -고 (and, then)
meaning: bringing
notes: 데리다 (derida) specifically means 'to bring' or 'to take' a person or an animal. It's often used with the verb 오다 (oda - to come) or 가다 (gada - to go) to form expressions like 데리고 오다 (derigo oda - to bring someone/something here) or 데리고 가다 (derigo gada - to take someone/something there). Here, it's part of the compound verb 데리고 오다.
--- word ---
term: 왔다는
type: Verb + Quotative modifying ending
breakdown: 오다 (to come) + -았- (past tense) + -다는 (quotative modifying ending)
meaning: came (that she said)
notes: 오다 (oda) means 'to come'. The past tense is 왔다 (watda). -다는 (-daneun) is a quotative modifying ending used to report what someone said, turning the preceding clause into a noun clause that modifies a subsequent noun (here, implied '것' - geot, thing). It's a contraction of -다고 하는 (dagohaneun - saying that).
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 것 (thing) + -이야 (copula)
meaning: it is that
notes: 거야 (geoya) is a colloquial contraction of 것이야 (geosiya). It's a common ending in informal speech, often used to explain a situation, express a realization, or emphasize a fact. When combined with a quotative like -다는, it forms a common pattern for indirect speech: 'X-다는 거야' means 'It is that X was said/reported' or 'She said that X'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb stem + -고 오다/가다
meaning: Used to express the action of 'going somewhere to do something and coming back' or 'doing something while coming/going'. 데리고 오다 means 'to bring someone/something here'.
example: 책을 사 가지고 왔어요. (Chaeg-eul sa gajigo wasseoyo.) - I bought a book and brought it here.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb/Adjective stem + -다는 것/거야
meaning: An indirect quotation pattern used to report what someone said or thought. It translates to 'the fact that [someone said]...' or 'it is that [someone said]...'. -다는 is the modifying form, and 것/거야 makes it a noun clause.
example: 친구가 내일 온다는 거야. (Chinguga naeil ondaneun geoya.) - My friend said they're coming tomorrow.
level: Intermediate

=== [3:11] 내 친구들이래 ===

=== [3:27] 어우 외롭지 않아 ===
meaning: Oh, I'm not lonely.
tone: Informal, playful, slightly sarcastic.
--- word ---
term: 어우
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Oh, Wow
notes: An exclamation often used to express a strong reaction, such as surprise, admiration, or sometimes a mild complaint/exasperation. Here, it adds to the playful, slightly sarcastic tone as Hyejin comments on not being lonely with her new 'friends'.

meaning: lonely (not)

meaning: to try talking (to them), you told me to?
notes: This is a contraction of 걸어 보라고 (georeo borago). The -아/어보다 ending means 'to try doing something'. The -라고 (rago) ending is used to report an indirect command or suggestion, often translated as 'to tell someone to do X' or 'to suggest doing X'. Here, it's used in a questioning tone, implying 'Did you tell me to try talking?' This is informal speech (반말, banmal).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어보다
meaning: Used after a verb stem to express trying an action or experiencing something. It implies 'to try doing something' or 'to do something and see what happens'.
example: 먹어보다 (meogeoboda) - to try eating; 가보다 (gaboda) - to try going/to visit.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)라고 하다
meaning: Used to indirectly quote a command or suggestion. It translates to 'to tell someone to do something' or 'to suggest that someone do something'. When used with a question mark, it can imply 'Did you tell me to...?'
example: 밥 먹으라고 했어 (bap meogeurago haesseo) - I told them to eat. 빨리 오라고! (ppalli orago!) - (He said) 'Come quickly!'
level: Intermediate

meaning: understood, got it
notes: This is the informal past tense conjugation of 알다 (alda), meaning 'to know' or 'to understand'. It's a common casual way to say 'Okay', 'Got it', or 'I understand'. This is informal speech (반말, banmal).

=== [3:58] 이름이 뭐였지? ===
meaning: What was their name?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly forgetful. Hyejin is trying to recall the name of the last 'friend'.
--- word ---
term: 이름이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 이름 (name) + -이 (subject particle)
meaning: name (subject)
notes: 이름 (ireum) means 'name'. The particle -이 (-i) is a subject particle, indicating that 'name' is the subject of the sentence. It is used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 뭐였지?
type: interrogative pronoun + verb + ending
breakdown: 뭐 (what) + 이다 (to be) + -었지 (past tense + rhetorical/recollective ending)
meaning: what was it, right? / what was it?
notes: This is a contraction of 무엇이었지 (mueosieotji). 뭐 (mwo) is an informal contraction of 무엇 (mueot), meaning 'what'. 이다 (ida) is the verb 'to be'. The ending -었지 (-eotji) combines the past tense -었- (-eot-) with the rhetorical/recollective ending -지 (-ji), which is used to confirm something the speaker believes to be true or to recall something. It often implies '...wasn't it?' or '...right?' This is informal speech (반말, banmal).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -이/가 뭐예요?
meaning: A common question pattern meaning 'What is [noun]?' or 'What is [noun]'s name?'.
example: 이게 뭐예요? (ige mwoyeyo?) - What is this? 이름이 뭐예요? (ireumi mwoyeyo?) - What is your name?
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -었/았지
meaning: Used to express recollection or to confirm something the speaker believes to be true, often with a rhetorical or slightly questioning nuance. It combines the past tense with the -지 ending.
example: 어제 왔었지? (eoje wasseotji?) - You came yesterday, didn't you? / I remember you came yesterday.
level: Intermediate

=== [4:04] 그 귀 나팔 나팔 거리는 하얀? ===
meaning: That white one with the floppy, floppy ears?
tone: Informal, questioning, descriptive. Hyejin is trying to recall the name of one of the 'friends' (pets/plushies).
--- word ---
term: 그
type: Demonstrative determiner
breakdown: 그
meaning: that
notes: Used to refer to something previously mentioned or understood by both the speaker and listener. It functions like 'the' or 'that' in English, pointing to a specific item.
--- word ---
term: 귀
type: Noun
breakdown: 귀
meaning: ear
notes: A simple noun meaning 'ear'.
--- word ---
term: 나팔
type: Mimetic word
breakdown: 나팔나팔
meaning: floppy, flappy
notes: 나팔나팔 is an onomatopoeic/mimetic word (의성어/의태어) describing something flapping or flopping loosely. It's often used with -거리다 to form a verb.
--- word ---
term: 거리는
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 거리다 + -는
meaning: that is flopping/flapping
notes: -거리다 is a common suffix attached to mimetic words (like 나팔나팔) or nouns to form a verb meaning 'to act like' or 'to keep doing [mimetic action]'. -는 is the present descriptive verb ending used to modify a following noun (e.g., 'the flapping ears').
--- word ---
term: 하얀
type: Adjective
breakdown: 하얗다 + -ㄴ
meaning: white
notes: 하얗다 (to be white) is a ㅂ-irregular adjective. When conjugated with vowel endings, the ㅂ changes to ㅜ or ㅗ. Here, it combines with the adjectival ending -ㄴ to modify an implied noun, becoming 하얀. This form is used before nouns to describe them (e.g., 하얀 눈 'white snow').
--- grammar ---
pattern: [Mimetic word] + -거리다
meaning: This pattern forms a verb meaning 'to act like' or 'to keep doing' the action described by the mimetic word. It's commonly used to describe repetitive or continuous actions or states.
example: 반짝반짝거리다 (to twinkle repeatedly), 흔들흔들거리다 (to sway back and forth)
level: Intermediate

=== [5:06] 역대급으로 힘들었어 이번 생일커버 ===
meaning: This birthday cover was the hardest ever.
tone: Informal, emphatic, slightly complaining.
--- word ---
term: 역대급으로
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 역대급 + -으로
meaning: to an unprecedented degree, as the best/worst ever
notes: 역대급 (yeokdaegeup) is a colloquial term meaning 'unprecedented', 'legendary', or 'the best/worst ever'. It literally combines 역대 (yeokdae, 'all time, successive generations') and 급 (geup, 'level, class'). -으로 is a particle indicating means, method, or degree. So, 'to an unprecedented degree' or 'as the most [adjective] ever'.
--- word ---
term: 힘들었어
type: adjective + informal past tense ending
breakdown: 힘들다 + -었어
meaning: was difficult, was hard
notes: 힘들다 (himdeulda) means 'to be difficult' or 'to be hard'. -었어 is the informal past tense ending. The polite informal form would be 힘들었어요.
--- word ---
term: 이번
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: this (time)
notes: Used before a noun to indicate 'this current' or 'this time's' item. E.g., 이번 주 (this week), 이번 달 (this month).
--- word ---
term: 생일커버
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: birthday cover
notes: See notes for segment 2.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -으로
meaning: Particle indicating means, method, direction, or degree. Here, it indicates the degree to which something was difficult.
example: 연필로 써요. (Write with a pencil.) / 한국어로 말해요. (Speak in Korean.)
level: Beginner

breakdown: 나 + -가
meaning: I (as subject)

It makes the statement sound more explanatory or descriptive, often used when sharing one's thoughts or feelings.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 거야

level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: 이제 vs 지금
meaning: 이제 (ije) refers to 'now' in the sense of 'from this point forward' or 'at this stage', implying a change. 지금 (jigeum) means 'right now' or 'at this very moment'.
example: 지금 가자! (Let's go right now!) / 이제 갈 시간이야. (It's time to go now [from this point].)

=== [5:22] 근데 이제 저 초에 불이 안 붙어서 당황하고 ===

--- word ---
term: 저
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: that (over there)
notes: A demonstrative determiner used to refer to something far from both the speaker and the listener, or something previously mentioned.
--- word ---
term: 초에
type: noun + locative particle
breakdown: 초 + -에
meaning: on the candle
notes: 초 (cho) means 'candle'. -에 is a locative particle indicating location ('on', 'at', 'in') or time. Here, it indicates the location where the action of lighting was attempted.
--- word ---
term: 불이
type: noun + subject particle
breakdown: 불 + -이
meaning: fire, light (as subject)
notes: 불 (bul) means 'fire' or 'light'. -이 is the subject particle. 불이 붙다 means 'for fire to catch' or 'to light'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: A short-form negative adverb placed directly before the verb or adjective it negates.
--- word ---
term: 붙어서
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 붙다 + -어서
meaning: because it didn't light/stick
notes: 붙다 (butda) means 'to stick', 'to adhere', or 'to catch fire/light'. -아서/어서 is a causal connective ending, meaning 'because' or 'so'. It indicates that the preceding clause is the reason for the following clause. Since the candle wouldn't light, Hyejin was flustered.
--- word ---
term: 당황하고
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 당황하다 + -고
meaning: was flustered and
notes: 당황하다 (danghwanghada) means 'to be flustered' or 'to be embarrassed/perplexed'. -고 is a sequential or listing connective ending, meaning 'and' or 'and then'. It connects two or more actions or states that occur in sequence or are listed together. Here, it implies that being flustered was one of the things that happened.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -에
meaning: Locative particle. Indicates location ('at', 'in', 'on') or time. Here, it specifies the location for the action.
example: 집에 있어요. (I am at home.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아서/어서
meaning: Causal connective ending. Connects two clauses, indicating that the first clause is the reason or cause for the second clause. It translates to 'because [first clause], [second clause]'.
example: 비가 와서 못 갔어요. (Because it rained, I couldn't go.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고
meaning: Connective ending. Used to connect two or more verbs or adjectives, indicating a sequence of actions or a list of states. It translates to 'and' or 'and then'.
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어요. (I ate and then watched a movie.)
level: Beginner

meaning: Indicates the instrument, means, or method by which an action is performed. Can also indicate direction or status.

breakdown: 무엇 + -인지
meaning: what it is
notes: 무엇 (mueot) means 'what'. -인지 is an indirect question ending, used when asking 'what it is' or 'whether it is'. It combines with verbs or nouns to form indirect questions.
--- word ---
term: 알지?
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 알다 + -지?
meaning: you know, right?
notes: 알다 (alda) means 'to know'. -지? is an informal sentence ending used to seek confirmation from the listener, often implying that the speaker expects agreement. It can also be used to confirm something the speaker already knows or believes to be true. Very common in casual conversation among friends or with a close audience like fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -인지
meaning: Used to form indirect questions, meaning 'whether it is' or 'what it is' (when combined with an interrogative pronoun like 무엇).
example: 그게 뭔지 알아요? (Geuge mwonji arayo?) - Do you know what that is?
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지?
meaning: An informal sentence ending used to seek confirmation from the listener, often implying that the speaker expects agreement. Similar to 'right?' or 'isn't it?'.
example: 이거 맛있지? (Igeo masitji?) - This is delicious, right?
level: Beginner

=== [6:09] 근데 하여튼 진짜 ===

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: anyway, at any rate

--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly

=== [6:28] 제일 조금 뭔가 탐나는 곡이라던지 ===

--- word ---
term: 제일

breakdown: 제일
meaning: most, best
notes: Used to indicate the highest degree of a quality or action. Often precedes an adjective or adverb. This is a common informal adverb.
--- word ---
term: 조금
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금
meaning: a little, a bit
notes: Can modify verbs, adjectives, or other adverbs. Indicates a small degree or amount. Casual/Informal.
--- word ---
term: 뭔가
type: Pronoun + particle (contracted)
breakdown: 무엇 (mueot) + -이 (i) + -ㄴ가 (n-ga) -> 뭔가
meaning: something, somewhat
notes: A common contraction of 무엇인가 (mueosinga). 무엇 (mueot) means 'what,' -이 (i) is a subject particle, and -ㄴ가 (n-ga) is a suffix indicating uncertainty or vagueness. Here, it means 'something' or 'somewhat' in a vague sense. Colloquial and informal.
--- word ---

meaning: desirable, tempting, covetable

--- word ---
term: 곡이라던지
type: Noun + copula + grammar pattern
breakdown: 곡 (gok) + -이다 (ida) + -라던지 (radeondeoji)
meaning: a song, or something like that
notes: 곡 (gok) means 'song.' -이라던지 (-iradeondeoji) is used to list examples or possibilities, implying 'whether it's X or Y, etc.' or 'something like X.' It attaches to nouns (이다 is the copula 'to be'). It suggests a non-exhaustive list. Casual/Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)라던지 (-(i)radeondeoji)
meaning: Used to list examples or possibilities, implying 'whether it's X or Y, etc.' or 'something like X.' It suggests a non-exhaustive list.
example: 커피라던지 차라던지 마실 것 좀 주세요. (Please give me something to drink, like coffee or tea.)

=== [6:35] 아니면 아 나도 한번 불러보고 싶다 라던지 ===

meaning: once, one time, try

--- grammar ---
pattern: -어보다 (-eoboda)
meaning: Means 'to try doing something' or 'to experience something.' It indicates an attempt or an experience.

--- grammar ---

meaning: Used to list examples of actions or desires, implying there might be others. It's a form of indirect quotation combined with the 'or something like that' pattern.
example: 여행을 가고 싶다라던지, 쉬고 싶다라던지. (Whether it's wanting to go on a trip, or wanting to rest.)
level: Intermediate

breakdown: 고민 (gomin) + -의 (ui)
meaning: of worry/dilemma
notes: 고민 (gomin) means 'worry,' 'dilemma,' or 'concern.' -의 (ui) is the possessive particle 'of.' Casual/Informal.
--- word ---
term: 그
type: Determiner
breakdown: 그
meaning: that, the
notes: Same as above. Repetition for emphasis.
--- word ---
term: 기로에
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 기로 (giro) + -에 (e)
meaning: at the crossroads, at the turning point
notes: 기로 (giro) means 'crossroads' or 'fork in the road,' often used metaphorically for a critical decision point. -에 (e) is a location particle 'at.' Casual/Informal.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 막
meaning: just, just now, intensely
notes: Can mean 'just now,' 'recklessly,' or 'intensely.' Here, it emphasizes the immediacy or intensity of being at that point. Casual/Informal.
--- word ---
term: 서있었는데
type: Verb + conjunctive ending
breakdown: 서있다 (seoitta) + -었 (eot) + -는데 (neunde)
meaning: I was standing (there) but/and
notes: 서있다 (seoitta) means 'to be standing.' -었 (eot) is the past tense marker. -는데 (neunde) sets up background or continuation, implying 'I was at that point, but (then something else happened or was decided).' Casual/Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지 않다 (-ji anta)
meaning: The standard way to form negative sentences in Korean, meaning 'to not do/be.' It attaches to the verb or adjective stem.
example: 저는 커피를 마시지 않아요. (I don't drink coffee.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -겠- (-get-)
meaning: A versatile suffix that can express conjecture/probability ('will probably,' 'must be') or intention/will ('I will'). The context determines the meaning. Here, it expresses conjecture about the difficulty.
example: 내일 비가 오겠다. (It will probably rain tomorrow.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)라고 하다 (-(i)rago hada)
meaning: Means 'to say that' or 'to think that.' Used for indirect quotation of speech or thoughts. When followed by an adnominal ending like -는, it becomes -(이)라는, meaning 'the (noun) that is called/thought of as.'
example: 그는 바쁘다고 했다. (He said that he was busy.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 있다 (-go itda)
meaning: Means 'to be doing (something)' or 'to be in a state of (something).' It indicates an ongoing action or a continuous state.
example: 책을 읽고 있어요. (I am reading a book.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데 (-neunde)
meaning: A versatile conjunctive ending that provides background information, introduces a contrast, or indicates a continuation of action or thought. It can be translated as 'but,' 'and,' 'so,' or 'while.'
example: 밥을 먹는데 전화가 왔어요. (I was eating, and the phone rang.)
level: Beginner

=== [7:01] 아 너무 좋다 했는데 와 진짜 쉽지 않겠다 라는 그 고민의 그 기로에 막 서있었는데.. ===
meaning: Ah, I thought it was really good, but then I was truly standing at the crossroads of that worry, thinking 'Wow, this won't be easy at all...'
tone: Reflective, slightly conflicted, informal. Hyejin is recalling an internal thought process about a difficult task.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah / Oh
notes: A common interjection used to express various emotions like realization, surprise, frustration, or agreement. Here, it expresses a realization or a sigh. Informal.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Too / Very
notes: Means 'too much' or 'very.' Can be used positively or negatively. In casual speech, it's often used interchangeably with '정말' (jeongmal) or '진짜' (jinjja) to mean 'really' or 'very.' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 좋다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 좋다
meaning: Good / Likeable
notes: Base form of the adjective 'to be good.' Often used to express something is pleasing or desirable. Informal.
--- word ---
term: 했는데
type: Verb ending + particle
breakdown: 하다 (to do/be) + -았/었- (past tense) + -는데 (connective ending)
meaning: I thought it was good, but... / I said it was good, but...
notes: This combines the verb '하다' (hada, to do/be) used here to mean 'to think/say,' the past tense marker '-았/었-' (at/eot), and the connective ending '-는데' (neunde). The ending '-는데' introduces background information, provides a contrast, or sets the stage for the next clause, often translating to 'but,' 'and,' 'so,' or 'when/while.' Here, it implies a contrast: 'I thought it was good, but...' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 와
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Wow / Oh
notes: An exclamation of surprise or admiration, similar to 'Wow!' in English. Informal.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Really / Truly
notes: Means 'really' or 'truly.' Can also be used as an adjective '진짜다' (jinjja-da) meaning 'to be real.' Very common in casual conversation. Informal.
--- word ---
term: 쉽지
type: Adjective + negative particle
breakdown: 쉽다 (to be easy) + -지 (negative particle)
meaning: Not easy
notes: '쉽다' (swipda) is an irregular adjective (ㅂ-irregular). When conjugated, ㅂ changes to ㅜ or ㅗ. Here, it's followed by '-지' (ji) as part of the negative construction '~지 않다' (ji anta), meaning 'not to do/be.' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 않겠다
type: Auxiliary verb + future/conjecture ending
breakdown: 않다 (not to do/be) + -겠- (future/conjecture) + -다 (declarative ending)
meaning: It won't be / It wouldn't be
notes: '않다' (anta) is the auxiliary verb for negation. The ending '-겠-' (get) indicates future tense, intention, or conjecture/strong possibility. Here, it expresses a strong realization or prediction about the difficulty: 'it will definitely not be easy' or 'it would not be easy.' '-다' (da) is a plain form declarative ending, often used in internal monologue or informal speech. Informal.
--- word ---
term: 라는
type: Quotative particle + adjective form
breakdown: -라고 하다 (to say that) + -는 (adjective-forming particle)
meaning: That (I thought/said) / Which is called
notes: This is a contraction of '-라고 하는' (rago haneun). The direct quotative particle '-라고 하다' (rago hada) means 'to say that...' or 'to be called...'. The '-는' (neun) turns the preceding clause into an adjective modifying the following noun. So, 'the thought *that it wouldn't be easy*.' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 그
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: That / The
notes: A demonstrative pronoun/determiner meaning 'that' or 'the.' Refers to something previously mentioned or understood in context.
--- word ---
term: 고민의
type: Noun + possessive particle
breakdown: 고민 (worry/concern) + -의 (possessive particle)
meaning: Of worry / Of concern
notes: '고민' (gomin) means 'worry,' 'concern,' or 'trouble.' '-의' (eui) is the possessive particle, similar to 's' or 'of' in English. It clarifies 'the crossroads *of* worry.' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 그
type: Determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: That / The
notes: Repeats '그' for emphasis or to refer back to the specific 'crossroads.'
--- word ---
term: 기로에
type: Noun + locative particle
breakdown: 기로 (crossroads/fork in the road) + -에 (locative particle)
meaning: At the crossroads
notes: '기로' (giro) literally means 'fork in the road' or 'crossroads,' often used metaphorically for a critical juncture or dilemma. '-에' (e) is a locative particle indicating 'at,' 'in,' or 'on' a place or time.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Just / Randomly / Roughly
notes: A versatile adverb. Here, it implies 'just' or 'right at that moment,' emphasizing the immediacy of being at the crossroads. Can also mean 'roughly,' 'recklessly,' or 'without thinking.' Informal.
--- word ---
term: 서있었는데
type: Verb + past progressive + connective ending
breakdown: 서다 (to stand) + -아/어 있다 (state of being) + -았/었- (past tense) + -는데 (connective ending)
meaning: I was standing there, and... / I was standing there, but...
notes: '서다' (seoda) means 'to stand.' The construction '-아/어 있다' (a/eo itda) indicates a state of being resulting from an action, similar to 'to be standing.' '-았/었-' (at/eot) is the past tense marker, and '-는데' (neunde) again provides background or a slight contrast. Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -았/었-는데 (at/eotneunde)
meaning: Connective ending used after a past tense verb or adjective. It provides background information, introduces a contrasting idea, or sets the stage for the next clause. It can mean 'but,' 'and,' 'so,' or 'when/while.'
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~지 않다 (ji anta)
meaning: A common way to form negative sentences, meaning 'not to do/be.' It attaches to the verb or adjective stem.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -겠- (get)
meaning: An ending indicating future tense, intention, or strong conjecture/possibility. It can express the speaker's strong belief or prediction.
level: Beginner/Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~라고 하다 / ~라는 (rago hada / raneun)
meaning: The direct quotative particle '-라고 하다' means 'to say that...' or 'to be called...'. When combined with '-는' (neun), it forms '-라는' (raneun), which turns the quoted clause into an adjective modifying a following noun.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 있다 (a/eo itda)
meaning: An auxiliary verb construction indicating a state of being that results from a completed action, often translated as 'to be [verb]-ing' or 'to be in a [verb]ed state.'
level: Intermediate

meaning: This connective ending has two main uses: 1. Causal: 'because, since' (often used when the speaker discovers a reason or result). 2. Temporal: 'when, upon doing something' (often implying a realization or discovery after the action). Here, it's used in the temporal sense, 'upon thinking about it, I realized...'
example: 집에 오니까 엄마가 없었어요. (When I came home, Mom wasn't there.)
level: Intermediate

--- grammar ---

=== [8:15] 그런 부분들이 있는데 ===
meaning: There are those kinds of aspects, but...
tone: Informal, transitional, slightly hesitant, setting background context.
--- word ---
term: 그런
type: demonstrative adjective
breakdown: 그렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: such, that kind of
notes: This is the adjectival form of 그렇다 (geureota, to be so/that way). -ㄴ is an adjectival ending for descriptive verbs, indicating a characteristic. It refers back to something previously mentioned or understood in context. Informal register.
--- word ---
term: 부분들이
type: noun + plural marker + subject particle
breakdown: 부분 + -들 + -이
meaning: aspects, parts (as subject)
notes: 부분 (bubun) means 'part' or 'aspect'. -들 (deul) is a plural marker. -이 (i) is the subject particle, used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant. Informal register.
--- word ---
term: 있는데
type: verb ending
breakdown: 있다 + -는데
meaning: there are, but / and
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to exist' or 'to have'. -는데 (neunde) is a versatile connective ending. Here, it functions to provide background information or a preceding situation, often implying a 'but' or 'and so' connection to the next thought. It's commonly used to transition between ideas in spoken Korean. Informal register.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-는데
meaning: Used to provide background information, introduce a new topic, or express a contrast/reason. It often implies a 'but' or 'and so' connection to the following clause, even if the following clause is omitted.
level: Beginner

pattern: N-(으)로

level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-도
meaning: Adds the meaning of 'also', 'too', or 'even' to a noun.

=== [8:55] 그는 돈을 가지고 있고, 그는 그것을 잃지 않는다. ===
meaning: He has money, and he doesn't lose it.
tone: Factual, descriptive, informal.
--- word ---
term: 그는
type: Pronoun + Topic Particle
breakdown: 그 (he/that) + 는 (topic particle)

term: 돈을
type: Noun + Object Particle
breakdown: 돈 (money) + 을 (object particle)
meaning: money
notes: 돈 (don) means 'money.' 을 (eul) is an object particle, used after nouns ending in a consonant to mark the direct object of a verb. Casual register.
--- word ---
term: 가지고
type: Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 가지다 (to have/possess) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: having, possesses
notes: 가지다 (gajida) means 'to have' or 'to possess.' The -고 (-go) ending connects two clauses, indicating a sequence of actions or a listing of facts. It can mean 'and,' 'and then,' or 'and also.' Casual register.
--- word ---
term: 있고
type: Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 있다 (to be/exist/have) + -고 (and/then)
meaning: has, exists
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to be,' 'to exist,' or 'to have.' When combined with 가지다 (gajida) as in 가지고 있다 (gajigo itda), it means 'to possess' or 'to have.' The -고 (-go) ending connects clauses, similar to the previous instance. Casual register.
--- word ---
term: 그는
type: Pronoun + Topic Particle
breakdown: 그 (he/that) + 는 (topic particle)

breakdown: 그것 (that thing/it) + 을 (object particle)

term: 잃지
type: Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 잃다 (to lose) + -지 (negative connective)
meaning: doesn't lose
notes: 잃다 (ilta) means 'to lose.' The -지 (-ji) here is part of the negative construction -지 않다 (-ji anta), meaning 'not to do something.' It connects the verb stem to the negative auxiliary verb 않다. Casual register.
--- word ---
term: 않는다
type: Auxiliary Verb (conjugated)
breakdown: 않다 (not to do) + -는다 (present tense declarative ending)
meaning: doesn't
notes: 않다 (anta) is an auxiliary verb used to form negative sentences. The -는다 (-neunda) ending is a plain form declarative ending for verbs, used for factual statements, often in written language or when speaking informally to oneself or close friends. It's a non-honorific, non-polite form. Casual register.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 V
meaning: Connects two or more clauses, indicating a sequence of actions, a listing of facts, or a cause-and-effect relationship. It can mean 'and,' 'and then,' or 'and also.'
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어요. (I ate and then watched a movie.)

--- grammar ---
pattern: V-지 않다
meaning: Forms a negative statement, meaning 'not to do V.'

breakdown: 다

=== [9:28] 그녀는 그녀의 위로의 보디오가 있다. ===
meaning: She has her comfort's bodio.
tone: Declarative, grammatically structured but sounds unnatural for casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 그녀는
type: Pronoun + particle
breakdown: 그녀 (she) + 는 (topic particle)
meaning: She (as for her)
notes: 그녀 (geunyeo) is the third-person feminine pronoun 'she'. While grammatically correct, using '그녀는' explicitly in casual conversation, especially when referring to oneself or when the subject is clear, can sound formal or unnatural. Native Korean speakers often omit pronouns in such contexts. The particle -는 (-neun) is a topic particle, marking the preceding noun as the sentence's topic.
--- word ---
term: 그녀의
type: Pronoun + possessive particle
breakdown: 그녀 (she) + 의 (possessive particle)
meaning: her
notes: 의 (-ui) is the possessive particle, equivalent to ''s' or 'of' in English. It indicates possession or a relationship between nouns. Similar to '그녀는', the repeated use of '그녀의' in a casual context can sound stiff; pronouns are often omitted or replaced with contextually understood references in natural conversation. When pronounced, 의 often sounds like '에' (e) in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 위로의
type: Noun + possessive particle
breakdown: 위로 (comfort/consolation) + 의 (possessive particle)
meaning: of comfort / comforting
notes: 위로 (wiro) means 'comfort' or 'consolation'. Adding the possessive particle 의 makes it 'of comfort' or, more naturally in English, 'comforting' or 'for comfort' when modifying another noun.
--- word ---
term: 보디오가
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 보디오 (bodio) + 가 (subject particle)
meaning: bodio (subject)
notes: 보디오 (bodio) is not a standard Korean word and appears to be a transcription error. It is highly likely a mishearing of 비디오 (bidio), meaning 'video'. If it were 비디오, the phrase '위로의 비디오' would mean 'comfort video' or 'video for comfort'. The particle -가 (-ga) is the subject particle, indicating the preceding noun is the subject of the verb.
--- word ---
term: 있다
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 (to have/to exist)
meaning: has / there is
notes: 있다 (itda) is a versatile verb meaning 'to exist,' 'to be (located),' or 'to have.' In this context, it means 'to have.' This is the dictionary form of the verb.

=== [9:40] 나도 한국어도 잘 못하는데. ===

term: 나도
type: Pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 (I/me) + 도 (also/even)
meaning: I also / Even I
notes: 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I' or 'me.' 도 (do) is an additive particle meaning 'also,' 'too,' or 'even.' Here, it emphasizes 'even I' or 'I myself,' implying a sense of shared difficulty or a humble admission.
--- word ---
term: 한국어도
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 한국어 (Korean language) + 도 (also/even)
meaning: Korean language also / even Korean language
notes: 한국어 (hangugeo) means 'Korean language.' The particle 도 (do) is used again, reinforcing the 'even' or 'also' aspect, suggesting 'even the Korean language (which is my native tongue).' This adds to the self-deprecating tone.
--- word ---
term: 잘
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: well
notes: 잘 (jal) is an adverb meaning 'well' or 'skillfully.' It typically precedes the verb or adjective it modifies. It's often paired with 하다 (hada, to do) to form 잘하다 (jalhada, to be good at) or 못하다 (mothada, to be bad at).
--- word ---
term: 못하는데

notes: 못하다 (mothada) is a verb meaning 'to be unable to do,' 'to be bad at,' or 'to fail to do.' It's the negative form of 잘하다 (jalhada, to be good at). The ending -는데 (-neunde) is a versatile informal connective ending that provides background information, expresses mild surprise, or sets up a contrast or further explanation. Here, it creates a trailing, conversational tone, implying 'I'm not even good at Korean myself, *so/but*...' or 'I'm not even good at Korean myself, *you know*.'
--- grammar ---
pattern: 못하다
meaning: To be unable to do something, or to be bad at something. It's the negative counterpart to 잘하다 (to be good at).
example: 저는 수영을 못해요. (I can't swim / I'm bad at swimming.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데 / -은데 / -는데
meaning: A connective ending that provides background information, expresses mild surprise, or sets up a contrast or further explanation for the following clause. It often translates to 'but,' 'so,' 'and,' or 'you know.'
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Intermediate

=== [9:41] 하여튼, 힘들었지만, 복잡했지만, ===
meaning: Anyway, it was tough, it was complicated, but...

--- word ---
term: 하여튼
type: Adverb/Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Anyway / At any rate
notes: 하여튼 (hayeoteun) is a colloquial adverb or interjection meaning 'anyway,' 'at any rate,' or 'in any case.' It's commonly used to transition topics, summarize a previous point, or bring the conversation back on track. It's an informal expression often heard in daily conversation.
--- word ---
term: 힘들었지만
type: Adjective + ending
breakdown: 힘들다 (to be difficult/tough) + -었 (past tense) + -지만 (but/although)
meaning: it was tough, but / although it was difficult
notes: 힘들다 (himdeulda) is an adjective meaning 'to be difficult,' 'to be tough,' or 'to be hard.' The past tense marker -었- (-eot-) is added, followed by the connective ending -지만 (-jiman), which means 'but' or 'although.' This ending connects two clauses, indicating a contrast or concession. It's a common and versatile ending used in both formal and informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 복잡했지만
type: Adjective + ending
breakdown: 복잡하다 (to be complicated) + -었 (past tense) + -지만 (but/although)
meaning: it was complicated, but / although it was complicated
notes: 복잡하다 (bokjapada) is an adjective meaning 'to be complicated,' 'to be complex,' or 'to be intricate.' Similar to 힘들었지만, it uses the past tense marker -었- and the connective ending -지만, indicating 'although it was complicated.' This structure sets up a contrasting positive outcome in the following sentence.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지만
meaning: A connective ending meaning 'but' or 'although.' It connects two clauses, where the second clause presents a contrast or concession to the first.
example: 날씨는 좋지만 바람이 많이 불어요. (The weather is good, but it's very windy.)
level: Beginner

=== [9:50] 결론적으로는 또 너무 즐거워. ===
meaning: In conclusion, it's also really enjoyable.
tone: Informal, conclusive, positive, reflective.
--- word ---
term: 결론적으로는
type: Adverb + particle
breakdown: 결론적 (conclusive) + 으로 (adverbial suffix) + 는 (topic particle)
meaning: Conclusively / In conclusion (as for)
notes: 결론적 (gyeollonjeok) means 'conclusive.' The suffix -으로 (-euro) turns it into an adverb, 'conclusively' or 'in conclusion.' The topic particle -는 (-neun) emphasizes 'as for the conclusion' or 'speaking conclusively,' highlighting the main point being made.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: also / again / moreover
notes: 또 (tto) is an adverb meaning 'again,' 'also,' 'moreover,' or 'in addition.' Here, it implies 'also' or 'still,' referring to the enjoyment despite the difficulties mentioned previously, suggesting it's an ongoing or recurring feeling.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too / very / really
notes: 너무 (neomu) is an adverb meaning 'too much,' 'excessively,' or 'very/really.' While traditionally meaning 'too much' with a negative connotation, in modern colloquial Korean, it's very commonly used simply as an intensifier meaning 'very' or 'really' with both positive and negative adjectives/verbs, especially in informal speech like this idol-fan interaction.
--- word ---
term: 즐거워
type: Adjective (informal polite form)
breakdown: 즐겁다 (to be enjoyable/pleasant) + -어 (informal polite ending)
meaning: it's enjoyable / it's pleasant
notes: 즐겁다 (jeulgeopda) is an adjective meaning 'to be enjoyable,' 'to be pleasant,' or 'to be joyful.' It undergoes a ㅂ irregular conjugation: ㅂ changes to ㅜ before a vowel ending. So, 즐겁다 becomes 즐거워 (jeulgeowo) in the informal polite (-아요/어요) form. This form is commonly used in casual conversation, especially when speaking to fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: ㅂ irregular verbs/adjectives
meaning: For verbs and adjectives ending in ㅂ (like 즐겁다, 춥다, 돕다), the ㅂ changes to ㅜ (or ㅗ for 돕다) when followed by a vowel ending (-아/어, -을/ㄹ, -으면, etc.).
example: 춥다 (to be cold) -> 추워요 (It's cold.)

=== [9:52] 이 매력이 있어. 그치? ===

--- word ---
term: 매력이
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 매력 (charm/attraction) + 이 (subject particle)
meaning: charm (subject)
notes: 매력 (maeryeok) means 'charm' or 'attraction.' The particle -이 (-i) is the subject particle, indicating that 'charm' is the subject of the verb '있다' (to have/exist). It is used after nouns ending in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb (informal polite form)
breakdown: 있다 (to have/to exist) + -어 (informal polite ending)
meaning: it has / there is
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to have' or 'to exist.' The informal polite ending -어 (-eo) is added to form 있어 (isseo), which is the common conversational form used in casual, polite speech. This is the 해요-form.
--- word ---

type: Interjection/Question tag (informal)
breakdown: 그렇다 (to be so/to be like that) + -지 (question tag)
meaning: Right? / Isn't it?
notes: 그치 (geuchi) is an informal contraction of 그렇지 (geureochi), which comes from 그렇다 (geureota, to be so/to be like that) + -지 (-ji, a question tag seeking agreement). It's equivalent to 'Right?' or 'Isn't it?' in English, used to confirm understanding or seek agreement from the listener. It's very common in casual conversation, especially among friends or with fans, as noted in the STORY BIBLE.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지?
meaning: A question tag attached to a verb or adjective stem, used to seek agreement or confirmation from the listener. It often translates to 'Right?' or 'Isn't it?' The informal contraction is often just '-치?'.
example: 재미있지? (It's fun, right?)

meaning: congratulated (for me)

meaning: the thing / what (object)

term: 보려고
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 보다 + -으려고
meaning: in order to see / intending to see
notes: 보다 (boda) means 'to see' or 'to look'. -으려고 (-euryeogo) is a grammatical ending indicating intention or purpose, meaning 'in order to' or 'with the intention of'.
--- word ---
term: 켰는데
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 켜다 + -았는데
meaning: I turned it on, but / and
notes: 켜다 (kyeoda) means 'to turn on' (e.g., a screen, light). -았는데 (-anneunde) is a past tense connective ending. It can mean 'but' (indicating a contrast or problem), 'and' (connecting sequential events), or 'so' (providing background information).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 주다 (-a/eo juda)
meaning: An auxiliary verb construction meaning 'to do something for someone' or 'to do someone a favor'. It indicates that the action of the main verb is performed for the benefit of another person.
example: 도와줘서 고마워요. (Thank you for helping me.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄴ/은 것 (-n/eun geot)
meaning: This pattern nominalizes a verb or adjective, turning it into a noun phrase meaning 'the thing that is/was [verb/adjective]'. -ㄴ is used after verb stems ending in a vowel or 'ㄹ', -은 after verb stems ending in a consonant (excluding 'ㄹ').
example: 내가 좋아하는 것 (the thing I like)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -으려고 (-euryeogo)
meaning: This ending is attached to a verb stem to express the speaker's intention or purpose, meaning 'in order to' or 'with the intention of'. -으려고 is used after consonant stems, -려고 after vowel stems or 'ㄹ' stems.
example: 한국어를 배우려고 한국에 왔어요. (I came to Korea to learn Korean.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데/은데/는데 (-neunde/eunde/neunde)
meaning: A versatile connective ending that can indicate background information, contrast ('but'), sequential events ('and'), or a reason ('so'). The specific nuance depends on context. -는데 is used after action verbs and '있다/없다', -은데 after descriptive verbs ending in a consonant, -는데 after descriptive verbs ending in a vowel or 'ㄹ'.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Beginner

breakdown: 뜨다 + -는
meaning: appearing / showing up (descriptive)
notes: 뜨다 (tteuda) has multiple meanings, including 'to float,' 'to rise,' 'to open (eyes),' and in this context, 'to appear' or 'to show up' (e.g., for a message, a sun, or comments). -는 is the present tense adnominal ending, modifying the following noun (것, implied in 거야).
--- word ---
term: 거야?
type: Noun + Copula + Interrogative Ending
breakdown: 것 + 이다 + -야?
meaning: Is it that...?
notes: 것 (geot) is a dependent noun meaning 'thing' or 'fact.' 이다 (ida) is the copula 'to be.' -야 is the informal interrogative ending. 거야? (geoya?) is a common informal contraction of 것이야? It's used to ask for confirmation or express surprise/question about a situation, often implying 'Is it the case that...?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 것
meaning: This pattern nominalizes a verb, turning it into a noun phrase. It refers to the 'act of V-ing' or 'the fact that V-s'.
example: 한국어를 배우는 것이 재미있어요. (Learning Korean is fun.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 거야?
meaning: An informal interrogative pattern asking for confirmation or expressing surprise about a present action or state. It can often be translated as 'Is it that (verb)?' or 'Are you (verb)-ing?'
example: 지금 가는 거야? (Are you going now?)
level: Beginner

=== [10:41] 처음이야. ===
meaning: It's the first time.
tone: Informal, declarative, expressing surprise or disbelief at the situation.
--- word ---
term: 처음이야.
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 처음 + 이다 + -야
meaning: It's the first time.
notes: 처음 (cheoeum) means 'first time' or 'beginning.' -이야 is the informal present tense conjugation of the copula 이다 (ida), meaning 'to be.' This is a common, concise way to state 'It is the first time.'
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 이야/야
meaning: This is the informal present tense conjugation of the copula 이다 (to be) when attached to a noun. Use 이야 after a consonant-ending noun, and 야 after a vowel-ending noun.
example: 학생이야. (I'm a student.)
level: Beginner

=== [10:42] 시무룩 말티즈가 아니라. ===
meaning: Not a sulky Maltese.
tone: Informal, playful, possibly self-referential or referencing a previous comment/situation. The speaker, Hyejin, often uses playful self-deprecating humor, sometimes referring to herself as a 'scruffy Maltese' or 'countryside puppy' (from STORY BIBLE).
--- word ---
term: 시무룩
type: Adjective/Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: sulky, glum, dejected
notes: 시무룩 (simuruk) describes a state of being sulky or glum. It can function as an adjective (시무룩한 표정 - a sulky expression) or an adverb (시무룩하게 앉아있다 - to sit glumly). Here, it modifies 'Maltese'.
--- word ---
term: 말티즈가
type: Noun + Subject Particle
breakdown: 말티즈 + -가
meaning: Maltese + subject particle
notes: 말티즈 (maltijeu) is the Korean loanword for 'Maltese' (dog breed). -가 is the subject particle, used after a vowel-ending noun.
--- word ---
term: 아니라.
type: Copula + Connective Ending
breakdown: 아니다 + -라
meaning: not (to be), but / not (to be), rather
notes: 아니다 (anida) is the negative copula, meaning 'to not be.' -라 is a connective ending used to link two clauses, often meaning 'not A, but B' or 'not A, rather B.' Here, it implies 'it's not a sulky Maltese (that's the problem), but something else (the comments not showing up).' The speaker might be playfully distinguishing the current problem from her own 'sulky' state.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 이/가 아니다
meaning: This pattern is used to state that something is not a particular noun. 이다 (to be) becomes 아니다 (to not be).
example: 저는 학생이 아니에요. (I am not a student.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 이/가 아니라
meaning: This pattern means 'not N, but rather...' It connects two clauses, stating that the first noun is not the case, but something else is. It can also be used to simply state 'not N' and imply the real situation.
example: 사과가 아니라 배예요. (It's not an apple, it's a pear.)
level: Intermediate

=== [10:54] 그치? ===
meaning: Right?
tone: Informal, seeking confirmation, conversational.
--- word ---
term: 그치?
type: Interjection/Ending
breakdown: 그렇지? (contracted)
meaning: Right? / Isn't it?
notes: 그치? (geuchi?) is a common informal contraction of 그렇지? (geureochi?), which comes from 그렇다 (geureota - 'to be so' or 'to be like that') + the interrogative ending -지?. It's used to seek agreement or confirmation from the listener, similar to 'right?' or 'isn't it?' in English. Very common in casual conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-지?
meaning: This ending is used to seek confirmation from the listener, often when the speaker expects agreement. It can also imply a rhetorical question or a gentle suggestion. In informal speech, it's often contracted to -치? or -죠? (for polite form).
example: 예쁘지? (It's pretty, right?)
level: Beginner

breakdown: 
meaning: Huh? / Oh?

=== [10:56] 멍청이들도 안 떠? ===
meaning: Even the silly geese aren't showing up?

term: 멍청이들도
type: Noun + Plural Marker + Particle
breakdown: 멍청이 + -들 + -도
meaning: even the silly geese / even you guys

However, in this context, as per the STORY BIBLE, Hyejin uses it affectionately and playfully towards her fans, similar to 'silly geese' or 'you guys.' -들 is a plural marker. -도 is a particle meaning 'also' or 'even,' emphasizing that not even the fans' comments are appearing.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: A short-form negative adverb placed directly before the verb or adjective it negates. It's a common way to express 'not' in informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 떠?

breakdown: 뜨다 + -어?
meaning: are showing up? / are appearing?
notes: 뜨다 (tteuda) means 'to appear' or 'to show up' (as in comments appearing on a screen). -어? is the informal interrogative ending for verbs and adjectives, used in casual speech to ask a question.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 들
meaning: The plural marker -들 is attached to nouns to indicate plurality. While often optional for inanimate objects or when plurality is clear from context, it's frequently used with people or when emphasizing a group.
example: 친구들 (friends)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 도
meaning: The particle -도 means 'also,' 'too,' or 'even.' It attaches to a noun or pronoun and implies that the preceding item is included in a group or shares a characteristic with something else.
example: 저도 좋아요. (I like it too.)
level: Beginner

=== [11:00] 왜 그래? ===

tone: Informal, questioning, slightly frustrated or confused.
--- word ---
term: 왜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: why
notes: An interrogative adverb used to ask for the reason or cause of something. It always precedes the verb or adjective it modifies.
--- word ---
term: 그래?
type: Verb + Interrogative Ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -어?
meaning: is it like that? / is it doing that?
notes: 그렇다 (geureota) means 'to be so' or 'to be like that.' -어? is the informal interrogative ending. 왜 그래? (wae geurae?) is a very common colloquial expression meaning 'Why are you doing that?' 'What's wrong?' or 'Why is it like this?' It's used when asking for an explanation for a situation or someone's behavior.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 왜 V/A-아/어요?
meaning: This is the basic interrogative structure for asking 'why' something is happening or why something is a certain way. The verb/adjective is conjugated into the informal polite (-아/어요) or informal casual (-아/어?) form.
example: 왜 안 와요? (Why aren't you coming?)
level: Beginner

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: cannot
notes: 못 (mot) is an adverb placed before a verb to express inability or impossibility to do something. It's a short form of ~지 못하다 (~ji motada). For example, 못 가요 (mot gayo) means 'I can't go'.

The pattern -는 거야? is used to ask for confirmation or an explanation in a casual tone. It often implies a rhetorical question or a question seeking clarification about a situation.

meaning: Indicates inability or impossibility to perform an action. It's a direct negation of ability.

meaning: Used in informal speech to ask for confirmation, explanation, or to express a rhetorical question about a current state or action. It often implies a sense of surprise or disbelief.
example: 지금 가는 거야? (Jigeum ganeun geoya?) - Are you going now?

breakdown:

--- word ---
term: 그
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: that
notes: Repetition of '그' (geu) for emphasis or as a filler word while the speaker gathers their thoughts.
