meaning: on the post

type: noun + topic particle
breakdown: 댓글 + -은
meaning: comments (as for)?
notes: 댓글 (daetgeul) means 'comment' (on a post, article, video). -은 (-eun) is a topic particle, used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant. By using the topic particle and an upward intonation, the speaker is asking 'What about the comments?' or 'As for the comments...?'

example: 저는 괜찮아요. 지민 씨는요? (Jeoneun gwaenchanayo. Jimin ssineunyo?) - I'm fine. How about Jimin?

meaning: to do / that will do

breakdown: 것
meaning: thing

meaning: to do / that will do
notes: 하다 (hada) means 'to do'. -ㄹ (-l) is the future tense adnominal ending, modifying the following noun (때, ttae).
--- word ---
term: 때,
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: time / when
notes: 때 (ttae) means 'time' or 'when'. When preceded by an adnominalized verb (like 할), it forms a clause meaning 'when (doing something)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -(으)ㄹ 생각이다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or plan to do something. 'To be thinking of doing X'.

level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Indicates the time or occasion when an action occurs. 'When (doing X)'.
example: 밥 먹을 때 조용히 하세요. (Bap meogeul ttae joyonghi haseyo.) - Please be quiet when eating.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective/Verb + -(으)ㄴ/는데
meaning: Provides background information or context for the following statement, or expresses a mild contrast. It can also introduce a question related to the preceding context.
example: 비가 오는데 우산 있어요? (Biga oneunde usan isseoyo?) - It's raining, do you have an umbrella?

--- grammar ---

=== [12:59] 우리 결혼할래? ===
meaning: Shall we get married? / Will you marry me?
tone: Playful, direct, informal, suggestive. This is the culmination of the imagined proposal scenario.
--- word ---
term: 우리
type: pronoun
breakdown: 우리
meaning: we, us
notes: The pronoun 우리 (uri) means 'we' or 'us.' In Korean, 우리 is often used even when referring to something personal (e.g., 우리 집, 'my house') or when making a suggestion involving 'you and I,' implying a shared experience or relationship. In a proposal, it emphasizes the shared future. This is informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 결혼할래?
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 결혼하다 + -(으)ㄹ래?
meaning: will you marry me? / shall we get married?
notes: This is formed from the verb 결혼하다 (gyeorehonhada, to marry) and the informal interrogative ending -(으)ㄹ래? (-(eu)llae?). This ending is used in casual speech to ask about the listener's intention or to make a suggestion. It can be translated as 'Do you want to...?', 'Will you...?', or 'Shall we...?'. For a proposal, it directly asks 'Will you marry me?' or 'Shall we get married?'. It's a very common and direct way to propose informally. A more formal alternative would be 결혼해 주시겠어요? (Gyeorehonhae jusigesseoyo? - Would you marry me?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb stem + -(으)ㄹ래?
meaning: Used in informal speech to ask about the listener's intention or to make a suggestion. It implies a direct, often casual, inquiry about what someone wants to do or if they'd like to do something together.
example: 뭐 먹을래? (Mwo meogeullae?) - What do you want to eat?
level: Intermediate

=== [13:14] 아무리 내 애인이어도, 그치? ===
meaning: Even if it's my lover, right?
tone: Informal, rhetorical, seeking agreement from the audience.
--- word ---
term: 아무리
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: no matter how (much/what), however
notes: Used to emphasize that something is true regardless of a certain condition. It often precedes a clause with -(아/어)도 (-(a/eo)do, even if/though).
--- word ---
term: 내
type: possessive pronoun
breakdown: 나 + 의 (contracted)
meaning: my
notes: A contraction of 나의 (naui), meaning 'my'. 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I'.
--- word ---
term: 애인이어도
type: noun + particle + verb ending
breakdown: 애인 + 이다 + -어도
meaning: even if it is (my) lover
notes: 애인 (aein) means 'lover' or 'significant other'. 이다 (ida, to be) is the copula, and -어도 (-eodo) is a connective ending meaning 'even if' or 'even though'. So, 'even if it is my lover'.
--- word ---
term: 그치
type: interjection/question particle
breakdown: 그렇지 (contracted)
meaning: right?, isn't it?
notes: A common informal contraction of 그렇지 (geureochi), which comes from 그렇다 (geureota, to be so/that's right). It's used to seek agreement or confirmation from the listener, similar to 'right?' or 'isn't it?' in English. Hyejin often uses this with her fans to engage them.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 아무리 A -(아/어)도 B
meaning: This pattern means 'no matter how A, B' or 'even if A, B'. It emphasizes that B is true regardless of the condition A.
example: 아무리 바빠도 밥은 먹어야지. (No matter how busy you are, you should eat.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)어도
meaning: This ending is attached to nouns (with 이다, the copula) or verb/adjective stems to mean 'even if/though'. If the noun ends in a consonant, 이어도 is used; if it ends in a vowel, 여도 is used.
example: 학생이어도 알 수 있어요. (Even if you're a student, you can know.)
level: Beginner

notes: Used as an adverb to mean 'really' or 'truly,' or as an adjective 'real.' In this sentence, it functions as an adverb modifying the question, and then as the core of the predicate '진짜야?' (is it real?). It's very common in casual speech.

meaning: is it real?
notes: This is the informal interrogative form of 진짜이다 (to be real). -이야/-야 is the informal declarative/interrogative ending for nouns/adjectives ending in a consonant/vowel, respectively. Here, 진짜 ends in a vowel, so -야 is used. The question mark indicates an interrogative tone.

--- word ---

meaning: When (verb happens), at the time of (verb)
example: 밥 먹을 때 전화 왔어. (The call came when I was eating.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지 않았어?
meaning: Didn't (do something)? / Wasn't there?
example: 어제 영화 봤지 않았어? (Didn't you watch a movie yesterday?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 거야
meaning: Will (do something) / It will be (something)
example: 내일 비가 올 거야. (It will rain tomorrow.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -밖에 + negative verb/adjective
meaning: Only, nothing but
example: 나 돈이 만 원밖에 없어. (I only have 10,000 won.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -긴 하다
meaning: It is true that... (but there's a reservation/contrast)
example: 그 책이 재미있긴 한데, 너무 길어. (That book is interesting, but it's too long.)
level: Intermediate

type: verb
breakdown: 생각하다 + -아 보다 + -ㄹ게
meaning: I'll think about it / I'll try thinking
notes: This is a combination of 세 elements: 1. 생각하다 (saenggakada) 'to think'. 2. -아 보다 (-a boda) 'to try doing something', indicating an attempt or experience. 3. -ㄹ게 (-lge) a future tense ending expressing the speaker's intention or promise to do something for the listener's benefit. The full form is 생각해 보다 + -ㄹ게, which contracts to 생각해볼게. This is casual speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 보다
meaning: To try doing something; to experience doing something. It indicates an attempt or an experience.
example: 먹어 보다 (to try eating), 가 보다 (to try going / to have been to)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄹ/을게
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or promise to do something, often for the benefit or consideration of the listener. It's a casual and friendly future tense ending.
example: 내가 할게 (I'll do it), 내일 전화할게 (I'll call you tomorrow)
level: Beginner

=== [15:06] 기다려봐봐. ===
meaning: Wait and see. / Just wait.
tone: Informal, imperative, playful, urging patience.
--- word ---
term: 기다려봐봐
type: verb
breakdown: 기다리다 + -아 보다 + -아 봐
meaning: wait and see / just wait
notes: This is a playful and emphatic informal imperative. It comes from 기다리다 (gidarida) 'to wait', combined with -아 보다 (-a boda) 'to try doing something', and then the imperative ending -아/어 봐 (-a/eo bwa) 'try doing it'. The repetition of 봐 (bwa) as 봐봐 (bwabwa) adds emphasis and a more casual, urging, or even slightly teasing tone, meaning 'just wait and see' or 'just wait for it'. This is casual speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 봐
meaning: An informal imperative ending meaning 'try doing (something)' or 'go ahead and do (something)'. It can also be used to suggest or invite someone to do something.
example: 먹어 봐 (Try eating it), 해 봐 (Try doing it)
level: Beginner

=== [15:30] 아, 썩 귀에 두렴. ===
meaning: Ah, just keep that in mind (playfully dismissive).
tone: Informal, playful, slightly dismissive or teasing, acknowledging a comment but not taking it too seriously.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: ah / oh
notes: 아 (a) is a common interjection expressing a range of emotions like surprise, realization, or, in this context, a casual acknowledgment or slight exasperation.
--- word ---
term: 썩
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just / completely (colloquial intensifier)
notes: 썩 (sseok) can mean 'rotten' or 'very much', but colloquially, especially in informal or slightly dismissive contexts, it can function as an intensifier meaning 'just' or 'completely', implying a casual or even 'whatever' attitude. Here, it adds to the playful dismissal.
--- word ---
term: 귀에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 귀 + -에
meaning: in the ear
notes: 귀 (gwi) means 'ear'. -에 (-e) is a particle indicating location ('in', 'at', 'on').
--- word ---
term: 두렴
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 두다 + -렴
meaning: keep (it) / put (it)
notes: 두렴 (duryeom) is an informal imperative form of 두다 (duda), meaning 'to put', 'to place', or 'to keep'. The ending -렴 (-ryeom) is a casual imperative used when speaking to someone younger or of lower status, or affectionately to close friends/family. The phrase 귀에 두다 (gwie duda) literally means 'to put in one's ear', but idiomatically it can mean 'to keep in mind' or 'to listen to'. In this playful context, combined with 썩, it suggests 'just keep that thought to yourself' or 'just listen to it and don't take it too seriously'.

=== [15:31] SO CUTE BOOM 이렇게 안될거 같애 ===
meaning: SO CUTE BOOM, I don't think it'll be like that.
tone: Informal, conversational, expressing doubt or disagreement playfully, possibly reacting to a fan's comment or idea.
--- word ---
term: SO CUTE BOOM
type: English phrase
breakdown: 
meaning: SO CUTE BOOM
notes: This is an English phrase, likely a direct quote from a fan's comment or a playful expression Hyejin is using. Its specific meaning here is contextual, possibly referring to an overly cute or dramatic proposal idea.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this / this way
notes: 이렇게 (ireoke) means 'like this' or 'this way'. It's formed by adding the adverbial suffix -게 (-ge) to the demonstrative adjective 이렇다 (ireota) 'to be like this'. It refers to the manner or way something is done or will be.
--- word ---
term: 안될거
type: verb + nominalizer
breakdown: 안 + 되다 + -ㄹ 것
meaning: won't be / won't work
notes: This is a contraction of 안 될 것 (an doel geot). 안 (an) is a short-form negation prefix meaning 'not'. 되다 (doeda) means 'to become', 'to work out', or 'to be possible'. -ㄹ 것 (-l geot) is a future tense nominalizer, meaning 'the thing that will be' or 'the possibility that it will be'. So, 안될거 means 'the thing that won't be' or 'it won't work out'. This is casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 같애
type: verb ending
breakdown: 같다 + -아/어
meaning: I think / it seems
notes: 같애 (gatae) is an informal contraction of 같아 (gata), which comes from 같다 (gatda) 'to be like', 'to seem', or 'to appear'. The -아/어 (-a/eo) ending is a casual speech ending. When combined with a nominalized verb (like -ㄹ 것), it means 'I think it will be...' or 'It seems like it will be...'. This is casual speech.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄹ/을 것 같다
meaning: Expresses a conjecture or opinion about a future event or state, meaning 'I think it will (verb)' or 'It seems like it will (verb)'. The '것' is often contracted to '거' in casual speech.
example: 비가 올 것 같아요 (I think it will rain), 그가 올 거 같아 (I think he will come)
level: Intermediate

=== [16:10] 그러니까 프로포즈를 해봤어야 알지 ===

tone: Informal, slightly exasperated, rhetorical, self-deprecating. Hyejin is responding to the fans' questions about proposals, implying her lack of experience.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: I mean; so; therefore
notes: Used to explain or elaborate on a previous statement, or to draw a conclusion. It can also express slight exasperation or a 'well, obviously' tone in informal speech. Here, Hyejin uses it to justify her lack of knowledge about proposals.
--- word ---
term: 프로포즈를
type: noun + object particle
breakdown: 프로포즈 + -를
meaning: proposal (object)
notes: 프로포즈 (peuro-pojeu) is 'proposal'. -를 (-reul) is the object particle, marking 'proposal' as the direct object of the verb 'to do/try'.
--- word ---
term: 해봤어야
type: verb + auxiliary verb + conditional ending
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어 보다 + -았/었어야
meaning: only if one had tried doing it
notes: 하다 (hada) 'to do'. -아/어 보다 (-a/eo boda) 'to try'. -았/었어야 (-at/eosseoya) is a conditional ending meaning 'only if (one had done something)'. --- word ---
term: 알지

breakdown: 알다 + -지
meaning: know, right?; you'd know
notes: 알다 (alda) means 'to know'. -지 (-ji) is an ending used to confirm something the speaker believes the listener already knows or should know, or to express the speaker's own realization. It can also be rhetorical, as here, implying 'then one would know, but I don't'. Informal and conversational.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 봤어야
meaning: This pattern means 'only if one had tried/experienced doing X, then Y would happen/be known'. It implies that the condition (trying X) was not met, thus Y is not possible. It often conveys a sense of 'if only I had...' or 'you'd only know if you...'.
example: 먹어봤어야 맛을 알지. (You'd only know the taste if you've tried eating it.)

=== [16:36] 저는 프로포즈에 대한 로망은 진짜 없는 거 같애 ===
meaning: I really don't seem to have any romantic fantasies about proposals.
tone: Informal, reflective, expressing a personal opinion.
--- word ---
term: 저는
type: pronoun + topic particle
breakdown: 저 + -는
meaning: I (topic)
notes: 저 (jeo) is the humble form of 'I'. -는 (neun) is the topic particle, indicating '저는' as the main topic of the sentence. Used in polite or slightly formal contexts, even if the overall sentence ending is informal.
--- word ---
term: 프로포즈에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 프로포즈 + -에
meaning: to/about proposal
notes: 프로포즈 (peuropeoju) is a loanword for 'proposal'. -에 (e) is a particle indicating direction, time, or in this context, 'about' or 'regarding' when paired with '대한'.
--- word ---
term: 대한
type: adjective/post-modifier
breakdown: 대하다 + -ㄴ
meaning: about, regarding
notes: 대하다 (daehada) means 'to face, to treat, to be about'. When conjugated as 대한 (daehan), it functions as a modifier meaning 'about' or 'regarding' the preceding noun. It's a common pattern: [Noun]에 대한 [Noun] (e.g., 한국 문화에 대한 책 - a book about Korean culture).
--- word ---
term: 로망은
type: noun + topic particle
breakdown: 로망 + -은
meaning: romantic fantasy (topic)
notes: 로망 (romang) is a loanword from French 'roman' or English 'romance', often used in Korean to mean 'romantic fantasy' or 'ideal dream'. -은 (eun) is a topic particle, used after a consonant-ending noun.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: 진짜 (jinjja) is an informal adverb meaning 'really' or 'truly'. It can also be used as an adjective 'real' or an exclamation 'Really?!'. It's very common in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 없는
type: adjective
breakdown: 없다 + -는
meaning: not existing, absent
notes: 없다 (eopda) means 'to not exist, to not have'. -는 (neun) is an adjectival ending that modifies the following noun (or nominalized clause). Here, it modifies 거 (geo), indicating 'the thing that doesn't exist'.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: nominalizer (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: 거 (geo) is a common colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), which nominalizes the preceding clause, turning it into a 'thing' or 'fact'. Used extensively in informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 같애
type: verb ending (contraction)
breakdown: 같다 + -아요/어요 -> 같아요
meaning: seems like, I think
notes: 같애 (gatae) is an informal contraction of 같아요 (gatayo), which comes from 같다 (gatda - to be like, to seem) + the informal polite ending -아요/어요. It expresses the speaker's opinion or conjecture. Hyejin uses this informal ending with her fans, which is typical for idol-fan interactions.
--- grammar ---
pattern: [Noun]에 대한 [Noun]
meaning: This pattern means 'a [Noun] about/regarding [Noun]'. It's used to specify the topic or subject of something.
example: 이 책은 한국 역사에 대한 책이에요. (This book is a book about Korean history.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 것 같다
meaning: This pattern is used to express the speaker's opinion, conjecture, or observation, meaning 'it seems that...' or 'I think that...'. -는 is used for present tense verbs, -(으)ㄴ for past tense verbs/adjectives, and -(으)ㄹ for future tense verbs/adjectives.
example: 비가 오는 것 같아요. (It seems like it's raining.)
level: Beginner

=== [16:42] 너무 하여튼 내가 모르게만 해줬으면 좋겠고 ===

meaning: too much, very

meaning: I hope it would be good, and...

--- grammar ---
pattern: -게 하다
meaning: This pattern means 'to make someone do something' or 'to cause something to be in a certain state'. It can also mean 'to do something in a certain way'.

--- grammar ---
pattern: -았/었으면 좋겠다
meaning: This pattern expresses a wish or hope, meaning 'I wish/hope that...' or 'It would be good if...'.

level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---

meaning: This connective ending means 'and' or 'and then'. It connects two or more clauses, indicating a sequence of events or a list of facts/actions.

notes: 튀김 (twigim) means "fried food" or "tempura." The particle -도 (-do) means "also" or "too," indicating that fried loach would be an addition to the chueotang.
--- word ---
term: 같이
type: adverb
breakdown: 같이
meaning: together
notes: 같이 (gachi) is an adverb meaning "together" or "with." It can also be pronounced as 가치 (gachi) but the spelling 같이 is more common.
--- word ---
term: 시켜가지고
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 시키다 (to order) + -어가지고 (because of doing, having done)
meaning: having ordered, because we ordered
notes: The ending -어가지고 (-eogajigo) (or -아가지고/-여가지고) is a colloquial and informal variant of -어서 (-eoseo) or -고 (-go). It indicates a sequence of actions or a cause/reason. It means "having done X, then Y" or "because of X." Here, it implies "having ordered (it) together, then..." This is a common spoken form.
--- word ---
term: 그치
type: interjection/ending
breakdown: 그렇다 (to be so) + -지 (confirmation)
meaning: right? isn't it?
notes: 그치 (geuchi) is a casual, informal contraction of 그렇지 (geureochi). It's used to seek agreement or confirmation, similar to "right?" or "isn't it?" in English. It's very common in conversational Korean among close relations.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어/여가지고
meaning: A colloquial and informal ending indicating a sequence of actions or a cause/reason. Similar to -아/어/여서 or -고, meaning 'having done X, then Y' or 'because of X.'
example: 늦게 일어나가지고 지각했어요. (Because I woke up late, I was tardy.)
level: Intermediate

=== [17:37] 곱창 곱창 먹으면서? ===

meaning: Indicates that two actions or states occur simultaneously. 'While doing X' or 'at the same time as X.'
example: 음악을 들으면서 공부했어요. (I studied while listening to music.)

=== [17:42] 뭔가 온몸에 뭔가 기름이 끼는 느낌이잖아 ===

--- word ---
term: 뭔가
type: adverb/noun
breakdown: 
meaning: somehow; something
notes: This term can function as an adverb meaning 'somehow' or 'somewhat,' or as a noun meaning 'something.' In this context, it's used twice, first as 'somehow' and then as 'something' (oily).

term: 온몸에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 온몸 + 에
meaning: on my whole body
notes: 온몸 (onmom) means 'whole body.' 에 (e) is a locative particle indicating 'on,' 'at,' or 'in.' This is a casual expression.

breakdown: 
meaning: something
notes: See previous note for '뭔가'. Here, it refers to the 'something' that is oily.
--- word ---
term: 기름이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 기름 + 이
meaning: oil/grease + subject particle
notes: 기름 (gireum) means 'oil' or 'grease.' 이 (i) is a subject particle, indicating that 기름 is the subject of the verb 끼다 (kkida).
--- word ---
term: 끼는
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 끼다 + -는
meaning: that is coating/stuck
notes: 끼다 (kkida) means 'to be stuck,' 'to be coated,' or 'to be caught.' The present adnominal ending -는 (neun) modifies the following noun (느낌, neukkim - feeling), creating 'a feeling that something is coating/stuck.'
--- word ---
term: 느낌이잖아
type: noun + verb ending
breakdown: 느낌 + 이다 + -잖아
meaning: it's a feeling, you know
notes: 느낌 (neukkim) means 'feeling' or 'sensation.' 이다 (ida) is the copula 'to be.' -잖아 (jana) is a casual ending used to state something obvious or to seek agreement, similar to 'you know?' or 'isn't it?' It implies shared knowledge or experience. This is a common informal conversational ending.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄴ/는 N
meaning: This pattern forms an adnominal phrase, where a verb modifies a noun. -는 is used for present tense verbs, -(으)ㄴ for past tense verbs, and -(으)ㄹ for future tense verbs.
example: 밥 먹는 사람 (a person eating rice), 어제 본 영화 (a movie seen yesterday)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N이잖아
meaning: It's N, you know? / Isn't it N? Used to state something obvious or to seek agreement, implying shared knowledge.
example: 내 친구잖아! (It's my friend, you know!)
level: Intermediate

=== [17:51] 그러니까 그러면 반지도 너무 미끄럽게 들어갈 거 같애 ===

meaning: so, therefore

--- word ---
term: 그러면
type: conjunction
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)면
meaning: if so, in that case
notes: 그렇다 (geureota) means 'to be so' or 'to be like that.' The conditional ending -(으)면 (eumyeon) means 'if' or 'when.' So, 그러면 (geureomyeon) means 'if so' or 'in that case.' It's often contracted to 그럼 (geureom) in casual speech.

term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, excessively
notes: 너무 (neomu) means 'too' or 'excessively.' It can be used with both positive and negative connotations, though traditionally it was more for negative. In modern colloquial Korean, it's often used simply to mean 'very' or 'really' without a negative implication.
--- word ---
term: 미끄럽게
type: adjective + adverbial ending
breakdown: 미끄럽다 + -게
meaning: slippery + adverbial ending
notes: 미끄럽다 (mikkeureopda) means 'to be slippery.' The ending -게 (ge) turns an adjective into an adverb, describing how an action is performed. So, 미끄럽게 (mikkeureopge) means 'slippery-ly' or 'in a slippery way.'

--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: 거 (geo) is a common colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), which means 'thing' or 'fact.' It's frequently used in patterns like V-(으)ㄹ 것 같다.
--- word ---
term: 같애
type: adjective (contraction)
breakdown: 같다 + -아요
meaning: seems like, looks like
notes: 같애 (gatae) is a common informal contraction of 같아요 (gatayo), the polite informal form of 같다 (gatda - to be like, to seem). It expresses conjecture or similarity. Hyejin uses this informal form, consistent with her casual interaction with fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄹ 것 같다
meaning: Used to express a conjecture or prediction about a future event or a current situation. It means 'It seems like V will...' or 'I think V will...'
example: 비가 올 것 같아요. (It seems like it will rain.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: A-게 V
meaning: This pattern transforms an adjective (A) into an adverb, describing the manner in which a verb (V) is performed. It means 'V in an A way' or 'V to be A.'
example: 예쁘게 말하다 (to speak beautifully), 조용하게 공부하다 (to study quietly)
level: Intermediate

meaning: even if it comes out / allowed to come out

meaning: that is allowed to be / that can be

=== [18:30] 진짜 너무 당황스러울 거 같거든? ===
meaning: I think I'd be really, really flustered, you know?
tone: Informal, reflective, slightly exasperated but still humorous.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: An adverb used for emphasis, meaning 'really' or 'truly'. Common in casual conversation.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: An adverb meaning 'too' or 'very'. Can be used positively or negatively, but often emphasizes an extreme degree.
--- word ---
term: 당황스러울
type: adjective
breakdown: 당황스럽다 + -을
meaning: would be flustered/embarrassed
notes: 당황스럽다 (danghwangseureopda) means 'to be flustered', 'to be embarrassed', or 'to be taken aback'. -을 (eul) is a future tense or conjectural adjectival modifier, indicating a hypothetical situation or a speaker's guess.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: A colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact'. Often used in the grammar pattern -ㄹ/을 것 같다.
--- word ---
term: 같거든?
type: verb ending + particle
breakdown: 같다 + -거든 + ?
meaning: I think it would be... you know?
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like' or 'to seem'. When combined with -ㄹ/을 것, it forms -ㄹ/을 것 같다 (eul geot gatda), meaning 'to seem like' or 'I think that'. It expresses conjecture or opinion. -거든 (geodeun) is a casual sentence ending used to provide background information or a reason, often implying 'you know' or 'because'. The question mark makes it a rhetorical question, inviting agreement or understanding from the listener.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄹ/을 것 같다
meaning: To seem like; I think that. Expresses conjecture, opinion, or likelihood.
example: 비가 올 것 같아요. (Biga ol geot gatayo.) - I think it's going to rain.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -거든
meaning: You know; because. Provides background information or a reason, often with a casual or explanatory tone.
example: 이거 맛있거든. (Igeo masitgeodeun.) - This is delicious, you know.
level: Intermediate

=== [18:33] 근데 너무 어이가 없어서 ===
meaning: But because it's so absurd...
tone: Informal, explanatory, leading into the next thought.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: conjunction (contraction)
breakdown: 그런데
meaning: but, however
notes: A colloquial contraction of 그런데 (geureonde), meaning 'but' or 'however'. Used to introduce a contrasting or additional point.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: An adverb meaning 'too' or 'very'. Can be used positively or negatively, but often emphasizes an extreme degree.
--- word ---
term: 어이가
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 어이 + -가
meaning: reason, sense (subject)
notes: 어이 (eoi) means 'reason', 'sense', or 'logic'. -가 (ga) is a subject particle. This is part of the idiom 어이가 없다.
--- word ---
term: 없어서
type: adjective + ending
breakdown: 없다 + -아서
meaning: because it's lacking / because it's absurd
notes: 없다 (eopda) means 'to not exist' or 'to be lacking'. 어이가 없다 (eoi-ga eopda) is an idiom meaning 'to be dumbfounded', 'to be speechless', or 'to be absurd'. It describes a situation so ridiculous or unexpected that one doesn't know how to react. -아서/어서 (aseo/eoseo) is a conjunctive ending indicating cause or reason, meaning 'because' or 'so'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아서/어서
meaning: Because; so. Connects two clauses, indicating that the first clause is the reason or cause for the second.
example: 배고파서 밥을 먹었어요. (Baegopaseo bab-eul meogeosseoyo.) - I ate because I was hungry.
level: Beginner

=== [18:39] 그 자리에서 배잡고 웃을 거 같애 ===
meaning: I think I'd hold my stomach and laugh right there.
tone: Informal, descriptive, humorous, expressing a strong reaction.
--- word ---
term: 그
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: that
notes: A demonstrative determiner meaning 'that'.
--- word ---
term: 자리에서
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 자리 + -에서
meaning: on the spot, right there
notes: 자리 (jari) means 'spot' or 'place'. -에서 (eseo) is a locative particle. Together, '그 자리에서' means 'right there' or 'on the spot'.
--- word ---
term: 배잡고
type: idiom + conjunction
breakdown: 배를 잡다 + -고
meaning: holding my stomach and
notes: 배를 잡다 (baereul japda) is an idiom meaning 'to hold one's stomach (from laughing hard)'. -고 (go) is a conjunctive ending meaning 'and' or 'then', connecting sequential actions.
--- word ---
term: 웃을
type: verb
breakdown: 웃다 + -을
meaning: would laugh
notes: 웃다 (utda) means 'to laugh'. -을 (eul) is a future tense or conjectural adjectival modifier, indicating a hypothetical action.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: A colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact'. Used here as part of the -ㄹ/을 것 같애 pattern.
--- word ---
term: 같애
type: verb ending (informal)
breakdown: 같다 + -아/어
meaning: I think / it seems
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like' or 'to seem'. -애 (-ae) is a common informal contraction of -아/어 (a/eo) when used with 같다 (gatda) in casual speech, making '같아' (gata) sound like '같애' (gatae). This completes the -ㄹ/을 것 같다 pattern, expressing conjecture.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고
meaning: And; then. Connects two or more verbs or clauses, indicating a sequence of actions or a list of facts.
example: 밥을 먹고 커피를 마셨어요. (Bab-eul meokgo keopireul masyeosseoyo.) - I ate and then drank coffee.
level: Beginner

=== [18:40] 너무 얼탱이가 없어가지고 ===
meaning: Because it's so utterly absurd...
tone: Informal, emphatic, reinforcing the previous statement.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: An adverb meaning 'too' or 'very'. Can be used positively or negatively, but often emphasizes an extreme degree.
--- word ---
term: 얼탱이가
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 얼탱이 + -가
meaning: sense, logic (subject)
notes: 얼탱이 (eoltaeng-i) is a highly colloquial and more emphatic variant of 어이 (eoi), meaning 'sense' or 'logic'. -가 (ga) is a subject particle. This is part of the idiom 얼탱이가 없다.
--- word ---
term: 없어가지고
type: adjective + ending (colloquial)
breakdown: 없다 + -아가지고
meaning: because it's lacking / because it's utterly absurd
notes: 없다 (eopda) means 'to not exist' or 'to be lacking'. 얼탱이가 없다 (eoltaeng-i-ga eopda) is a highly colloquial and more emphatic version of 어이가 없다 (eoi-ga eopda), meaning 'to be utterly dumbfounded', 'to be completely absurd', or 'to be speechless'. It conveys a stronger sense of disbelief or ridiculousness. -아가지고/어가지고 (agajigo/eogajigo) is a colloquial variant of -아서/어서 (aseo/eoseo), indicating cause or reason, often used in spoken language.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아가지고/어가지고
meaning: Because; so. A colloquial and more emphatic version of -아서/어서, indicating cause or reason. Commonly used in spoken Korean.
example: 피곤해가지고 일찍 잤어요. (Pigonhaegajigo iljjik jasseoyo.) - Because I was tired, I slept early.
level: Intermediate

=== [18:44] 아니면 아이스크림 먹는데 반지 ===
meaning: Or, while eating ice cream, a ring...
tone: Informal, brainstorming, trailing off as the speaker imagines another scenario.
--- word ---
term: 아니면
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: or, if not
notes: Used to introduce an alternative possibility or scenario.
--- word ---
term: 아이스크림
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: ice cream
notes: A common dessert.
--- word ---
term: 먹는데
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 먹다 + -는데
meaning: while eating
notes: 먹다 (meokda) means 'to eat'. -는데 (neunde) is a conjunctive ending that can mean 'while', 'when', 'but', or 'and'. Here, it sets the scene for an action happening concurrently or as background.
--- word ---
term: 반지
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: ring
notes: The sentence is cut off here, implying a continuation like '반지가 나오면' (if a ring comes out) or '반지가 있으면' (if there's a ring), similar to the gukbap scenario. This creates a sense of the speaker thinking aloud and trailing off.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데/은데/는데
meaning: While; when; but; and. Provides background information, contrasts, or connects clauses sequentially.
example: 공부하는데 친구가 왔어요. (Gongbuhaneunde chinguga wasseoyo.) - While I was studying, my friend came.
level: Beginner

=== [18:49] 그건 좀 위험할 것 같아 ===
meaning: That might be a bit dangerous.
tone: Informal, speculative, slightly cautious.
--- word ---
term: 그건
type: pronoun + topic particle
breakdown: 그것 + -은
meaning: that (as for that)
notes: 그것 (geugeot) means 'that thing'. -은/는 (eun/neun) is the topic particle. 그건 (geugeon) is a common contraction of 그것은 (geugeoseun) in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a bit, a little
notes: Short for 조금 (jogeum). Used to soften a statement or indicate a small degree. Very common in spoken Korean, informal.
--- word ---
term: 위험할
type: adjective + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 위험하다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: dangerous (will be)
notes: 위험하다 (wiheomhada) means 'to be dangerous'. -(으)ㄹ (eu/l) is an adnominal ending that modifies a following noun, often indicating future tense or conjecture. Here, it's part of the 'seems like' construction.
--- word ---
term: 것
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: thing, fact
notes: A common dependent noun used in various grammatical constructions, often referring to an abstract 'thing' or 'fact'.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: descriptive verb ending
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: seems like, I think
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like, to seem'. -아/어 (a/eo) is an informal, polite ending. The full pattern -(으)ㄹ 것 같다 (eu/l geot gatda) means 'it seems like, I think it will be...'. This is a common way to express conjecture or personal opinion in both formal and informal contexts.

=== [18:53] 내가 아이스크림을 너무 좋아하다 보니까 ===
meaning: Because I like ice cream so much...
tone: Informal, explanatory, slightly self-deprecating.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: pronoun + subject particle
breakdown: 나 + -가
meaning: I (subject)
notes: 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I'. -가 (ga) is the subject particle. 내가 (naega) is used when 'I' is the subject of the sentence. Contrast with 나는 (naneun) which marks 'I' as the topic.
--- word ---
term: 아이스크림을
type: noun + object particle
breakdown: 아이스크림 + -을
meaning: ice cream (object)
notes: 아이스크림 (aiseukeurim) is 'ice cream'. -을/를 (eul/reul) is the object particle, marking the direct object of a verb.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too much, very
notes: 너무 (neomu) means 'too much' or 'excessively'. In modern colloquial Korean, it's often used simply to mean 'very' or 'really', similar to 정말 (jeongmal) or 아주 (aju), even in positive contexts.
--- word ---
term: 좋아하다
type: verb
breakdown: 
meaning: to like
notes: 좋아하다 (joahada) means 'to like'. It's a common verb. The base form is used here before the -다 보니까 ending.
--- word ---
term: 보니까
type: verb ending
breakdown: 보다 + -(으)니까
meaning: because, as (I was doing/realized)
notes: This is a contraction of -다 보니까 (da bonikka). The full pattern -(으)니까 (eu/nikka) indicates a reason or cause. The -다 보니까 (da bonikka) pattern specifically implies that 'as one was doing something, they realized or discovered something, or something happened as a result'. It often carries a nuance of 'because I've been doing X, Y happened/I realized Y'. Informal.

=== [18:55] 뭐 있는 줄도 모르고 ===
meaning: I wouldn't even know there was something in it.
tone: Informal, hypothetical, slightly exaggerated.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: something, what
notes: 뭐 (mwo) is an informal contraction of 무엇 (mueot), meaning 'what'. It can also mean 'something' in certain contexts, as it does here.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: existing, being
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to exist, to be'. -는 (neun) is the present tense adnominal ending, modifying the following noun (줄 in this case). So, 'something that exists'.
--- word ---
term: 줄도
type: dependent noun + particle
breakdown: 줄 + -도
meaning: the fact that (even)
notes: 줄 (jul) is a dependent noun used in the pattern -(으)ㄴ/는 줄 알다/모르다 (eu/n/neun jul alda/moreuda), meaning 'to know/not know that...'. -도 (do) means 'also' or 'even', adding emphasis that she wouldn't *even* know.
--- word ---
term: 모르고
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 모르다 + -고
meaning: not knowing (and)
notes: 모르다 (moreuda) means 'to not know'. -고 (go) is a connective ending meaning 'and' or 'and then'. Here it connects the state of 'not knowing' to the potential action of 'swallowing'.

=== [18:58] 꼴딱 삼켜버릴 수도 있어 ===
meaning: I might just gulp it down.
tone: Informal, hypothetical, slightly humorous/exaggerated.
--- word ---
term: 꼴딱
type: adverb/onomatopoeia
breakdown: 
meaning: gulp, with a gulping sound
notes: An onomatopoeic adverb describing the sound or action of quickly swallowing something. Adds a vivid, informal touch.
--- word ---
term: 삼켜버릴
type: verb + auxiliary verb + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 삼키다 + -아/어 버리다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: swallow completely (will)
notes: 삼키다 (samkida) means 'to swallow'. -아/어 버리다 (a/eo beorida) is an auxiliary verb construction meaning 'to do something completely' or 'to finish doing something', often with a nuance of regret or finality. Here, it emphasizes the quick, complete action. -(으)ㄹ (eu/l) is the future adnominal ending, part of the -(으)ㄹ 수도 있다 (eu/l sudo itda) possibility pattern.
--- word ---
term: 수도
type: dependent noun + particle
breakdown: 수 + -도
meaning: possibility (even)
notes: 수 (su) is a dependent noun meaning 'way, method, possibility'. -도 (do) means 'even' or 'also', emphasizing the possibility. It's part of the common pattern -(으)ㄹ 수도 있다 (eu/l sudo itda), meaning 'it is possible that, might, could'.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: verb ending
breakdown: 있다 + -어
meaning: there is, it exists
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to exist, to be'. -아/어 (a/eo) is an informal, polite ending. The full pattern -(으)ㄹ 수도 있다 (eu/l sudo itda) means 'it is possible that, might, could'. Informal.

=== [19:00] 화장실 나올 때 딱 반지 주면 어때? ===
meaning: How about giving me the ring right when I come out of the bathroom?
tone: Informal, playful, suggestive, humorous. This is a continuation of the proposal discussion.
--- word ---
term: 화장실
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: bathroom, restroom
notes: 화장실 (hwajangsil) means 'bathroom' or 'restroom'. A common noun.
--- word ---
term: 나올
type: verb + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 나오다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: come out (will)
notes: 나오다 (naoda) means 'to come out'. -(으)ㄹ (eu/l) is the future adnominal ending, modifying the following noun 때 (ttae). So, 'the time when one will come out'.
--- word ---
term: 때
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: when, at the time of
notes: 때 (ttae) means 'time' or 'when'. It follows a verb in adnominal form (e.g., -(으)ㄹ 때) to indicate 'when [action] happens'.
--- word ---
term: 딱
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: exactly, precisely, just
notes: 딱 (ttak) is an adverb indicating exactness, precision, or a sudden, fitting moment. It adds emphasis to 'right when'.
--- word ---
term: 반지
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: ring
notes: 반지 (banji) means 'ring'. This is a key item in the proposal discussion.
--- word ---
term: 주면
type: verb + conditional ending
breakdown: 주다 + -(으)면
meaning: if you give
notes: 주다 (juda) means 'to give'. -(으)면 (eu/myeon) is a conditional ending meaning 'if' or 'when'. So, 'if you give'.
--- word ---
term: 어때?
type: interrogative adjective + informal ending
breakdown: 어떻다 + -어
meaning: how about?
notes: 어떻다 (eotteota) means 'how is it?'. 어때? (eottae?) is an informal contraction of 어때요? (eottaeyo?) and means 'How about it?' or 'What do you think?'. It's commonly used to ask for an opinion or make a suggestion. Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Used to indicate the time when an action or state occurs. It means 'when' or 'at the time of'.
example: 밥 먹을 때 (bap meogeul ttae) - when eating a meal
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)면 어때?
meaning: Used to suggest an idea or action and ask for an opinion on it. It means 'How about if...?' or 'What if...?'
example: 우리 영화 볼까? (uri yeonghwa bolkka?) - Shall we watch a movie? / 영화 보면 어때? (yeonghwa bomyeon eottae?) - How about watching a movie?
level: Beginner

=== [19:17] 생각을 안 해봤는데 잠깐만 ===
meaning: I haven't thought about it, but wait a minute.
tone: Informal, contemplative, transitional.
--- word ---
term: 생각을
type: noun + object particle
breakdown: 생각 + -을
meaning: thought (object)
notes: 생각 (saenggak) means 'thought' or 'idea'. -을/를 (eul/reul) is the object particle. 생각을 하다 (saenggageul hada) means 'to think'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: 안 (an) is a short-form negation adverb placed directly before the verb or adjective it negates. It means 'not'.
--- word ---
term: 해봤는데
type: verb + past tense + connective ending
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어 보다 + -았/었- + -는데
meaning: tried doing (but)
notes: 하다 (hada) means 'to do'. -아/어 보다 (a/eo boda) means 'to try doing something' or 'to experience doing something'. -았/었- (at/eot-) is the past tense marker. -는데 (neunde) is a connective ending that provides background information, introduces a contrasting idea ('but'), or leads into the next statement ('so'). Informal.
--- word ---
term: 잠깐만
type: adverb + particle
breakdown: 잠깐 + -만
meaning: just a moment, wait a minute
notes: 잠깐 (jamkkan) means 'a moment'. -만 (man) means 'only' or 'just'. 잠깐만 (jamkkanman) is a common phrase used to ask someone to wait briefly. Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 보다
meaning: Attached to a verb stem to express trying to do something, or experiencing doing something. It means 'to try to do' or 'to have done/experienced'.
example: 김치 먹어 봤어요? (gimchi meogeo bwasseoyo?) - Have you tried kimchi?
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데
meaning: A versatile connective ending that can introduce background information, express a contrast ('but'), or lead to a subsequent action or thought ('so', 'and').
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (biga oneunde usani eopseoyo.) - It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.
level: Intermediate

=== [19:22] 화장실 나올 때 반지를 준다 ===
meaning: Giving the ring when coming out of the bathroom.
tone: Informal, contemplative, repeating a suggestion to herself.
--- word ---
term: 화장실
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: bathroom, restroom
notes: 화장실 (hwajangsil) means 'bathroom' or 'restroom'. A common noun.
--- word ---
term: 나올
type: verb + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 나오다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: come out (will)
notes: 나오다 (naoda) means 'to come out'. -(으)ㄹ (eu/l) is the future adnominal ending, modifying the following noun 때 (ttae). So, 'the time when one will come out'.
--- word ---
term: 때
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: when, at the time of
notes: 때 (ttae) means 'time' or 'when'. It follows a verb in adnominal form (e.g., -(으)ㄹ 때) to indicate 'when [action] happens'.
--- word ---
term: 반지를
type: noun + object particle
breakdown: 반지 + -를
meaning: ring (object)
notes: 반지 (banji) means 'ring'. -을/를 (eul/reul) is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 준다
type: verb ending (plain form)
breakdown: 주다 + -ㄴ다
meaning: gives
notes: 주다 (juda) means 'to give'. -ㄴ다/는다 (n/neunda) is the plain form (해라체, haerache) declarative ending. It's used when talking to oneself, writing, or making a factual statement in a very informal, non-polite way. Here, Hyejin is repeating the idea to herself, processing it.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Used to indicate the time when an action or state occurs. It means 'when' or 'at the time of'.

level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄴ다/는다 (plain form)
meaning: The declarative ending for the plain form (해라체, haerache), used for statements of fact, often when talking to oneself, in writing, or when speaking to someone of lower status in a very informal context.
example: 밥 먹는다. (bap meongneunda.) - (I/He/She) eats rice.
level: Intermediate

=== [19:29] 가장 시원할 때 ===
meaning: When it's most refreshing/relieving.
tone: Informal, humorous, suggestive. This is a punchline related to the bathroom scenario.
--- word ---
term: 가장
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: most
notes: 가장 (gajang) is an adverb meaning 'most' or 'the most', used to form superlatives when placed before an adjective or adverb.
--- word ---
term: 시원할
type: adjective + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 시원하다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: refreshing, cool, relieved (will be)
notes: 시원하다 (siwonhada) means 'to be cool, refreshing'. It can also mean 'to feel relieved' or 'to feel refreshed' after an uncomfortable situation, which is the humorous implication here in the context of coming out of the bathroom. -(으)ㄹ (eu/l) is the future adnominal ending, modifying 때 (ttae).
--- word ---
term: 때
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: when, at the time of
notes: 때 (ttae) means 'time' or 'when'. It follows a verb or adjective in adnominal form (e.g., -(으)ㄹ 때) to indicate 'when [state/action] occurs'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 가장 + Adjective/Adverb
meaning: Used to form the superlative degree, meaning 'the most [adjective/adverb]'.
example: 가장 예쁜 꽃 (gajang yeppeun kkot) - the most beautiful flower
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 때
meaning: Used to indicate the time when an action or state occurs. It means 'when' or 'at the time of'.
example: 졸릴 때 (jollil ttae) - when feeling sleepy
level: Beginner

=== [19:32] 아 그러네 기분 너무 좋아서 ===
meaning: Oh, that's right. Because I'd be in such a good mood...
tone: Informal, reflective, setting up a hypothetical scenario.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Oh
notes: An exclamation used to express realization, surprise, or a sudden thought. It's very common in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 그러네
type: verb ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -네
meaning: that's right / I see
notes: A contraction of 그렇다 ('to be so, to be right') and the 감탄형 종결어미 (-네), an exclamatory ending. It expresses the speaker's realization or agreement with something just observed or heard. It's informal and often used to acknowledge a new piece of information or a sudden thought. For example, '비 오네' (It's raining, I see) or '맛있네' (It's delicious, I see).
--- word ---
term: 기분
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: mood, feeling
notes: Refers to one's emotional state or feeling. Common phrases include '기분이 좋다' (to be in a good mood) and '기분이 나쁘다' (to be in a bad mood).
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very, excessively
notes: Originally meant 'too much' or 'excessively' with a negative connotation, but in modern colloquial Korean, it's widely used as an intensifier meaning 'very' or 'so' in both positive and negative contexts. For example, '너무 예쁘다' (so pretty) or '너무 좋다' (so good).
--- word ---
term: 좋아서
type: adjective + connective ending
breakdown: 좋다 + -아서
meaning: because it's good / being good
notes: Combines the adjective 좋다 ('to be good') with the connective ending -아서/어서. This ending indicates cause or reason, meaning 'because' or 'so'. It's used when the preceding clause is the reason for the following clause. It cannot be used with past tense in the first clause (e.g., '좋았어서' is incorrect). Instead, you'd say '좋았기 때문에'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아서/어서
meaning: Indicates the reason or cause for the action or state in the following clause. It can be translated as 'because', 'since', or 'so'.
example: 배고파서 밥 먹었어요. (I ate because I was hungry.)
level: Beginner

=== [19:34] 막 프러포즈 그냥 받아버릴 수도 있겠다 그치? ===
meaning: I might just end up accepting a proposal, right?
tone: Informal, humorous, speculative, rhetorical. Hyejin is continuing the playful discussion about absurd proposal scenarios.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, randomly, wildly, without much thought
notes: In this context, '막' implies an action done impulsively, without much planning or consideration. It adds a sense of spontaneity or even recklessness. It can also mean 'just now' or 'at the last minute' in other contexts.
--- word ---
term: 프러포즈
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: proposal (marriage)
notes: A loanword from English 'proposal', specifically referring to a marriage proposal. Often used with the verb 하다 (to do) as '프러포즈하다' (to propose).
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, simply, without any particular reason
notes: Indicates that an action is done without a specific purpose, simply, or as is. Here, it reinforces the idea of accepting 'just like that' or 'without much fuss'.
--- word ---
term: 받아버릴
type: verb + auxiliary verb + future modifier
breakdown: 받다 + -아버리다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: will end up accepting (completely/regrettably)
notes: Combines 받다 ('to receive, accept') with the auxiliary verb -아/어버리다, which emphasizes the completion of an action, often with a nuance of regret, relief, or doing something completely/irrevocably. Here, it suggests accepting a proposal impulsively or without much thought, perhaps with a slight humorous 'oh well' feeling. The -(으)ㄹ is a future adnominal ending, modifying the following noun '수'.
--- word ---
term: 수도
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 수 + -도
meaning: possibility also
notes: 수 refers to 'a way, a method, a possibility'. The particle -도 means 'also' or 'even'. Together, '수도 있다' means 'there is also a possibility' or 'it could also be that...'. This construction is used to express possibility or conjecture. The full pattern is -(으)ㄹ 수도 있다.
--- word ---
term: 있겠다
type: verb + conjecture ending
breakdown: 있다 + -겠- + -다
meaning: it might be / I guess it would be
notes: Combines 있다 ('to exist, to be') with the suffix -겠- and the declarative ending -다. The suffix -겠- here expresses the speaker's conjecture or assumption about a future event or state. It's often translated as 'I guess', 'I suppose', or 'it seems like it would be'. It can also express the speaker's will or intention.
--- word ---
term: 그치?
type: interjection/question particle
breakdown: 그렇다 + -지?
meaning: right? / isn't it?
notes: A common informal contraction of 그렇지?. It's used to seek agreement or confirmation from the listener, similar to 'right?' or 'isn't it?' in English. It implies that the speaker expects agreement.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어버리다
meaning: Attached to a verb stem, it indicates that an action has been completed, often with a nuance of regret, relief, or doing something completely/irrevocably. It can also imply an action done impulsively.
example: 숙제를 다 해버렸어. (I finished all my homework (completely).)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 수도 있다
meaning: Expresses possibility or conjecture, meaning 'there is also a possibility that...' or 'it could also be that...'.
example: 내일 비가 올 수도 있어요. (It might also rain tomorrow.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -겠- (conjecture)
meaning: Used to express the speaker's conjecture, assumption, or strong belief about a situation or future event. It can also express the speaker's will or intention.
example: 맛있겠네요! (It looks delicious!)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지?
meaning: Attached to a verb or adjective, it forms a question that seeks confirmation or agreement from the listener. It implies that the speaker expects a 'yes' or agreement.
example: 이거 예쁘지? (This is pretty, isn't it?)
level: Beginner

=== [19:39] 진짜 세계 최초다 ===

meaning: world

meaning: it's the first / a world first

--- grammar ---
pattern: -이다 (copula)
meaning: The Korean copula 'to be', used to equate a noun with another noun or to describe a noun. It conjugates based on formality and the preceding noun's final sound.

=== [19:41] 그래서 아 다 싸고 나왔어 했는데 ===
meaning: So, "Oh, I finished everything and came out," I said, but...
tone: Informal, narrative, setting up a punchline. Hyejin is recounting a hypothetical scenario.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: A common connective adverb indicating a result or consequence. It links the preceding statement as the reason for the following one.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Oh
notes: Same as in segment 0, expressing a sudden thought or realization within the hypothetical scenario.
--- word ---
term: 다
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: all, completely
notes: Indicates that an action is done entirely or that something is finished. Here, it refers to finishing one's business in the restroom (implied by the context of the previous segments about proposals after using the restroom).
--- word ---
term: 싸고
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 싸다 + -고
meaning: to finish (one's business) and
notes: 싸다 has multiple meanings, including 'to wrap', 'to pack', or colloquially, 'to excrete' (e.g., '똥 싸다' for 'to poop'). In this humorous context (following the discussion of proposals after using the restroom), it implies finishing one's business. The -고 ending connects sequential actions or lists items.
--- word ---
term: 나왔어
type: verb
breakdown: 나오다 + -았어
meaning: came out
notes: The past tense informal conjugation of 나오다 ('to come out'). -았어/었어 is the casual past tense ending used in 반말 (banmal) speech.
--- word ---
term: 했는데

breakdown: 하다 + -았는데
meaning: said/did, but / when I said/did
notes: Combines 하다 ('to do, to say') with the past tense suffix -았- and the connective ending -는데. The -는데/은데/는데 ending provides background information, expresses contrast, or sets up a subsequent statement. Here, it sets up the next action as something happening 'when I said/did that' or 'but then'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 (sequential action)
meaning: Connects two or more actions that occur in sequence, or lists items. It can be translated as 'and' or 'and then'.
example: 밥 먹고 영화 봤어요. (I ate and then watched a movie.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -았/었어 (informal past tense)
meaning: The informal, casual past tense ending used in 반말 (banmal) speech, typically among close friends or to subordinates.
example: 어제 뭐 했어? (What did you do yesterday?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데/은데/는데
meaning: Provides background information, sets the scene for the following clause, expresses contrast, or indicates that the speaker is expecting a response or further information. It can be translated as 'but', 'and', 'so', or 'when/while'.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Intermediate

=== [19:44] 어 혜진아 이러면서 반지 껴주면 ===
meaning: "Oh, Hyejin-ah," they say, and then put a ring on my finger...
tone: Informal, narrative, hypothetical. Continuing the absurd proposal scenario.
--- word ---
term: 어
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Oh
notes: Similar to '아', an exclamation expressing surprise or calling attention.
--- word ---
term: 혜진아
type: proper noun + vocative particle
breakdown: 혜진 + -아
meaning: Hyejin-ah (calling Hyejin)
notes: 혜진 is the speaker's name. -아/야 is an informal vocative particle used to call someone's name. -아 is used after a name ending in a consonant, and -야 after a name ending in a vowel. This particle is only used in casual speech (반말).
--- word ---
term: 이러면서
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 이러다 + -면서
meaning: while saying/doing this
notes: 이러다 means 'to do/say this way'. The -면서/으면서 ending indicates that two actions occur simultaneously or that one action is performed while another is happening. Here, it means 'while saying (my name) and doing (something else)'.
--- word ---
term: 반지
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: ring
notes: Refers to a finger ring, commonly associated with engagements or marriage.
--- word ---
term: 껴주면
type: verb + auxiliary verb + conditional ending
breakdown: 끼다 + -어주다 + -(으)면
meaning: if they put on (for me)
notes: 끼다 means 'to put on (a ring, gloves, glasses)'. -어주다 is an auxiliary verb indicating that an action is done as a favor or for the benefit of someone else. -(으)면 is a conditional ending meaning 'if' or 'when'. So, 'if someone puts a ring on (for me)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어주다
meaning: Attached to a verb stem, it indicates that the action of the verb is performed as a favor or for the benefit of someone else. It can be translated as 'to do (something) for someone'.
example: 문 좀 열어 주세요. (Please open the door for me.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)면
meaning: A conditional ending meaning 'if' or 'when'. It introduces a condition or assumption.
example: 비가 오면 집에 있을 거예요. (If it rains, I will stay home.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)면서
meaning: Indicates that two actions or states occur simultaneously. It can be translated as 'while (doing something)' or 'at the same time as'.
example: 밥을 먹으면서 TV를 봐요. (I watch TV while eating.)
level: Intermediate

=== [19:46] 난 그것도 배잡고 웃을 것 같아 ===
meaning: I think I'd also hold my stomach and laugh.
tone: Informal, humorous, speculative. Hyejin's reaction to the absurd scenario.
--- word ---
term: 난
type: pronoun + topic particle
breakdown: 나 + -는
meaning: I (topic)
notes: A contraction of 나 ('I', informal) and the topic particle -는/은. Used to mark the topic of the sentence. '나' is used when speaking informally.
--- word ---
term: 그것도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 그것 + -도
meaning: that also / even that
notes: 그것 means 'that thing'. The particle -도 means 'also' or 'even'. Here, it implies 'even that (absurd scenario)'. The speaker is saying that this particular scenario, in addition to others, would also make her laugh.
--- word ---
term: 배잡고
type: noun + verb + connective ending
breakdown: 배 + 잡다 + -고
meaning: holding (my) stomach and
notes: 배 means 'stomach' or 'belly'. 잡다 means 'to hold' or 'to grab'. '배 잡고 웃다' is an idiomatic expression meaning 'to laugh so hard that you hold your stomach', indicating extreme laughter.
--- word ---
term: 웃을
type: verb + future adnominal ending
breakdown: 웃다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: will laugh / to laugh
notes: 웃다 means 'to laugh'. The -(으)ㄹ ending is a future adnominal ending, used to modify a following noun (here, '것') and indicate a future action or possibility.
--- word ---
term: 것
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: thing, fact, what
notes: A dependent noun that often nominalizes the preceding clause. In the pattern -(으)ㄹ 것 같다, it functions to express conjecture or likelihood.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: adjective
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: it seems like / I think
notes: The informal conjugation of 같다 ('to be like, to seem'). When combined with a verb + -(으)ㄹ 것, it means 'it seems like (verb)' or 'I think (verb) will happen'. This expresses the speaker's opinion or conjecture.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는/은 (topic particle)
meaning: Marks the topic of the sentence, indicating what the sentence is about. It can also imply contrast.
example: 저는 한국 사람이에요. (I am Korean.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -도 (particle)
meaning: Means 'also', 'too', or 'even'. It attaches to nouns or pronouns.
example: 저도 갈게요. (I'll go too.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 것 같다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's conjecture or opinion about a future event or a current situation. It means 'it seems like', 'it looks like', or 'I think (it will)'.
example: 비가 올 것 같아요. (It looks like it will rain.)
level: Intermediate

=== [19:54] 아무도 없는 골목길에서 키트와 함께 주는 건? ===

--- word ---
term: 아무도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 아무 + -도
meaning: no one, nobody
notes: 아무 means 'any'. When combined with the particle -도 and followed by a negative verb, it means 'no one' or 'not anyone'. For example, '아무도 없어' (there's no one).
--- word ---
term: 없는
type: adjective
breakdown: 없다 + -는
meaning: not existing, without

type: noun + particle
breakdown: 골목길 + -에서

type: noun + particle
breakdown: 키트 + -와
meaning: with a kit
notes: 키트 is a loanword from English 'kit'. -와/과 is a particle meaning 'and' or 'with'. -와 is used after a noun ending in a vowel.
--- word ---
term: 함께
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: together
notes: An adverb meaning 'together' or 'along with'. It often follows a noun + -와/과 (e.g., '친구와 함께' - together with a friend).
--- word ---
term: 주는
type: verb + present adnominal ending
breakdown: 주다 + -는
meaning: giving
notes: 주다 means 'to give'. The -는 ending is a present descriptive adnominal ending, modifying the following noun '건' (것) to mean 'the act of giving'.
--- word ---
term: 건?
type: dependent noun + topic particle + question mark
breakdown: 것 + -은?
meaning: the thing? / how about?
notes: A contraction of 것은?. 것 is a dependent noun meaning 'thing' or 'fact'. -은 is the topic particle. When used at the end of a sentence with a question mark, -(으)ㄴ/는 건? is a common informal way to ask 'How about (doing) this?' or 'What about (this thing)?', often suggesting an idea or asking for an opinion.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 아무도 + negative verb
meaning: Used to express 'no one' or 'nobody'. '아무' means 'any', and '-도' means 'even'. When combined with a negative verb, it negates the existence of anyone.
example: 아무도 안 왔어요. (No one came.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 (present descriptive adnominal ending)
meaning: Attached to a verb stem, it modifies a following noun, indicating that the noun is currently performing the action of the verb. It translates to 'the (noun) that is (verb-ing)'.
example: 책을 읽는 사람 (the person who is reading a book)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -에서 (location of action)
meaning: Indicates the place where an action takes place. It can also indicate the starting point of an action or the source of something.
example: 학교에서 공부해요. (I study at school.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -와/과 함께
meaning: Means 'together with' or 'along with'. -와 is used after vowels, -과 after consonants.
example: 친구와 함께 영화를 봤어요. (I watched a movie with a friend.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ/는 건?
meaning: An informal way to suggest an idea or ask for an opinion, meaning 'How about (doing) this?' or 'What about (this thing)?'. It's a shortened form of -(으)ㄴ/는 것은 어때요/어때?
example: 우리 영화 보는 건? (How about us watching a movie?)
level: Intermediate

=== [20:08] 그럼 이제 나 아 뭐하는 짓이야 이게 ===
meaning: Then now, ah, what kind of act is this?
tone: Informal, rhetorical, exasperated, slightly self-admonishing or playfully critical.
--- word ---
term: 그럼
type: Adverb
breakdown: 그리하다 + -면 (contracted)
meaning: Then; In that case
notes: A contraction of '그리하면' (geuri-hamyeon), meaning 'if it is so' or 'if that's the case'. It acts as a transitional word, often meaning 'then' or 'in that case'. It's very common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Now (from this point onward)
notes: Refers to 'now' in the sense of a new state or a change from a previous one, or 'from now on'. It's often used when something is about to start or has just started. Compare with '지금' (jigeum), which refers to 'now' as the current moment without necessarily implying a change or new phase.
--- word ---
term: 나
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I; Me
notes: The informal first-person pronoun. The polite form is '저' (jeo). Used when speaking to people of equal or lower status, or when speaking to oneself in a casual context, as Hyejin is doing here.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah; Oh
notes: An exclamation used to express various emotions like realization, surprise, frustration, or a sigh. Here, it conveys a sense of exasperation or mild annoyance.
--- word ---
term: 뭐하는
type: Verb + Adjective form
breakdown: 뭐하다 + -는
meaning: What kind of... doing; What is... doing
notes: '뭐하다' (mwohada) means 'to do what' or 'to be what'. The '-는' (neun) ending is a present descriptive modifier, turning the verb into an adjective that modifies the following noun. So, '뭐하는 짓' means 'an act that is doing what' or 'what kind of act'.
--- word ---
term: 짓이야
type: Noun + Copula
breakdown: 짓 + -이야
meaning: It's an act/deed
notes: '짓' (jit) refers to an 'act' or 'deed', often implying a negative, foolish, or inappropriate action. '-이야' (iya) is the informal form of the copula '이다' (ida), meaning 'to be'. It's used after nouns ending in a consonant. If the noun ended in a vowel, it would be '-야' (ya). The rising intonation implies a question.
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: Pronoun + Particle (contraction)
breakdown: 이것 + -이 (contracted to 이게)
meaning: This (subject)
notes: A contraction of '이것이' (igeosi), where '이것' (igeot) means 'this thing' and '-이' (i) is the subject particle. '이게' is very common in spoken Korean. The full form '이것이' is more formal or emphatic.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 뭐하는 짓이야?
meaning: This is a common rhetorical question used to express exasperation, disapproval, or bewilderment at someone's (or one's own) actions. It literally means 'What kind of act is this?' but implies 'What on earth are you/I doing?' or 'This is ridiculous!'
example: 지금 뭐하는 짓이야? (What are you doing right now? - implying disapproval)
level: Beginner

meaning: Huh?; Right?

=== [20:16] 그래야지 ===
meaning: That's how it should be.
tone: Informal, assertive, slightly admonishing or self-correcting.
--- word ---
term: 그래야지
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -아야지
meaning: Should be so; That's how it should be
notes: Formed from '그렇다' (geureota, to be so/that way) and the ending '-아야지' (ayaji). This ending expresses obligation, strong recommendation, or a firm resolution. It can mean 'should/must do X' or 'X is the way it should be'. It often carries a nuance of self-admonishment or advising others, sometimes playfully.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아야지 / -어야지 / -여야지
meaning: This ending expresses obligation, strong recommendation, or a firm resolution. It means 'should/must do X' or 'X is the way it should be'. It can be used for self-admonishment, advising others, or expressing a strong expectation.

=== [20:22] 어? 이게 뭐하는 거야? 어? ===

type: Verb + Adjective form

=== [20:25] 창피한 줄 모르고 ===
meaning: Not knowing shame.
tone: Informal, critical, exasperated, implying shamelessness.
--- word ---
term: 창피한
type: Adjective
breakdown: 창피하다 + -ㄴ
meaning: Shameful; Embarrassing
notes: Derived from the adjective '창피하다' (changpihada), meaning 'to be shameful' or 'to be embarrassed'. The '-ㄴ' (n) ending is a descriptive modifier, turning the adjective into a form that modifies a noun (e.g., '창피한 사람' - a shameful person). Here, it modifies the implied '줄' (jul).
--- word ---
term: 줄
type: Noun (dependent)
breakdown: 
meaning: The fact that; The way
notes: A dependent noun used after verbs or adjectives to express 'the fact that', 'the way of doing something', or 'the knowledge of how to do something'. It often appears with '알다' (alda, to know) or '모르다' (moreuda, to not know). Here, '창피한 줄' means 'the fact of being shameful' or 'the shamefulness'.
--- word ---
term: 모르고
type: Verb + Conjunction
breakdown: 모르다 + -고
meaning: Not knowing and; Without knowing
notes: Formed from the verb '모르다' (moreuda, to not know) and the connective ending '-고' (go). This ending connects two clauses, often meaning 'and' or 'and then'. In this context, it implies 'without knowing' or 'while not knowing', suggesting a lack of awareness or disregard for the shamefulness of the situation. This phrase '창피한 줄 모르고' is a common idiom meaning 'shamelessly' or 'without an ounce of shame'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는/-(으)ㄴ/-(으)ㄹ 줄 모르다
meaning: This pattern means 'to not know the fact that...' or 'to not know how to...'. When used with an adjective like '창피하다' (to be shameful), it means 'to not know that one is shameful' or 'to be shameless'.
example: 그는 거짓말하는 줄도 모르고 있었다. (He didn't even know he was lying.)
level: Intermediate

--- word ---
term: 스타일이라고?
type: noun + quotative particle + interrogative

meaning: you're saying (it's) the type?

--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)라고 (하다)
meaning: Used for indirect quotation of nouns, meaning 'to say that it is [noun]'. The '하다' is often omitted in casual speech, especially when asking a question.
example: 학생이라고? (You're saying he's a student?)

term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: 안 (an) is a short-form negative adverb placed directly before the verb or adjective it negates. It means 'not'. For '하다' verbs, it typically comes before '하다', e.g., 안 좋아하다 (an joahada, not to like).
--- word ---

It can also express mild complaint or reminder, as it does here.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -밖에 안/못
meaning: This pattern means 'only' or 'nothing but'. It always combines with a negative verb or adjective (안/못).

--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아
meaning: Used to state something the speaker assumes the listener already knows or should know, often implying 'you know, right?' or 'don't you know?'. It can also express mild complaint or reminder.

term: 좀
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little, please
notes: 좀 (jom) is an adverb meaning 'a little' or 'a bit'. It's often used to soften requests or commands, making them more polite, similar to 'please' in English. It's a very common filler word in casual conversation.
--- word ---
term: 줘봐
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 주다 (to give) + -어 보다 (try doing) + -아 (informal imperative ending)
meaning: try giving (it to me)
notes: 줘봐 (jwobwa) is a contraction of 주어 봐. 주다 (juda) means 'to give'. -어 보다 (eo boda) means 'to try doing something'. The final -아 (a) is an informal imperative ending, making it a casual command or request. So, 'try giving (me some examples)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지 못하다
meaning: This pattern expresses inability or impossibility, meaning 'cannot do' or 'be unable to do'. It's more formal than just using 못 before the verb.
example: 저는 한국어를 잘하지 못해요. (Jeoneun hangugeoreul jalhaji mothaeyo.) - I cannot speak Korean well.

--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄹ/을 (adnominal ending)
meaning: This ending attaches to verbs to modify a following noun, indicating a future action, a potential action, or a supposition. It can be translated as 'will do', 'to do', or 'that would do'.
example: 먹을 음식 (meogeul eumsik) - food to eat / food that will be eaten

--- grammar ---

meaning: This auxiliary verb construction means 'to try doing something' or 'to experience doing something'.
example: 김치를 먹어 봤어요. (Kimchireul meogeo bwasseoyo.) - I tried eating kimchi (I've eaten kimchi before).

=== [21:12] 그랬어? ===
meaning: Was it like that? / Did I say that?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly surprised, reacting to a fan's comment.
--- word ---
term: 그랬어?
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 그렇다 (to be so) + -었- (past tense) + -어? (informal interrogative ending)
meaning: was it so? / did I say that?
notes: 그랬어? (geuraesseo?) is a contraction of 그러하였어?. It's the informal past tense interrogative form of 그렇다 (geureota, to be so). In this context, Hyejin is likely reacting to a fan's comment, asking if what they said (or what she said earlier) was 'like that'.

=== [21:15] 잘못 났다 싶은 일상에서의 프러포즈? ===
meaning: A proposal in everyday life that makes you think, 'This went wrong'?
tone: Informal, questioning, humorous, reflective.
--- word ---
term: 잘못
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: wrongly, mistakenly
notes: 잘못 (jalmot) is an adverb meaning 'wrongly' or 'mistakenly'. It can also function as a noun meaning 'mistake' or 'fault'. Here it modifies '났다'.
--- word ---
term: 났다
type: verb
breakdown: 나다 (to happen/occur) + -았- (past tense)
meaning: happened, occurred
notes: 났다 (natda) is the past tense of 나다 (nada), meaning 'to happen', 'to occur', 'to come out', or 'to break out'. In this context, '잘못 났다' implies something 'went wrong' or 'turned out wrong'.
--- word ---
term: 싶은
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 싶다 (to want to/to feel like) + -은 (adnominal ending)
meaning: that one feels/thinks
notes: 싶은 (sipeun) is the adnominal form of 싶다 (sipta), meaning 'to want to' or 'to feel like'. When used with a preceding clause (e.g., 잘못 났다 싶은), it means 'that one feels/thinks [the preceding clause]'. Here, it modifies '프러포즈'.
--- word ---
term: 일상에서의
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 일상 (daily life) + -에서 (at/in) + -의 (possessive particle)
meaning: in daily life's
notes: 일상 (ilsang) means 'daily life' or 'everyday life'. -에서 (eseo) indicates location ('in' or 'at'). -의 (ui) is the possessive particle, similar to 'of' or 's in English. The combination -에서의 means 'in the [noun]'s' or 'of the [noun] that is in'.
--- word ---
term: 프러포즈?
type: noun + interrogative
breakdown: 프러포즈 (proposal) + ?
meaning: a proposal?
notes: 프러포즈 (peureopojeu) means 'proposal'. The question mark indicates an interrogative tone.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -다 싶다
meaning: This pattern means 'to feel like' or 'to think that' a certain situation or state is true. It often follows a declarative clause.
example: 그가 올 것 같다 싶었어요. (Geuga ol geot gatda sipeosseoyo.) - I felt like he would come.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -에서의
meaning: This particle combination indicates 'in/at [place] (of)' or 'related to [place]'. It specifies the location or context of the modified noun.
example: 학교에서의 생활 (hakgyoeseoui saenghwal) - life at school
level: Intermediate

=== [21:21] 부모님한테 인사드리는 거? ===
meaning: Meeting the parents?
tone: Informal, questioning, suggesting a scenario.
--- word ---
term: 부모님한테
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 부모님 (parents - honorific) + -한테 (to/from a person)
meaning: to parents
notes: 부모님 (bumonim) is the honorific form of 'parents'. -한테 (hante) is an informal particle meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. The more formal equivalent is -께 (kke).
--- word ---
term: 인사드리는
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 인사드리다 (to greet - honorific) + -는 (present adnominal ending)
meaning: greeting (honorific)
notes: 인사드리다 (insadeurida) is the honorific form of 인사하다 (insahada, to greet). 드리다 (deurida) is the honorific equivalent of 주다 (juda, to give), used when the action is directed towards a superior or elder. -는 (neun) is the present tense adnominal ending, modifying the following noun (implied '거' or '것').
--- word ---
term: 거?
type: noun + interrogative
breakdown: 것 (thing) + -어? (informal interrogative ending, contracted)
meaning: the thing? / is it?
notes: 거 (geo) is a common contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact'. The question mark indicates an interrogative tone. The phrase '인사드리는 거?' implies 'the act of greeting (parents)?' or 'is it about greeting (parents)?'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 거
meaning: This pattern nominalizes a verb, turning it into a noun phrase meaning 'the act of doing something' or 'the thing that is done'. '거' is a contraction of '것'.
example: 책 읽는 거 좋아해요. (Chaek ingneun geo joahaeyo.) - I like reading books.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -한테
meaning: A particle meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. It's used in informal contexts.
example: 친구한테 전화했어요. (Chinguhante jeonhwahaesseoyo.) - I called my friend.
level: Beginner

=== [21:34] 그런 거 있잖아 ===
meaning: You know, things like that.
tone: Informal, conversational, seeking understanding or agreement.

term: 그런

breakdown: 그렇다 (to be so) + -ㄴ (adnominal ending)
meaning: such, like that

--- word ---
term: 거

breakdown: 것 (thing)
meaning: thing
notes: 거 (geo) is the contracted form of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing'.
--- word ---

breakdown: 있다 (to exist) + -잖아 (you know/don't you know)
meaning: there are, you know
notes: 있잖아 (itjana) is formed from 있다 (itda, to exist/to have) and the ending -잖아 (jana). It means 'there is/are, you know' or 'you know what I mean'. It's used to bring up a topic or remind the listener of something familiar, often to seek their understanding or agreement.
--- grammar ---

meaning: Used to state something the speaker assumes the listener already knows or should know, often implying 'you know, right?' or 'don't you know?'. It can also express mild complaint or reminder.

=== [21:42] 난 그게 프러포즈 같아 ===
meaning: I think that's a proposal.
tone: Informal, declarative, expressing an opinion.
--- word ---
term: 난
type: pronoun + topic particle
breakdown: 나 + 는
meaning: I
notes: Contraction of 나 (na, I) + 는 (neun, topic particle). 나 is the informal first-person pronoun. The topic particle 는 indicates 'I' as the topic of the sentence.
--- word ---
term: 그게
type: pronoun + subject particle
breakdown: 그것 + 이
meaning: that (as the subject)
notes: Contraction of 그것 (geugeot, that thing) + 이 (i, subject particle). This is a common colloquial contraction.
--- word ---
term: 프러포즈
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: proposal
notes: A loanword from English 'proposal'. Used commonly in Korean for marriage proposals.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: descriptive verb
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: seems like, looks like
notes: The base form 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like, to resemble, to seem'. -아/어 is an informal, plain speech ending. When attached to a noun (N + 같다), it means 'it seems like N' or 'it is like N'. This is a very common way to express opinion or similarity in casual conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 같다
meaning: Used to express that something 'seems like N' or 'is like N'. It indicates a similarity or an impression.
example: 이거 사과 같아. (Igeo sagwa gata. This looks like an apple.)
level: Beginner

=== [21:44] 아 그러니까 뭐라 그럴까 ===
meaning: Ah, so, what should I say?
tone: Informal, hesitant, rhetorical, thinking aloud.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah, Oh
notes: A common interjection used to express realization, surprise, or as a filler word when thinking.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: conjunction
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)니까
meaning: so, therefore, that's why
notes: Connects sentences, indicating a reason or consequence. In spoken Korean, it's often used as a transitional phrase, similar to 'so' or 'well' in English, especially when pausing to think or elaborate.
--- word ---
term: 뭐라
type: adverbial form of pronoun
breakdown: 무엇 + (이)라고
meaning: what (to say)
notes: Contraction of 무엇이라고 (mueosirago), meaning 'what (to say/call)'. It's commonly used with verbs like 말하다 (malhada, to speak) or 부르다 (bureuda, to call). Here, it's implied with '그럴까' (geureolkka, should I say/do).
--- word ---
term: 그럴까
type: verb ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -ㄹ까?
meaning: should I say/do?
notes: The base form 그렇다 (geureota) means 'to be so, to be like that'. The ending -ㄹ까? (-(eu)lkka?) is used to ask a rhetorical question, express a suggestion, or wonder about something. In this context, it's Hyejin thinking aloud, 'what should I say/do?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -(으)니까
meaning: Used to indicate a reason or cause for the following clause, meaning 'because, so, therefore'. It can also be used as a conversational filler.
example: 비가 오니까 집에 가자. (Biga onikka jibe gaja. Since it's raining, let's go home.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -ㄹ/을까?
meaning: Used to ask a rhetorical question, make a suggestion, or express wonder/doubt about a future action or state. Can be translated as 'Shall I/we...?', 'I wonder if...', or 'Do you think...?'
example: 점심 먹을까? (Jeomsim meogeulkka? Shall we eat lunch?)
level: Beginner

=== [21:49] 상상의 나례를 펼쳐볼까? ===
meaning: Shall we spread our wings of imagination?
tone: Informal, playful, inviting, rhetorical question.
--- word ---
term: 상상의
type: noun + possessive particle
breakdown: 상상 + 의
meaning: of imagination, imaginative
notes: 상상 (sangsang) means 'imagination'. 의 (ui) is the possessive particle, equivalent to 'of' or 's in English. In spoken Korean, 의 is often pronounced as 에 (e).
--- word ---
term: 나례를
type: noun + object particle
breakdown: 나래 + 를
meaning: wings (object)
notes: 나래 (narae) means 'wings'. 를 (reul) is the object particle, indicating that 'wings' is the direct object of the verb '펼쳐볼까?'.
--- word ---
term: 펼쳐볼까?
type: verb + auxiliary verb + ending
breakdown: 펼치다 + -어보다 + -ㄹ까?
meaning: shall we try spreading/unfolding?
notes: The base verb is 펼치다 (pyeolchida, to spread, to unfold). -아/어보다 (a/eoboda) is an auxiliary verb construction meaning 'to try doing something' or 'to do something and see'. Here, it implies 'let's try spreading'. The final -ㄹ까? (-(eu)lkka?) makes it a rhetorical question, inviting the listener to join in the action or thought, 'Shall we...?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: N1 + 의 + N2
meaning: Indicates possession or a relationship between two nouns, similar to 'N2 of N1' or 'N1's N2' in English.
example: 친구의 책 (chingu-ui chaek. My friend's book.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아/어보다
meaning: Means 'to try doing something' or 'to do something and see'. It can also imply experiencing something.
example: 먹어보다 (meogeoboda. To try eating.)
level: Beginner
