=== [21:56] 어 여진이 너희 본가라도 한번 놀러 갈까? ===
meaning: Uh, Yujin, shall I go visit your parents' home just once?
tone: Informal, playful, hypothetical, rhetorical question, role-playing a proposal scenario.
--- word ---
term: 어
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Uh, Oh
notes: A common interjection used as a filler word, to express slight hesitation, or to get someone's attention.
--- word ---
term: 여진이
type: name + informal particle
breakdown: 유진 + 이
meaning: Yujin (vocative)
notes: 유진 (Yujin) is a hypothetical name Hyejin is using in her role-play. The particle 이 (i) is an informal vocative particle, often used when calling out to someone or referring to them casually, especially if they are younger or a close friend. It can also be a subject particle, but here it functions as a casual address.
--- word ---
term: 너희
type: pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: your (plural informal)
notes: The informal plural form of 'you' (너, neo). Here, it's used to refer to 'your' in a casual context, addressing Yujin. It can also mean 'you guys' or 'your (plural)'.
--- word ---
term: 본가라도
type: noun + particle + particle
breakdown: 본가 + 이라도
meaning: even your parents' home, your parents' home at least
notes: 본가 (bongga) means 'parents' home' or 'family home'. -(이)라도 (-(i)rado) is a particle that means 'even N', 'N at least', or 'N if nothing else'. It implies a concession or a minimum option. Here, it suggests 'even if it's just your parents' home'.
--- word ---
term: 한번
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: once, one time
notes: Means 'once' or 'one time'. It can also imply 'just for a moment' or 'for a try'.
--- word ---
term: 놀러
type: verb ending for purpose
breakdown: 놀다 + -러
meaning: to play/visit (for the purpose of)
notes: The base verb 놀다 (nolda) means 'to play, to hang out, to visit'. The ending -러 (-(eu)reo) is attached to a verb stem to indicate the purpose of going or coming somewhere, meaning 'in order to V' or 'to V'. It's always followed by a verb of motion like 가다 (gada, to go) or 오다 (oda, to come).
--- word ---
term: 갈까?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 가다 + -ㄹ까?
meaning: shall I go?
notes: The base verb 가다 (gada) means 'to go'. The ending -ㄹ까? (-(eu)lkka?) is used here to ask a rhetorical question or make a suggestion, 'Shall I go?' in the context of the role-play.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + (이)라도
meaning: Indicates a minimum choice or a concession, meaning 'even N', 'N at least', or 'N if nothing else'.
example: 커피라도 마실까? (Keopirado masilkka? Shall we at least drink coffee?)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -러 가다/오다
meaning: Used to express the purpose of going or coming somewhere, meaning 'to go/come to V' or 'to go/come in order to V'.
example: 밥 먹으러 가자. (Bap meogeureo gaja. Let's go eat.)
level: Beginner

=== [22:00] 너희 어머님 아버님이 너무 보고 싶구나 ===

--- word ---
term: 어머님

breakdown: 
meaning: mother (honorific)

--- word ---
term: 아버님이
type: honorific noun + subject particle
breakdown: 아버지 + 님 + 이
meaning: father (honorific, subject)
notes: 아버지 (abeoji) means 'father'. 님 (nim) is an honorific suffix added to titles or names to show respect. 이 (i) is the subject particle. So, 'your respected father' as the subject.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: very, too much
notes: Means 'too much' or 'very'. While it can sometimes imply excess, it's very commonly used colloquially to simply mean 'very' or 'really' without a negative connotation.
--- word ---
term: 보고
type: verb stem
breakdown: 보다 + -고
meaning: seeing
notes: The verb 보다 (boda) means 'to see'. -고 (go) is a connective ending, often used to link verbs in a sequence or to connect a verb to an auxiliary verb like 싶다 (sipta, to want).

term: 싶구나
type: auxiliary verb + ending
breakdown: 싶다 + -구나
meaning: I want to see (exclamatory)

Here, it emphasizes the speaker's strong desire to see them.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's desire to perform an action, meaning 'to want to V'.
example: 한국에 가고 싶어요. (Hanguge gago sipeoyo. I want to go to Korea.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + -구나 / -는구나
meaning: An informal exclamatory ending used to express realization, surprise, or a strong feeling about something the speaker has just observed or understood. -구나 is for descriptive verbs and past tense, -는구나 for action verbs in present tense.
example: 예쁘구나! (Yeppeuguna! How pretty!)
level: Intermediate

=== [22:03] 이런 식이야 ===
meaning: It's this kind of style/way.
tone: Informal, explanatory, concluding a thought or example.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: demonstrative adjective
breakdown: 이러하다 + -ㄴ
meaning: this kind of, such
notes: Derived from the descriptive verb 이러하다 (ireohada, to be like this). -ㄴ (n) is an adjectival ending that modifies the following noun, meaning 'this kind of' or 'such a'.

type: noun + informal copula
breakdown: 식 + 이다 + -야
meaning: it's a style/way
notes: 식 (sik) means 'style, way, method'. 이다 (ida) is the copula 'to be'. -야 (ya) is the informal, plain speech ending for the copula, used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant (이야) or a vowel (야). Here, it means 'it is this kind of way/style'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + (이)야
meaning: The informal, plain speech form of the copula '이다' (to be). Used to state that something 'is N'.

=== [22:07] 우리 엄마 아빠가 보고 싶다고? ===
meaning: You said you miss my mom and dad?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly surprised/amused, quoting a fan.
--- word ---
term: 우리
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: our / my
notes: '우리' literally means 'our', but it's very commonly used in Korean to mean 'my' when referring to family members, close friends, or things that are shared in a collective sense (e.g., '우리 집' - 'my house', '우리 회사' - 'my company'). It implies a sense of shared belonging or intimacy, even when the speaker is the sole owner or relation.
--- word ---
term: 엄마
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: mom
notes: A common, informal term for 'mother'. The more formal term is '어머니'.
--- word ---
term: 아빠가
type: Noun + Subject particle
breakdown: 아빠 + -가
meaning: dad (subject)
notes: '아빠' is a common, informal term for 'father'. The more formal term is '아버지'. '-가' is a subject particle used when the preceding noun ends in a vowel. It marks the noun as the subject of the sentence.
--- word ---
term: 보고
type: Verb stem
breakdown: 보다 + -고
meaning: seeing / to see
notes: '보다' means 'to see' or 'to look'. The '-고' ending connects verbs in a sequence or forms part of a compound verb. Here, it's used with '싶다' to form '보고 싶다' (to want to see/miss).
--- word ---
term: 싶다고?
type: Verb ending + Indirect quotation particle + Interrogative ending
breakdown: 싶다 + -다고 + -아/어?
meaning: you said you miss?
notes: This is a contraction of '싶다고 하다' (to say that one misses/wants to see) and then shortened with an informal interrogative ending. '-다고 하다' is the indirect quotation marker for descriptive verbs or '있다/없다'. Here, Hyejin is quoting what a fan might have said or implied. The final '?' indicates a questioning tone, often implying slight surprise or confirmation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 싶다
meaning: This pattern expresses a desire or wish to do something. It attaches to the stem of a verb.
example: 밥 먹고 싶어요. (I want to eat rice.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -다고 하다 / -(이)라고 하다
meaning: This is an indirect quotation pattern used to report what someone said. '-다고 하다' is used for action verbs, adjectives, and '있다/없다'. '-(이)라고 하다' is used for nouns.
example: 친구가 예쁘다고 했어요. (My friend said it was pretty.)
level: Intermediate

meaning: mom

term: 아빠를
type: Noun + Object particle
breakdown: 아빠 + -를
meaning: dad (object)
notes: '아빠' is an informal term for 'father'. '-를' is an object particle used when the preceding noun ends in a vowel. It marks the noun as the direct object of the verb.

meaning: seeing / to see

--- word ---
term: 싶다
type: Adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: want to / miss
notes: The base form of 'to want to' or 'to miss' when combined with a verb stem + -고. Here, Hyejin is quoting the phrase '우리 엄마 아빠를 보고 싶다' as a complete thought.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 싶다
meaning: This pattern expresses a desire or wish to do something. It attaches to the stem of a verb.
example: 여행 가고 싶어요. (I want to go on a trip.)

=== [22:11] 아 뭐 이런 말 자체만으로도 너무 뭔가 고맙고 막 그렇잖아 ===
meaning: Ah, just the words themselves, something like that, it's already so touching and all, you know?
tone: Informal, appreciative, slightly emotional, rhetorical, conversational.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: Exclamation
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah / Oh
notes: An interjection expressing realization, surprise, or a gentle sigh. Often used to start a thought or reaction.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: Interrogative pronoun / Filler
breakdown: 
meaning: what / something like
notes: Literally 'what', but often used as a filler word or to mean 'something like' or 'you know' in casual conversation, indicating a generalization or slight vagueness. Here it implies 'something like that' or 'such things'.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: such / this kind of
notes: Derived from '이렇다' (to be like this). It modifies a noun, meaning 'this kind of' or 'such a'.
--- word ---
term: 말
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: words / speech
notes: Refers to spoken words or language.
--- word ---
term: 자체만으로도
type: Noun + Particle + Particle + Particle
breakdown: 자체 + -만 + -(으)로 + -도
meaning: even by the words themselves
notes: '자체' means 'itself' or 'the thing itself'. '-만' means 'only'. '-(으)로' means 'by means of' or 'with'. '-도' means 'even' or 'also'. Combined, it emphasizes that the mere existence of the words is enough to evoke a feeling.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too / very
notes: An adverb meaning 'too much' or 'very'. While traditionally used for negative connotations, in modern colloquial Korean, it's frequently used simply to mean 'very' or 'really' in positive contexts, as it is here.
--- word ---
term: 뭔가
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 무엇 + -ㄴ가
meaning: something
notes: A contraction of '무엇인가' (what is it?). Used to mean 'something' or 'a certain feeling/thing'.
--- word ---
term: 고맙고
type: Adjective stem + Conjunction
breakdown: 고맙다 + -고
meaning: thankful and
notes: '고맙다' means 'to be thankful' or 'to be grateful'. The '-고' ending connects adjectives or verbs, indicating a sequence or listing of qualities/actions.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really / just / wildly
notes: A versatile adverb used to emphasize intensity, spontaneity, or a lack of restraint. It can mean 'randomly', 'just', 'really', or 'wildly'. Here, it adds emphasis to the feeling, suggesting it's overwhelming or just 'all over the place'.
--- word ---
term: 그렇잖아
type: Adjective + Rhetorical ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -잖아
meaning: isn't it? / you know?
notes: A contraction of '그렇지 않아?'. The '-잖아' ending is used to state something that the speaker assumes the listener already knows or agrees with, often seeking confirmation or expressing a shared understanding. It adds a conversational and rhetorical tone. '그렇다' means 'to be so'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -만으로도
meaning: This pattern emphasizes that 'even just by/with' something, a certain result or feeling occurs. It combines '-만' (only), '-(으)로' (by means of), and '-도' (even/also).
example: 그의 미소만으로도 행복해졌어요. (Even just by his smile, I became happy.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 그렇다
meaning: This pattern is used to list a few examples or characteristics, implying 'and so on' or 'and such'. It often appears at the end of a sentence or clause.
example: 피곤하고 졸리고 그래요. (I'm tired and sleepy and such.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아
meaning: This ending is used to state something that the speaker believes the listener already knows or should know, often seeking agreement or confirmation. It adds a rhetorical, conversational tone.
example: 우리 친구잖아. (We're friends, you know.)
level: Beginner

=== [22:43] 제발 면조하지 말래 ===
meaning: They said, 'Please don't interview (me).'
tone: Playful, quoting someone, slightly exasperated or rejecting an idea.
--- word ---
term: 제발
type: adverb
breakdown: 제발
meaning: please
notes: An adverb used to express a strong plea or request, often conveying a sense of urgency or desperation. It can also be used playfully, as here.
--- word ---
term: 면조하지
type: verb + negative particle
breakdown: 면조하다 + -지
meaning: to interview (or similar action) + don't
notes: The word '면조하다' (myeonjohada) is not a standard Korean verb. Given the context of Hyejin discussing proposal ideas and rejecting some as 'disqualifications' (탈락), it is highly probable that '면조' is a playful mispronunciation or a typo for '면접' (myeonjeop), which means 'interview'. A proposal that feels like an 'interview' would certainly be a 'disqualification'. The -지 is part of the negative imperative -지 마 (ji ma), meaning 'don't do'.
--- word ---
term: 말래
type: verb ending
breakdown: 말다 + -래
meaning: to stop/not do + they said
notes: A contraction of 말라고 해 (mallago hae), which is the informal indirect quotation form for imperative sentences (commands or requests). It means 'they said not to do' or 'they told me not to do'. The base verb 말다 (malda) means 'to stop' or 'not to do'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지 마 (-ji ma)
meaning: A common negative imperative construction meaning 'don't do [verb]'. The -지 is attached to the verb stem, and 마 is the shortened informal form of 말다 (malda, to stop/not do).
example: 가지 마. (Gaji ma.) - Don't go.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -래 (-rae)
meaning: An informal contracted form of the indirect quotation ending for imperative sentences (-라고 해). It means 'they said to do/not to do' or 'they told me to do/not to do'. Used when reporting a command or request made by someone else.
example: 엄마가 밥 먹으래. (Eommaga bap meogeurae.) - Mom said to eat.
level: Intermediate

This ending indicates that the preceding action is completed and serves as the cause or prerequisite for the following action. It's a more casual and spoken form of -아서/어서. Here, it means 'after setting up' or 'having set up'.
--- word ---
term: 거기서
type: adverb + particle
breakdown: 거기 + -서
meaning: there (at that place)
notes: 거기 (there) combined with the locative particle -서, which indicates the place where an action occurs. It sets the scene for the next action in the hypothetical proposal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 예를 들어서
meaning: For example, for instance
example: 예를 들어서, 저는 한국 음식을 좋아해요. (For example, I like Korean food.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(아/어) 가지고
meaning: Indicates that the preceding action is completed and serves as the cause or prerequisite for the following action. A colloquial alternative to -(아/어)서.

meaning: Father (honorific)

breakdown: 
meaning: I (humble)

breakdown: 혜진 + -이 + -와

--- grammar ---

term: 언니가
type: Noun + particle
breakdown: 언니 (eonni) + -가 (ga)
meaning: Unnie (subject)

type: Noun + particle + verb ending

meaning: (as) a 'free pass for meeting parents-in-law' type (person)
notes: 프리패스상 (peurippaeseusang) is a compound noun meaning 'a face/appearance that gets a free pass for meeting parents-in-law'. For nouns ending in a vowel, it's -라고 (rago); for nouns ending in a consonant, it's -이라고 (irago).

meaning: do you think?
notes: 생각하다 (saenggakada) means 'to think'. -아/어 (a/eo) is the informal polite ending (hae-form). The question mark indicates an interrogative tone. Informal.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -라고/이라고 생각하다 (rago/irago saenggakada)
meaning: Used to express one's opinion or belief about a noun, meaning 'to think that (someone/something) is (something)'.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: 성견례 프리패스상 (seonggyeollye peurippaeseusang)

level: Intermediate

notes: A colloquial adverb meaning 'very' or 'really'. It's a common informal alternative to '정말' (jeongmal) or '아주' (aju).
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a lot, much
notes: Modifies verbs or adjectives to indicate quantity or degree.
--- word ---
term: 예뻐해
type: verb
breakdown: 예뻐하다 + -아/어
meaning: like, cherish, find pretty
notes: The base verb 예뻐하다 (yeppeohada) means 'to cherish,' 'to find beautiful,' or 'to like/adore.' It's a combination of 예쁘다 (yeppeuda, to be pretty) and 하다 (hada, to do). Here, it's conjugated informally in the present tense.
--- word ---
term: 주시는데
type: verb ending
breakdown: 주다 + -시- + -는 + -데
meaning: give (honorific) and...
notes: 주다 (juda) means 'to give.' When used as an auxiliary verb after -어/아, it means 'to do something for someone.' -시- (si) is an honorific suffix, showing respect to the subject (어르신분들). -는 (neun) is a present tense adnominal ending. -데 (de) is a connective ending used to provide background information or context for the following statement. The full pattern is -어/아 주시다 (do something for someone respectfully).
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun
breakdown: 것
meaning: the thing, the fact
notes: A colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact.' Often used to nominalize clauses.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: adjective
breakdown: 같다 + -아/어
meaning: it seems, it is like
notes: The base adjective 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like' or 'to seem.' Conjugated informally in the present tense. The pattern -는 것 같다 (neun geot gatda) means 'it seems that...' or 'I think that...'. This expresses Hyejin's observation or opinion.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -어/아 주시다
meaning: Used to express that an action is done for the benefit of someone else, with respect to the person performing the action.
example: 선생님이 숙제를 도와주셨어요. (The teacher helped me with my homework.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 것 같다
meaning: Used to express a conjecture or opinion, meaning 'it seems that...' or 'I think that...'.
example: 비가 오는 것 같아요. (It seems like it's raining.)
level: Beginner

=== [23:58] 그리고 나도 너무 좋아하고 뭔가 뭔지 알지? ===
meaning: And I like them a lot too, and you know what I mean, right?
tone: Informal, conversational, seeking agreement. Hyejin is engaging directly with her audience.
--- word ---
term: 그리고
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: and, then
notes: Connects sentences or clauses, indicating continuation or sequence.

term: 나도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + -도
meaning: I also, me too
notes: 나 (na) is the informal 'I/me'. -도 (do) is a particle meaning 'also' or 'too'.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too much, very
notes: Originally meant 'too much' (with a negative connotation), but colloquially it's widely used to simply mean 'very' or 'really' in positive contexts, as it is here.
--- word ---
term: 좋아하고
type: verb ending
breakdown: 좋아하다 + -고
meaning: like and
notes: 좋아하다 (joahada) means 'to like.' -고 (go) is a connective ending that links two or more verbs or adjectives, indicating a sequence of actions or states. It can also mean 'and' or 'and then'.
--- word ---
term: 뭔가
type: pronoun
breakdown: 무엇 + -ㄴ가
meaning: something, somehow
notes: A contraction of 무엇인가 (mueosinga), meaning 'something' or 'what is it.' It can also imply a vague or indefinite 'somehow' or 'for some reason.'
--- word ---
term: 뭔지
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 무엇 + -인지
meaning: what it is
notes: A contraction of 무엇인지 (mueosinji), meaning 'what it is' or 'whether it is what.' It's often used in the pattern '뭔지 알다' (to know what it is).
--- word ---
term: 알지?
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 알다 + -지?
meaning: you know, right?
notes: 알다 (alda) means 'to know.' -지? (ji?) is a sentence-final ending used to confirm information or seek agreement from the listener. It often translates to 'right?' or 'you know?' This is a very common way for Hyejin to engage her fans, as noted in the Story Bible ('You know what I mean?').
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고
meaning: Connects two or more verbs or adjectives, indicating a sequence of actions, a list of facts, or simply 'and'.
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어요. (I ate and then watched a movie.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지?
meaning: Used to confirm information or seek agreement from the listener, often implying that the speaker expects a 'yes' answer. Can also be used rhetorically.
example: 이거 맛있지? (This is delicious, right?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: 뭔지 알지?
meaning: An idiomatic expression meaning 'You know what I mean, right?' or 'You get it, right?'. Used to check if the listener understands the speaker's implied meaning or context.
example: 말 안 해도 뭔지 알지? (You know what I mean even if I don't say it, right?)
level: Intermediate

=== [24:39] 그러면서 먹을 거 진짜 많이 챙겨주시고 ===
meaning: And while doing that, they really take care of me by giving me a lot of food.
tone: Informal, appreciative, descriptive.
--- word ---
term: 그러면서
type: conjunction/adverb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -(으)면서
meaning: while doing so, and at the same time
notes: 그렇다 (geureota, 'to be so/that way') + -(으)면서 (-(eu)myeonseo, 'while doing'). This connects two actions that happen simultaneously or sequentially, often implying a cause-and-effect or descriptive relationship. It can also mean 'and at the same time'.
--- word ---
term: 먹을
type: verb (future modifier)
breakdown: 먹다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: to eat (something to eat)
notes: 먹다 (meokda, 'to eat') + -(으)ㄹ (-(eu)l), which is the future/prospective adnominal ending. It modifies the following noun, meaning 'something to eat' or 'something that will be eaten'.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: dependent noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing
notes: A common colloquial contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'item'. When combined with a preceding verb modifier like '먹을', it forms '먹을 거' (meogeul geo), meaning 'something to eat' or 'food'.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: An adverb used to emphasize the truth or intensity of a statement. Can also be used as an exclamation 'Really?!'.
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a lot, much
notes: The adverbial form of 많다 (manta, 'to be many/much'). It modifies verbs or adjectives to indicate quantity or degree.
--- word ---
term: 챙겨주시고
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 챙기다 + -어 주다 + -고
meaning: take care of (me) and
notes: 챙기다 (chaenggida) means 'to take care of,' 'to pack,' or 'to prepare.' -아/어 주다 (-a/eo juda) is a common auxiliary verb pattern meaning 'to do something for someone' (benefactive). -고 (-go) is a conjunctive ending meaning 'and' or 'and then', connecting clauses.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)면서
meaning: Used to indicate that two actions occur simultaneously, meaning 'while doing V' or 'at the same time as V'. It can also connect two related actions or states.
example: 밥을 먹으면서 TV를 봐요. (I watch TV while eating.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)ㄹ 것
meaning: Attaches to a verb stem to create a noun phrase meaning 'the thing to V' or 'something that will V'. It functions as a future or prospective noun modifier.
example: 할 일 (things to do)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아/어 주다
meaning: This benefactive auxiliary verb means 'to do something for someone' or 'to do someone a favor'. It implies that the action benefits another person.
example: 도와주세요. (Please help me.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -고
meaning: A conjunctive ending that connects two or more clauses, meaning 'and' or 'and then'. It indicates a sequential or additive relationship between actions or states.
example: 밥을 먹고 커피를 마셨어요. (I ate and then drank coffee.)
level: Beginner

=== [24:45] 화삼 히트 이러면서 이렇게 엄청 많이 챙겨주셔 ===
meaning: Saying 'Hwasam hit!' like this, they take care of me so much.
tone: Informal, illustrative, appreciative.
--- word ---
term: 화삼
type: proper noun
breakdown: 
meaning: Hwasam
notes: See segment 1 for notes on '화삼'.
--- word ---
term: 히트
type: noun/exclamation
breakdown: 
meaning: hit, success
notes: A loanword from English 'hit,' often used to mean a success, a popular item, or a great achievement. Here, it's used as an exclamation of approval or praise.
--- word ---
term: 이러면서
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 이렇다 + -(으)면서
meaning: while saying this, like this
notes: 이렇다 (ireota, 'to be like this') + -(으)면서 (-(eu)myeonseo, 'while doing'). Similar to '그러면서' (geureomyeonseo) but referring to 'this' action or statement. It often implies 'while saying/doing this' or 'like this'.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: like this, in this way
notes: An adverb meaning 'like this' or 'in this manner,' often used to describe how an action is performed or to refer back to a previously mentioned way of doing something.
--- word ---
term: 엄청
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: very, extremely, tremendously (colloquial)
notes: A colloquial adverb meaning 'very,' 'extremely,' or 'tremendously.' It's stronger and more informal than '아주' (aju) or '매우' (maeu).
--- word ---
term: 많이
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a lot, much
notes: See segment 3 for notes on '많이'.
--- word ---
term: 챙겨주셔
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 챙기다 + -어 주다 + -어
meaning: they take care of (me)
notes: 챙기다 (chaenggida, 'to take care of') + -아/어 주다 (-a/eo juda, 'to do for someone') + -어 (-eo), which is the informal polite ending (contracted from -어요). This form is commonly used in casual conversation, especially when speaking to an audience or close relations.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)면서
meaning: Used to indicate that two actions occur simultaneously, meaning 'while doing V' or 'at the same time as V'. It can also connect two related actions or states.
example: 밥을 먹으면서 TV를 봐요. (I watch TV while eating.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아/어 주다
meaning: This benefactive auxiliary verb means 'to do something for someone' or 'to do someone a favor'. It implies that the action benefits another person.
example: 도와주세요. (Please help me.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -아/어 (요)
meaning: The informal polite declarative sentence ending. It is a versatile ending used for statements, questions, and commands in polite, everyday speech. The '요' is often dropped in very casual or intimate contexts, as seen here.
example: 밥 먹어요. (I eat/Let's eat.)
level: Beginner

=== [24:50] 근데 너무 감사한 거야 ===
meaning: But I'm so grateful for it.
tone: Informal, reflective, appreciative.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: conjunction (contraction)
breakdown: 그런데
meaning: but, however, by the way
notes: A common colloquial contraction of 그런데 (geureonde). It functions as a conjunction meaning 'but,' 'however,' or 'and by the way,' often used to introduce a new topic or a contrasting idea.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: Originally meaning 'too much' (with a negative connotation), '너무' (neomu) is now widely used colloquially to simply mean 'very' or 'so' in both positive and negative contexts, especially in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 감사한
type: adjective (modifier)
breakdown: 감사하다 + -(으)ㄴ
meaning: grateful (that is)
notes: 감사하다 (gamsahada, 'to be grateful') + -(으)ㄴ (-(eu)n), the adnominal ending for adjectives. It modifies a noun, but here it's used with '거야' to explain a state or feeling.
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: dependent noun + verb ending (colloquial)
breakdown: 것 + -이야
meaning: it is that (thing), the fact is
notes: A colloquial explanatory ending, formed by 것 (geot, 'thing') + -이야 (-iya, informal declarative ending). It's used to explain a situation, provide background information, or emphasize a fact. Often translated as 'it is that...' or 'the thing is...'. The '것' is often contracted to '거' in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A + -(으)ㄴ/는 거야
meaning: This is a colloquial explanatory ending used to explain a situation, provide a reason, or emphasize a fact. It literally means 'it is the fact that A is so'. For adjectives (A), -(으)ㄴ is used.
example: 피곤한 거야. (It's because I'm tired.)

=== [24:53] 그래도 프리패스인지는 모르겠지만 ===
meaning: Still, I don't know if it's a free pass, but...

--- word ---
term: 그래도
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: still, nevertheless
notes: Used to introduce a contrasting or continuing thought. It's a common conversational filler.
--- word ---
term: 프리패스인지는
type: noun + particle + verb ending
breakdown: 프리패스 (free pass) + -이 (to be) + -ㄴ지 (whether) + -는 (topic particle)
meaning: whether it's a free pass
notes: 프리패스 (free pass) is a Konglish term, meaning something that grants automatic approval or passage. The pattern -인지/-ㄴ지 is used to express uncertainty or to ask 'whether or not' something is the case. The topic particle -는 emphasizes 'the free pass' as the subject of the speaker's uncertainty.
--- word ---
term: 모르겠지만

breakdown: 모르다 (to not know) + -지만 (but, although)
meaning: I don't know, but
notes: 모르다 is 'to not know'. The ending -지만 connects two clauses, indicating a contrast or concession. It's a very common way to transition thoughts in conversation.
--- grammar ---

meaning: To know/not know whether... / To know/not know if...
example: 이게 맞는지 모르겠어요. (I don't know if this is correct.)

=== [25:09] 좋아해 주시지 않을까 싶네 ===
meaning: I wonder if they'll like me.
tone: Informal, hopeful, speculative, continuing the previous thought about meeting parents-in-law.
--- word ---
term: 좋아해
type: verb
breakdown: 좋아하다 (to like)
meaning: like
notes: 좋아하다 is the verb 'to like'. It's conjugated here in the plain form, which is common in speculative or rhetorical questions in informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 주시지
type: verb + honorific suffix + ending
breakdown: 주다 (to give) + -시- (honorific suffix) + -지 (rhetorical ending)
meaning: give (honorific) / do for (honorific), don't you think?
notes: 주다 means 'to give'. The honorific suffix -시- is used to show respect to the subject of the action (here, the implied parents-in-law). The ending -지 is often used to ask a rhetorical question, express a mild suggestion, or seek agreement, similar to 'isn't it?' or 'don't you think?'. In this context, it adds a touch of hopeful speculation.
--- word ---
term: 않을까
type: auxiliary verb + ending
breakdown: 않다 (to not do) + -을까 (I wonder if...)
meaning: won't they? / I wonder if they will
notes: This is a common pattern for expressing speculation or a rhetorical question. -지 않을까 싶다 (I wonder if... / I think they might...) is a very natural way to express a soft opinion or prediction. The full form would be 좋아해 주시지 않을까 (I wonder if they won't like me / I wonder if they will like me).
--- word ---
term: 싶네
type: auxiliary verb + ending
breakdown: 싶다 (to want to / to think / to feel) + -네 (realization/exclamatory ending)
meaning: I think / I feel
notes: 싶다 is an auxiliary verb meaning 'to want to' or 'to think/feel'. The ending -네 expresses a mild realization or exclamation, often used when the speaker is stating a personal thought or observation. It adds a casual and reflective tone.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ까 싶다
meaning: To wonder if... / To think that (it might be)...
example: 비가 올까 싶어요. (I wonder if it will rain.)

=== [25:35] 어쩔 수 없어 ===
meaning: There's nothing I can do. / It can't be helped.
tone: Informal, resigned, slightly regretful, often used when an unavoidable situation arises.

meaning: what to do
notes: A contraction of 어찌할 (어찌하다 + -ㄹ), meaning 'what to do'. It's always used in the idiom 어쩔 수 없다.
--- word ---
term: 수
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: way / method / possibility
notes: A dependent noun meaning 'way' or 'method'. It always follows a verb ending in -(으)ㄹ and is typically used with 있다/없다.
--- word ---

breakdown: 없다 (to not exist / to not have)
meaning: there isn't / I don't have
notes: The informal form of 없다. When combined with 어쩔 수, it forms the common idiom 어쩔 수 없다, meaning 'there's nothing that can be done' or 'it can't be helped'. This is a very common colloquial expression.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 수 없다
meaning: Cannot do / There is no way to do. Expresses impossibility or inability.

--- grammar ---

meaning: It can't be helped / There's nothing to be done. An idiomatic expression for resignation.
example: 늦어서 어쩔 수 없었어. (I was late, so it couldn't be helped.)
level: Beginner

=== [25:49] 그러니까 어쩔 수가 없는데 ===
meaning: So, there's nothing that can be done, but...
tone: Informal, transitional, slightly resigned, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: adverb
breakdown: 그러니까
meaning: So, therefore
notes: This adverb connects the current statement to a previous one, indicating a logical consequence or continuation. It's an informal way to say 'so' or 'therefore'. The more formal equivalent is 그러므로.
--- word ---
term: 어쩔
type: verb
breakdown: 어쩌다 + -ㄹ
meaning: what to do
notes: This is the prospective/future adnominal form of 어쩌다 (to do what, to be how). It's most commonly seen in the fixed expression 어쩔 수 없다 (there's nothing that can be done, cannot help it), where 수 (way, method, possibility) is a dependent noun.
--- word ---
term: 수가
type: dependent noun + particle
breakdown: 수 + -가
meaning: a way/possibility (as subject)
notes: 수 is a dependent noun meaning 'way,' 'method,' or 'possibility.' It always follows a verb in the -ㄹ/을 form. Here, -가 is the subject particle. The phrase -ㄹ/을 수 없다 means 'cannot' or 'there is no way to do something.'
--- word ---
term: 없는데
type: adjective + ending
breakdown: 없다 + -는데
meaning: there isn't, but
notes: This is the adjective 없다 (to not exist, to not have) combined with the connective ending -는데. This ending serves multiple functions: it can provide background information, introduce a contrast, or express a gentle suggestion or hesitation, often trailing off to imply an unstated continuation. It's informal here.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄹ/을 수 있다/없다
meaning: This pattern expresses ability or possibility ('can/cannot') or the existence/non-existence of a way to do something ('there is a way to/there is no way to').
example: 한국말 할 수 있어요. (I can speak Korean.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데/은데/는데
meaning: This versatile connective ending provides background information, introduces a contrast, or expresses a mild suggestion, question, or hesitation. It often implies 'but' or 'and so'.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Intermediate

level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄴ다면/는다면
meaning: This conditional ending is used to express a hypothetical situation or a supposition, meaning 'if (it were to happen)' or 'supposing that'. -ㄴ다면 is used after verb stems ending in a vowel or 'ㄹ', -는다면 after verb stems ending in a consonant.

=== [26:18] 그건 뭐 잠만 ===
meaning: That's, well, just a moment.
tone: Informal, conversational, pausing, slightly contemplative.

term: 그건
type: pronoun + particle

meaning: That (as topic)
notes: This is a contraction of 그것은. 그것 means 'that thing', and -은 is the topic particle, indicating 'that' as the topic of discussion. Contractions like 이건 (이것은) and 저건 (저것은) are very common in spoken Korean.

breakdown: 뭐
meaning: well, something
notes: 뭐 literally means 'what', but it's frequently used as a filler word in conversation, similar to 'well' or 'um' in English, or to express slight uncertainty or a dismissive tone.
--- word ---
term: 잠만
type: adverb + particle
breakdown: 잠깐 + -만
meaning: just a moment
notes: This is a common contraction of 잠깐만, which means 'just a moment' or 'wait a second'. 잠깐 means 'a moment', and -만 means 'only' or 'just'.

=== [26:19] 청희들이 나한테 프러포즈를 한다면 ===

breakdown: 청희 + -들 + -이

breakdown: 나 + -한테
meaning: to me
notes: 나 is the informal first-person pronoun 'I' or 'me'. -한테 is an informal particle meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. The formal equivalent is -께 (for 'to') or -에게 (for 'to' or 'from').

breakdown: 프러포즈 + -를

meaning: if they propose
notes: Same as segment 2. 하다 (to do) with the hypothetical conditional ending -ㄴ다면. Here, it sets up the condition for Hyejin's subsequent reaction.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -한테
meaning: This particle indicates the recipient of an action or the source of something, meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. It is used in informal contexts.
example: 친구한테 전화했어요. (I called my friend.)
level: Beginner

=== [26:26] 그 말이지 ===
meaning: That's what you mean.
tone: Informal, confirming, understanding.
--- word ---
term: 그

breakdown: 그
meaning: that
notes: 그 is a determiner meaning 'that' or 'the'. It refers to something previously mentioned or known to both the speaker and listener.
--- word ---

breakdown: 말 + -이지
meaning: (you mean) that word/thing
notes: 말 means 'word' or 'speech'. -이지 is an informal declarative/question ending. When used declaratively, it confirms understanding or states something obvious, often implying 'you know what I mean, right?' or 'that's what it is.' When used as a question, it seeks confirmation.

=== [26:29] 그러면은 이제 어디서 또 이런 걸 사왔어 응? ===
meaning: Then, now, where did you buy something like this again, huh?
tone: Informal, playful, rhetorical question, slightly teasing.
--- word ---
term: 그러면은
type: conjunction + particle
breakdown: 그러면 + -은
meaning: Then, in that case
notes: This is a contraction of 그러면 + -은. 그러면 means 'then' or 'in that case', connecting to the previous statement. Adding the topic particle -은 emphasizes 'that case' as the new topic.
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: adverb
breakdown: 이제
meaning: now, from now on
notes: 이제 means 'now' or 'from now on', often implying a change in situation or a new point in time. It can be contrasted with 지금 (jigeum), which simply means 'right now' or 'at this moment'.
--- word ---
term: 어디서
type: adverb + particle
breakdown: 어디 + -서
meaning: where (from/at)
notes: 어디 means 'where'. -서 is a particle indicating the location where an action takes place or the starting point/origin ('from').
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 또
meaning: again, also
notes: 또 means 'again' or 'also'. Here, it implies a repeated action or a similar occurrence.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: such a, this kind of
notes: This is the adnominal form of the adjective 이렇다 (to be like this). It modifies the following noun, meaning 'this kind of' or 'such a'.
--- word ---
term: 걸
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 것 + -을
meaning: thing (as object)
notes: This is a contraction of 것을. 것 is a dependent noun meaning 'thing', and -을 is the object particle.
--- word ---
term: 사왔어
type: verb
breakdown: 사오다 + -았어
meaning: bought and brought
notes: This is the informal past tense of 사오다, a compound verb formed from 사다 (to buy) and 오다 (to come). It means 'to buy and bring (back)'. The -았어 ending indicates past tense and informal speech.
--- word ---
term: 응?
type: interjection
breakdown: 응 + ?
meaning: huh?
notes: 응 is an informal way to say 'yes' or to seek confirmation. Here, with a question mark, it's a playful, rhetorical 'huh?' or 'right?' often used to tease or prompt a response.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 오다
meaning: This pattern indicates that an action is performed and then the subject comes (back) or brings something. It implies movement towards the speaker or a return.
example: 책을 사왔어요. (I bought a book and brought it back.)
level: Intermediate

=== [26:38] 어디서 또 이런 걸 이렇게 해가지고 ===

tone: Informal, playful, rhetorical, trailing off, implying a continuation.
--- word ---
term: 어디서
type: adverb + particle
breakdown: 어디 + -서
meaning: where (from/at)
notes: Same as segment 6. 어디 (where) + -서 (at/from).
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 또
meaning: again, also
notes: Same as segment 6. Indicates repetition.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 + -ㄴ
meaning: such a, this kind of
notes: Same as segment 6. Adnominal form of 이렇다 (to be like this).
--- word ---
term: 걸
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 것 + -을
meaning: thing (as object)
notes: Same as segment 6. Contraction of 것을.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this, in this way
notes: This is the adverbial form of 이렇다 (to be like this). It modifies a verb, meaning 'in this manner' or 'like this'.
--- word ---
term: 해가지고
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어 가지고
meaning: did (something) and so/then
notes: This is the verb 하다 (to do) combined with the connective ending -아/어 가지고. This ending indicates a sequence of actions, where the first action is completed before the second, or it can imply a cause/reason for the following action. It's an informal and colloquial expression, often used in spoken Korean. The sentence trails off, implying Hyejin is about to describe the consequence or a further action.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 가지고
meaning: This colloquial connective ending indicates a sequence of actions (do X, then do Y) or a cause/reason (because X, Y happened). It is commonly used in spoken Korean and is more informal than -고 or -아서/어서.
example: 밥을 먹어 가지고 배가 불러요. (I ate, so I'm full.)
level: Intermediate

=== [26:50] 고마워 하지 뭔지 알지 ===
meaning: Thank you, right? You know what I mean, right?
tone: Informal, conversational, rhetorical, seeking confirmation.
--- word ---
term: 고마워
type: verb
breakdown: 고맙다 + -어
meaning: thank you (informal)
notes: This is the informal, casual form of '고맙습니다' (gomapseumnida) or '고마워요' (gomawoyo). Used with close friends or in casual settings like an idol-fan livestream.
--- word ---
term: 하지
type: verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -지
meaning: you do, right? / it is, right?
notes: The ending '-지' (ji) is attached to a verb stem to ask a rhetorical question or seek confirmation. It implies that the speaker expects agreement. It can also be used to express the speaker's assumption or mild suggestion. This is a casual form.
--- word ---
term: 뭔지
type: pronoun + noun clause ending
breakdown: 뭐 + -ㄴ지
meaning: what it is
notes: Combined form of '뭐' (mwo - what) and the noun clause ending '-ㄴ지' (n-ji). '-ㄴ지/는지' (n-ji/neun-ji) is used to turn a question into a noun clause, often followed by verbs like '알다' (alda - to know) or '모르다' (moreuda - to not know). For example, '이게 뭔지 알아요?' (Ige mwonji arayo?) means 'Do you know what this is?'
--- word ---
term: 알지
type: verb ending
breakdown: 알다 + -지
meaning: you know, right?
notes: From '알다' (alda - to know) + '-지' (ji). This is a common colloquial expression, often used to check if the listener understands or agrees, as noted in the STORY BIBLE for '뭔지 알지?' (meonji alji? - You know what I mean?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지
meaning: Used to ask a rhetorical question, seek confirmation, or express an assumption. It implies the speaker expects agreement.
example: 예쁘지? (Yeppeuji?) - It's pretty, isn't it?
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-(으)ㄴ/는지
meaning: Used to form a noun clause from a question, often with verbs like '알다' (to know) or '모르다' (to not know).
example: 어디에 가는지 알아요? (Eodie ganeunji arayo?) - Do you know where they are going?
level: Intermediate

=== [26:53] 원래 애정이 클수록 혼내는 법이야 ===
meaning: Originally, the greater the affection, the more you scold.
tone: Informal, explanatory, slightly playful and teasing, justifying actions.
--- word ---
term: 원래
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: originally, by nature, usually
notes: Used to indicate something is naturally or inherently true, or how things typically are. It can also mean 'originally' in the sense of 'at first'.
--- word ---
term: 애정이
type: noun + subject particle
breakdown: 애정 + -이
meaning: affection (subject)
notes: '애정' (aejeong) means 'affection' or 'love'. '-이' (i) is the subject particle, attached to nouns ending in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 클수록
type: verb ending
breakdown: 크다 + -ㄹ수록
meaning: the bigger/greater it is
notes: From '크다' (keuda - to be big/great). The '-ㄹ수록' (l-surok) pattern means 'the more... the more...' or 'as... increases'. It indicates a proportional relationship between two actions or states. For example, '많이 먹을수록 배가 불러요' (Mani meogeulsurok baega bulleoyo) - 'The more you eat, the fuller your stomach gets'.
--- word ---
term: 혼내는
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 혼내다 + -는
meaning: scolding (present participle)
notes: From '혼내다' (honnaeda - to scold, to reprimand). '-는' (neun) is the present tense adnominal ending, used to modify a following noun (here, '법' - beop).
--- word ---
term: 법이야
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 법 + -이야
meaning: it's the way/rule/nature
notes: '법' (beop) means 'law, rule, method, way'. When combined with the informal copula '-이야' (iya), '-는 법이다' (neun beopida) means 'it is natural that...', 'it is the way things are', or 'it is a rule/principle'. It's a common way to express a general truth or a natural consequence. The speaker is implying that scolding is a natural expression of deep affection.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-ㄹ수록
meaning: The more (verb/adjective), the more (result). Indicates a proportional relationship.
example: 공부할수록 어려워요. (Gongbuhalsurok eoryeowoyo.) - The more I study, the harder it gets.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 법이다
meaning: It is natural that...; It is the way things are; It is a rule/principle. Expresses a general truth or natural consequence.
example: 사람은 누구나 실수하는 법이다. (Sarameun nuguna silsuhaneun beopida.) - It's natural for everyone to make mistakes.
level: Intermediate

=== [27:08] 나랑 같은 생일이야 생일 축하한다 ===
meaning: It's the same birthday as mine. Happy birthday!
tone: Informal, friendly, celebratory, excited.
--- word ---
term: 나랑
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + -랑
meaning: with me
notes: '나' (na) means 'I' or 'me' (informal). '-랑' (rang) is an informal particle meaning 'with' or 'and', used interchangeably with '하고' (hago) in casual speech. The more formal equivalent is '와/과' (wa/gwa).
--- word ---
term: 같은
type: adjective
breakdown: 같다 + -은
meaning: same, like
notes: From the descriptive verb '같다' (gatda - to be the same, to be like). '-은' (eun) is the adnominal ending for descriptive verbs (adjectives) in the present tense, used to modify a following noun ('생일' - saengil).
--- word ---
term: 생일이야
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 생일 + -이야
meaning: it's birthday
notes: '생일' (saengil) means 'birthday'. '-이야' (iya) is the informal form of the copula '이다' (ida - to be), used when the preceding noun ends in a consonant. If the noun ended in a vowel, it would be '-야' (ya).
--- word ---
term: 생일
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: birthday
notes: A common noun meaning 'birthday'. Often followed by '축하하다' (chukahada - to congratulate) to form '생일 축하하다' (saengil chukahada - to celebrate/congratulate a birthday).
--- word ---
term: 축하한다
type: verb
breakdown: 축하하다 + -ㄴ다
meaning: congratulate (informal declarative)
notes: From '축하하다' (chukahada - to congratulate). '-ㄴ다' (nda) is the informal declarative ending for verbs ending in a vowel (or '-는다' (neunda) for verbs ending in a consonant). It's a casual way to state a fact or make a declaration, often used among close friends or in a monologue. The full phrase '생일 축하한다' is a casual 'Happy Birthday'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N(이)랑
meaning: With N; N and (informal). Used to indicate accompaniment or conjunction.
example: 친구랑 영화 봤어. (Chingurang yeonghwa bwasseo.) - I watched a movie with my friend.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: N이다
meaning: To be N. The copula connecting a noun to a predicate.
example: 저는 학생입니다. (Jeoneun haksaengimnida.) - I am a student.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄴ/는다
meaning: Informal declarative ending. Used to state a fact or make a declaration in casual speech.
example: 밥 먹는다. (Bap meongneunda.) - I'm eating.
level: Beginner

--- word ---
term: 뭐

breakdown: 
meaning: what/anything
notes: An informal interrogative pronoun meaning 'what.' In this context, it functions as 'anything' or 'something'.

meaning: fun/interesting

--- word ---

notes: An adverb meaning 'these days' or 'recently.' It refers to the current period.

breakdown: 문물 + 과
meaning: with trends/culture

--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, too much
notes: 너무 (neomu) means 'too' or 'too much.' While it can sometimes have a negative connotation (e.g., 'too difficult'), it's often used colloquially to simply mean 'very' or 'really' without a strong negative sense, especially in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 벌어진
type: verb (past participle)
breakdown: 벌어지다 + -ㄴ
meaning: that has widened/separated

meaning: thing
notes: 거 (geo) is a common contraction of 것 (geot), meaning 'thing' or 'fact.' It's widely used in casual spoken Korean.

term: 같아
type: verb ending
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: it seems/I think
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like,' 'to seem,' or 'to appear.' The -아 ending is an informal, casual speech ending. When attached to a verb or adjective stem (or a noun + 이다), it expresses a personal opinion or observation, often translated as 'I think' or 'it seems.'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ 것 같다
meaning: It seems/appears that (past tense verb/adjective)
example: 비가 온 것 같아요. (It seems like it rained.)

=== [28:01] 이번에 공항에서였나? ===

=== [28:13] 나는 원래 야호란 단어를 정말 좋아하거든 ===

--- word ---
term: 나는
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + 는
meaning: I (as for me)
notes: 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I.' 는 (neun) is the topic particle, highlighting 'I' as the subject of the statement, often implying 'as for me' or 'speaking of me.'
--- word ---
term: 원래
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: originally, normally, usually
notes: 원래 (wollae) means 'originally,' 'normally,' or 'by nature.' It indicates something that has always been the case or is typical.
--- word ---
term: 야호란
type: noun + particle (contraction)
breakdown: 야호 + (이)라는
meaning: the word called 'yaho'
notes: This is a contraction of 야호(이)라는 (yaho(i)raneun). (이)라는 is a grammatical pattern meaning 'called' or 'named,' used to introduce or define a term. The 이 is added if the preceding noun ends in a consonant. Here, 야호 ends in a vowel, so 라는 is used, then contracted to 란 in casual speech.

term: 단어를
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 단어 + 를
meaning: the word
notes: 단어 (daneo) means 'word.' 를 (reul) is the object particle, marking '단어' as the direct object of the verb '좋아하거든' (like).
--- word ---
term: 정말
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: 정말 (jeongmal) means 'really' or 'truly.' It's used to emphasize the degree of an adjective or verb.
--- word ---
term: 좋아하거든
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 좋아하다 + -거든
meaning: I like (because/you see)
notes: 좋아하다 (joahada) means 'to like.' -거든 (geodeun) is a conversational ending used to provide background information or a reason for a previous statement or action. It often implies 'you see,' 'because,' or 'the thing is...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(이)라는
meaning: The noun called/named...
example: 서울이라는 도시 (the city called Seoul)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -거든
meaning: Provides background information or a reason; 'you see,' 'because'
example: 피곤하거든. (I'm tired, you see.)
level: Intermediate

=== [28:17] 이러면서 하는데 ===
meaning: I do it like this...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 이러면서
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 이러다 + -(으)면서
meaning: while doing this/like this
notes: 이러다 (ireoda) means 'to do like this' or 'to be like this.' -(으)면서 (eumyeonseo) is a grammatical ending meaning 'while doing (something)' or 'at the same time as (something).' It indicates simultaneous actions or states.
--- word ---
term: 하는데
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 하다 + -는데
meaning: I do it (and.../but.../so...)

--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)면서
meaning: While doing (something); at the same time as
example: 책을 읽으면서 음악을 들어요. (I listen to music while reading a book.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데
meaning: Provides background, context, or a mild contrast; often implies a continuation of thought.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)
level: Beginner

--- word ---

type: noun + particle
breakdown: 사람들 + 이
meaning: people
notes: 사람들 (saramdeul) is the plural form of 사람 (saram, person). 이 (i) is the subject particle, indicating that 'people' is the subject of the sentence.

type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: yaho
notes: An exclamation of joy or a yodel-like sound. The Story Bible notes this is a key, evolving expression that becomes an interactive game with fans.
--- word ---
term: 하는
type: verb + adnominal ending
breakdown: 하다 + 는
meaning: doing, saying
notes: 하다 (hada, to do/say) is a common verb. -는 (-neun) is the present tense adnominal ending, which modifies the following noun (in this case, the implied 'thing' or 'fact' in 거야).
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: dependent noun + verb ending
breakdown: 것 + 이다 + 아/야
meaning: it is that, the thing is
notes: 것 (geot, thing/fact) is a dependent noun. 이다 (ida, to be) is the copula. -아/야 (-a/ya) is an informal, casual ending. -는 거야 (-neun geoya) is a common informal pattern used to explain something, state a fact, or express realization. It often conveys a sense of 'it's that...' or 'the thing is...'. The full form would be -는 것이다.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -부터
meaning: Indicates the starting point of an action or state, meaning 'from' or 'since'.
example: 어제부터 비가 와요. (It's been raining since yesterday.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 거야
meaning: An informal expression used to explain a situation, state a fact, or express a realization. It often implies 'it is that...' or 'the thing is...'.
example: 지금 가는 거야. (I'm going now.)
level: Intermediate

term: 근데
type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: but, however
notes: A common informal conjunction, a contraction of 그런데 (geureonde). Used to introduce a contrasting idea or to transition to a new topic. It can also set the scene for what follows.
--- word ---
term: 이거를
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이거 + 를
meaning: this (thing)

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: definitely, surely, without fail

breakdown: 하다 + -아야
meaning: must do, have to do
notes: 하다 (hada, to do) conjugated with -아야 (-aya), which is part of the -아야 하다/되다 grammar pattern meaning 'must' or 'have to'.

breakdown: 되다 + -는데
meaning: have to (do it), but...
notes: 되다 (doeda, to become, to be allowed) is used here in the -아야 되다 construction, meaning 'must' or 'have to'. -는데 (-neunde) is a versatile connective ending that can indicate background information, a mild contrast ('but'), or an expectation, often trailing off to imply more to be said or a slight hesitation. Here, it suggests 'I have to do it, but (there's something more to consider/explain)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 근데
meaning: An informal conjunction meaning 'but' or 'however', used to introduce a contrasting idea or a new point.
example: 근데 왜 안 왔어? (But why didn't you come?)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어야 하다/되다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, meaning 'must' or 'have to'. 하다 and 되다 are often interchangeable in this pattern, with 되다 sometimes implying a slightly stronger sense of 'it needs to be done'.
example: 숙제를 해야 해요. (I have to do my homework.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데
meaning: A connective ending that provides background information, introduces a contrast ('but'), or expresses an expectation. It can also be used to trail off, implying more to be said.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.)

=== [29:33] 뭔데? ===
meaning: What is it?
tone: Curious, informal, questioning.
--- word ---
term: 뭔데?
type: interrogative pronoun + verb ending
breakdown: 뭐 + -ㄴ데
meaning: What is it? What's that?
notes: This is a contraction of 무엇인데 (mu-eot-in-de). 뭐 is a casual contraction of 무엇 (what). The -ㄴ데 ending (or -은데/-는데) is a versatile connective ending that can provide background, express mild surprise, or, as here, ask for more information or clarification in an informal tone. It implies a request for explanation or context. Informal register.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ데/는데
meaning: A versatile connective ending that provides background information, expresses mild surprise, or sets up a question. When used with interrogatives like 뭐 (what), it forms a direct, informal question asking for clarification or explanation.
example: 이거 뭔데? (What is this?)
level: Intermediate

=== [29:43] 뭘까? ===
meaning: What could it be?
tone: Informal, wondering, curious.

term: 뭘까?
type: pronoun + particle + ending
breakdown: 무엇 + -을 + -까?
meaning: what could it be? / I wonder what it is?
notes: Contraction of '무엇을 할까?' (mu-eoseul halkka?). '무엇' (mu-eot) means 'what', '-을' (eul) is the object particle. '-ㄹ까?' (lkka?) is a versatile ending that can express wonder, speculation, or a suggestion. Here, it conveys Hyejin's curiosity about the correct way to do the 'yaho' trend.

=== [29:44] 궁금해졌어 ===
meaning: I've become curious.
tone: Informal, declarative, expressing a change of state.
--- word ---
term: 궁금해졌어
type: verb
breakdown: 궁금하다 + -아/어지다 + -었어
meaning: became curious
notes: '궁금하다' (gunggeumhada) means 'to be curious'. The '-아/어지다' (a/eojida) ending indicates a change of state, meaning 'to become' or 'to get'. The '-었어' (eosseo) is the informal past tense ending. So, '궁금해졌어' means 'I became curious' or 'I've grown curious'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adj + -아/어지다
meaning: To become [adjective]; indicates a change of state or condition.
example: 날씨가 추워졌어요. (The weather became cold.)
level: Intermediate

=== [30:04] 이거야? ===
meaning: Is it this?
tone: Questioning, seeking confirmation, informal.
--- word ---
term: 이거
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 이 (this) + 것 (thing)
meaning: this thing
notes: 이거 is a contraction of 이것 (this thing). It's a casual, informal pronoun used to refer to something close to the speaker or something being discussed. It's commonly used in everyday conversation.
--- word ---
term: 야?
type: Verb ending + question particle
breakdown: -이다 (to be) + -야 (informal interrogative ending)
meaning: is it?
notes: -야 is an informal interrogative ending used with nouns or pronouns followed by the copula -이다 (to be). It's used in casual speech (반말, banmal) to ask a question. The full, more formal form would be -이에요? or -입니까?.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + -이야? / -야?
meaning: Used to form an informal question asking 'Is it N?' or 'Are you N?'. -이야? is used after a noun ending in a consonant, and -야? after a noun ending in a vowel.
example: 학생이야? (Are you a student?) / 의사야? (Are you a doctor?)
level: Beginner

=== [30:08] 야호를 갖고 노네? ===
meaning: They're playing around with 'yaho'?
tone: Informal, questioning, slightly amused. Hyejin observes her fans' creative interaction with the 'yaho' trend.
--- word ---
term: 야호를
type: Noun + object particle
breakdown: 야호 + 를
meaning: 'yaho' (as an object)
notes: 야호 (yaho) is an exclamation of joy, similar to 'hooray' or a yodel. In this context, it's being treated as a concept or a thing that fans are interacting with. 를 (-reul) is the object particle, indicating that 야호 is the direct object of the verb. This is casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 갖고
type: Verb + connective ending
breakdown: 가지다 (to have/hold) + -고 (and/then, while)
meaning: having and/holding (as in, 'taking and playing with')
notes: 가지다 (gajida) means 'to have' or 'to hold.' The -고 (-go) ending connects it to the following verb, indicating a sequential action or a continuous state. Here, it implies 'taking/holding [yaho] and playing [with it].' It's a common construction: 가지다 + 놀다 (to have/hold and play) often contracts to 갖고 놀다 (to play with something). Informal.
--- word ---
term: 노네?
type: Verb + informal declarative/question ending
breakdown: 놀다 (to play) + -네? (surprise/realization/question)
meaning: are playing, huh? / are they playing?
notes: 놀다 (nolda) means 'to play' or 'to hang out.' The -네 (-ne) ending is used to express mild surprise, realization, or observation. When used with a rising intonation (as indicated by the question mark), it can also form an informal question, often seeking confirmation or expressing a thought out loud. This is a casual, conversational ending.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 놀다 (V-go nolda)
meaning: To play with something, or to do something playfully/casually. Often implies a lighthearted or even somewhat careless interaction with the object/concept.
example: 인형을 갖고 놀다 (inhyeong-eul gatgo nolda) - 'to play with a doll.'
level: Intermediate

=== [30:10] 이거 지점토였어? ===

term: 이거
type: Pronoun (contraction)
breakdown: 이것 (this thing)
meaning: this / this thing
notes: 이거 (igeo) is a common colloquial contraction of 이것 (igeot), meaning 'this thing.' It's used very frequently in casual spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 지점토였어?

breakdown: 지점토 (clay) + 이다 (to be) + -었- (past tense) + -어? (informal question)
meaning: was it clay?

This ending is highly casual and used among close friends or when speaking down to someone (not the case here, as Hyejin is speaking to fans in a friendly, informal way).
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + 이다/아니다 (to be/not to be) past tense informal question
meaning: To ask 'Was it Noun?' in a casual, conversational way.
example: 학생이었어? (haksaeng-ieosseo?) - 'Were you a student?'

=== [30:31] 어 그거야 그거 ===
meaning: Oh, that's it, that's it.
tone: Informal, affirmative, confirming. Hyejin confirms something, possibly a fan's action or a previous statement.
--- word ---
term: 어
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: oh / uh
notes: 어 (eo) is a common interjection used to express surprise, realization, agreement, or simply to fill a pause. It's highly informal and conversational.
--- word ---
term: 그거야
type: Pronoun + copula + informal declarative ending (contraction)
breakdown: 그것 (that thing) + 이다 (to be) + -아 (informal declarative)
meaning: that's it / it is that
notes: 그거 (geugeo) is a colloquial contraction of 그것 (geugeot), meaning 'that thing.' The copula 이다 (ida, 'to be') is conjugated to its informal declarative form -이야 (-iya) after a consonant or -야 (-ya) after a vowel. Here, 그것 + 이야 becomes 그거야 (geugeoya). This is a very common way to confirm something in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 그거
type: Pronoun (contraction)
breakdown: 그것 (that thing)
meaning: that / that thing
notes: 그거 (geugeo) is a common colloquial contraction of 그것 (geugeot), meaning 'that thing.' Here, it's repeated for emphasis, reinforcing the confirmation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + 이야/야 (iya/ya)
meaning: Informal declarative form of the copula 이다 (ida, 'to be'). Used to state 'It is Noun' in a casual way. -이야 is used after a consonant-ending noun, -야 after a vowel-ending noun.
example: 내 친구야. (nae chinguya.) - 'She's my friend.'
level: Beginner

=== [30:33] 자 이제 나 5분 있다 갈 거야 ===
meaning: Okay, I'm going to leave in 5 minutes now.
tone: Informal, direct, announcing a departure to fans.
--- word ---
term: 자
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Okay; Well
notes: Used to get attention, start a new topic, or indicate a slight pause before continuing. It's informal and common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now; from now on
notes: Refers to 'now' in the sense of 'from this point forward' or 'at this stage'. It often implies a change or a new phase. Compare with 지금 (jigeum), which means 'right now' or 'at this very moment' without necessarily implying a change.
--- word ---
term: 나
type: pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I
notes: Informal first-person pronoun. Used among close friends or when speaking casually to a younger person or, in this context, to fans in a relaxed setting. The more formal equivalent is 저 (jeo).
--- word ---
term: 5분
type: noun
breakdown: 오분
meaning: 5 minutes
notes: A combination of the Sino-Korean number 오 (o, five) and 분 (bun, minute). Korean uses both native Korean numbers (하나, 둘, 셋...) and Sino-Korean numbers (일, 이, 삼...) depending on what is being counted. Time in minutes uses Sino-Korean numbers.
--- word ---
term: 있다
type: verb
breakdown: 
meaning: to be; to exist; to stay
notes: Here, it's used in the construction 'X 시간/분 있다 (가다/오다)' meaning 'to go/come after X time'. It implies a duration or a waiting period before the action.
--- word ---
term: 갈
type: verb ending
breakdown: 가다 + -ㄹ
meaning: will go (future tense modifier)
notes: This is the future tense adnominal ending -ㄹ/-을, which modifies the following noun (or implied noun). Here, it's part of the future tense informal ending -ㄹ 거야. The base verb is 가다 (gada), meaning 'to go'.
--- word ---
term: 거야
type: verb ending
breakdown: 것 + 이야
meaning: will (be)
notes: A contraction of 것 (geot, thing/fact) and 이야 (iya, informal form of 이다, to be). When combined with the future adnominal ending -ㄹ/-을, it forms the informal future tense -ㄹ/을 거야, meaning 'will do' or 'is going to do'. This ending is casual and commonly used in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -ㄹ/을 거야
meaning: This is an informal future tense ending, indicating a speaker's intention or a future event. It's a contraction of -ㄹ/을 것 + 이다 (to be).
example: 내일 갈 거야. (I will go tomorrow.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: X 시간/분 있다 (가다/오다)
meaning: This pattern means 'to go/come after X amount of time'. The verb 있다 here signifies a duration or a waiting period.
example: 10분 있다 올게. (I'll come in 10 minutes.)
level: Intermediate

meaning: see (exclamatory)

pattern: -어도/-아도

level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -네
meaning: An informal exclamatory ending used to express a sudden realization, observation, or mild surprise. It often conveys a sense of 'Oh, I see that...' or 'It turns out that...'.
example: 벌써 왔네! (Oh, you're already here!)

meaning: Indicates means, method, direction, or status/role. 'as/with/by means of.'

=== [32:45] 뭔가 막 울리는 게 아니니까 ===

breakdown: 
meaning: just, carelessly, wildly, suddenly
notes: The adverb 막 (mak) has multiple meanings. In this context, it implies 'just' or 'randomly/loudly,' suggesting an uncontrolled or unrefined action. Other meanings include 'just now,' 'carelessly,' 'wildly,' or 'suddenly.'
--- word ---
term: 울리는
type: verb (present participle)
breakdown: 울리다 (to ring, to make something resound) + -는 (present participle ending)
meaning: ringing / that rings
notes: 울리다 (ullida) means 'to ring' (e.g., a phone, an alarm) or 'to make something resound.' It can also be the causative form of 울다 (ulda - to cry). The -는 (neun) ending turns the verb into a descriptive clause, modifying the implied noun '것' (geot - thing).
--- word ---
term: 게
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 것 (thing) + -이 (subject particle)
meaning: thing (as subject)
notes: This is a common contraction of 것이 (geot-i). 것 (geot) is a dependent noun meaning 'thing' or 'fact,' and -이 (i) is the subject particle. It nominalizes the preceding clause, turning 'something that rings' into the subject of the sentence.
--- word ---

breakdown: 아니다 (to not be) + -니까 (because)
meaning: because it's not
notes: 아니다 (anida) is the descriptive verb 'to not be.' -니까 (nikka) is a conjunctive ending meaning 'because' or 'since.' It often implies that the reason is known to the listener or is a direct cause for the following action/statement. It's commonly used in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 것
meaning: This pattern nominalizes a verb, turning a verb phrase into a noun phrase. It often translates to 'the act of doing X' or 'X-ing.' When combined with the subject particle, it becomes -는 것이 or its contraction -는 게.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -니까
meaning: Expresses a reason or cause for the following statement or action. It often implies that the reason is a direct cause or is known to the listener. It can also be used to suggest a course of action based on the preceding reason.
level: Beginner

example: 여기서 담배 피우면 안 돼요. (You can't smoke here.)
level: Beginner

=== [33:26] 그리고 그 야호 소리에 출처는 ===

type: conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: and, then

--- word ---
term: 그
type: demonstrative adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: that
notes: Used to modify a noun, indicating something previously mentioned or known to both speaker and listener, but not necessarily close to either. Here, 'that yaho sound' refers to the sound they were just discussing.

meaning: yaho (exclamation)

--- word ---
term: 소리에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 소리 (sound) + -에 (possessive/locative particle)
meaning: of the sound, to the sound
notes: 소리 (sori) means 'sound'. The particle -에 (e) can indicate location, time, or, in this context, possession or association, meaning 'of the sound' or 'related to the sound'. It implies 'the source *of* the sound'.
--- word ---
term: 출처는
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 출처 (source) + -는 (topic particle)
meaning: the source (as topic)

Hyejin trails off, implying that the source (herself) should not be revealed, as per the STORY BIBLE context (segment 445-454: 'under the condition that they don't reveal the source').

=== [33:28] 그 누구에게도 밝히지 않아서 ===
meaning: Because you didn't reveal it to anyone.
tone: Informal, explanatory, slightly emphatic.
--- word ---
term: 그
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: that
notes: Used here to refer to something previously implied or understood, setting up the condition.
--- word ---
term: 누구에게도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 누구 + -에게도
meaning: to anyone
notes: 누구 (nugu) means 'who.' -에게 (ege) is a dative particle meaning 'to' or 'for' a person. -도 (do) means 'also' or 'even,' but when combined with a question word like 누구 and a negative verb, it creates the meaning 'not even anyone' or 'no one/anyone at all.' This is a common way to express 'not to anyone' or 'not to anybody' in Korean.
--- word ---
term: 밝히지
type: verb stem + negative connective
breakdown: 밝히다 + -지
meaning: reveal, disclose (and not)
notes: 밝히다 (balkida) means 'to reveal' or 'to disclose.' The -지 (ji) ending here connects to the negative auxiliary verb 않다 (anta) to form the negation -지 않다 (ji anta), meaning 'not to do something.' It's a common way to negate verbs and adjectives in Korean, often used in more formal or written contexts than the short negation (e.g., 안 밝히다).
--- word ---
term: 않아서
type: auxiliary verb + connective ending
breakdown: 않다 + -아서
meaning: because (you didn't)
notes: 않다 (anta) is the auxiliary verb for negation, meaning 'not to be' or 'not to do.' The -아서/어서 (aseo/eoseo) ending is a common connective ending used to indicate reason or cause, meaning 'because' or 'so.' The full construction -지 않아서 (ji anaseo) means 'because (one) did not do (something).'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-지 않다
meaning: A common way to negate a verb (V) or adjective in Korean, meaning 'not to V' or 'not to be A.'
example: 가지 않아요 (gaji anayo) - I'm not going.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-아서/어서
meaning: Connective ending used to express cause or reason, meaning 'because' or 'so.' It can also indicate a sequence of actions.
example: 배고파서 먹었어요 (baegopaseo meogeosseoyo) - I ate because I was hungry.
level: Beginner

=== [33:30] 그쵸? ===
meaning: Right?
tone: Informal, seeking confirmation, rhetorical.
--- word ---
term: 그쵸?
type: interjection/ending
breakdown: 그렇죠 (geureochyo)
meaning: Right? / Isn't it so?
notes: This is a common contraction of 그렇죠 (geureochyo), which itself is a polite form of 그렇다 (geureota, 'to be so') combined with the interrogative/confirmatory ending -죠 (jyo). It's used informally to seek agreement or confirmation, similar to 'right?' or 'isn't it?' in English. Hyejin uses this often in her casual interactions with fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -죠
meaning: A polite, informal ending used to confirm information, seek agreement, or state something obvious. It's a contraction of -지요.
example: 맛있죠? (masitjyo?) - It's delicious, right?
level: Beginner

=== [33:31] 이게 ===
meaning: This is...
tone: Informal, introductory, transitional.
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: pronoun + particle (contracted)
breakdown: 이것 + -이 + -가
meaning: this (subject)
notes: 이게 (ige) is a common contraction of 이것이 (igeosi), where 이것 (igeot) means 'this thing' and -이 (i) is the subject marker. It is used very frequently in spoken Korean in informal contexts. The full form 이것이 is more formal or emphatic.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 이것이 -> 이게
meaning: A common contraction in spoken Korean where the demonstrative pronoun 이것 (this thing) combines with the subject particle -이 to become 이게. Similarly, 그것이 becomes 그게, and 저것이 becomes 저게.
example: 이게 뭐야? (ige mwoya?) - What is this?
level: Beginner

=== [33:32] 나의 승낙 조건이라고 할 수 있어? ===
meaning: Can I say this is my condition for acceptance?
tone: Informal, questioning, rhetorical, playful.
--- word ---
term: 나의
type: pronoun + possessive particle
breakdown: 나 + -의
meaning: my
notes: 나 (na) is the informal first-person pronoun 'I.' -의 (ui) is the possessive particle, equivalent to 'of' or 'apostrophe s' in English. In spoken Korean, -의 is often omitted or replaced with 내 (nae) for 'my' (e.g., 내 조건 instead of 나의 조건), but 나의 is still common.
--- word ---
term: 승낙
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: acceptance, approval
notes: 승낙 (seungnak) refers to the act of accepting or approving something. This relates to the ongoing discussion about proposal ideas in the STORY BIBLE, where Hyejin is setting conditions for her 'acceptance.'
--- word ---
term: 조건이라고
type: noun + particle + verb ending
breakdown: 조건 + -이라고
meaning: as a condition, saying it's a condition
notes: 조건 (jogeon) means 'condition' or 'term.' -(이)라고 (irago) is a particle used to quote or define something, meaning 'saying it is X' or 'as X.' When the preceding noun ends in a consonant, -이라고 is used; if it ends in a vowel, -라고 is used. This is part of the -(이)라고 하다 (irago hada) pattern.
--- word ---
term: 할
type: verb (future/modifying form)
breakdown: 하다 + -ㄹ
meaning: to do, to say (future/potential)
notes: 하다 (hada) means 'to do' or 'to say.' The -ㄹ (l) ending here is a future/potential adnominal ending, modifying the following noun 수 (su, 'way/possibility'). It's part of the common grammar pattern -ㄹ 수 있다/없다.
--- word ---
term: 수
type: dependent noun
breakdown: 
meaning: way, possibility
notes: 수 (su) is a dependent noun that always follows a verb in the -ㄹ/을 form. It is used in the grammar pattern -ㄹ/을 수 있다/없다 to express ability or possibility.
--- word ---
term: 있어?
type: verb + interrogative ending
breakdown: 있다 + -어?
meaning: is there? / can (I)? (question)
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to exist' or 'to have.' When combined with -ㄹ 수, it means 'can' or 'to be able to.' The -어? (eo?) ending is an informal, casual interrogative ending used for questions among close friends or in casual settings. Hyejin uses this casual speech with her fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-(이)라고 하다
meaning: Used to quote or define something as a noun (N), meaning 'to say it is N' or 'to call it N.'
example: 이것은 책이라고 해요 (igeoseun chaegirago haeyo) - They call this a book.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄹ/을 수 있다/없다
meaning: Expresses ability or possibility ('can do' / 'cannot do'). -ㄹ 수 있다 is used if the verb stem ends in a vowel or ㄹ; -을 수 있다 if it ends in a consonant (not ㄹ).
example: 한국말 할 수 있어요 (hangungmal hal su isseoyo) - I can speak Korean.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-아/어?
meaning: An informal, casual interrogative ending used to ask questions. It is used with close friends or in very casual settings.
example: 밥 먹었어? (bap meogeosseo?) - Did you eat?
level: Beginner

=== [33:35] 알람도 안 돼 ===
meaning: Alarms are not allowed either.
tone: Informal, declarative, slightly strict/emphatic.
--- word ---
term: 알람도
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 알람 + -도
meaning: alarm, also / even alarm
notes: 알람 (allam) is a loanword from English 'alarm.' The particle -도 (do) means 'also,' 'too,' or 'even.' Here, it implies that in addition to other restrictions, alarms are also not allowed.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: 안 (an) is a short-form negation adverb placed directly before a verb or adjective to mean 'not.' It's a very common and informal way to negate in Korean, often interchangeable with the longer -지 않다 form, though 안 is generally more casual.
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: verb (contracted)
breakdown: 되다 + -어
meaning: to be allowed, to work, to become
notes: 돼 (dwae) is a common contraction of 되어 (doeeo), the informal present tense form of 되다 (doeda). 되다 has multiple meanings, including 'to become,' 'to be,' 'to work,' or 'to be allowed.' In this context, with negation (안 돼), it means 'it's not allowed' or 'it doesn't work.' This is a very common expression in daily conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 안 V/A
meaning: A short-form negation where 안 (not) is placed directly before a verb (V) or adjective (A).
example: 안 먹어요 (an meogeoyo) - I don't eat.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: 되다 -> 돼
meaning: The verb 되다 (to become, to be allowed, to work) often contracts to 돼 in its informal present tense form (되어 -> 돼). This is a very common contraction in spoken and written Korean.
example: 안 돼요 (an dwaeyo) - It's not allowed / It doesn't work (polite).
level: Beginner

=== [33:37] 아 배에서 자꾸 꼬르륵 소리나 ===
meaning: Ah, my stomach keeps making gurgling sounds.
tone: Informal, slightly embarrassed/humorous, personal observation. Relates to Hyejin having just woken up and it being lunchtime.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: ah, oh
notes: A common interjection used to express a sudden realization, surprise, or a slight complaint, similar to 'ah' or 'oh' in English.
--- word ---
term: 배에서
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 배 + -에서
meaning: from my stomach
notes: 배 (bae) means 'stomach' or 'belly.' -에서 (eseo) is a particle indicating the location where an action takes place or the starting point 'from' which something originates. Here, it indicates the sound is coming 'from' her stomach.
--- word ---
term: 자꾸
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: repeatedly, continuously, constantly
notes: 자꾸 (jakku) is an adverb used to describe an action that happens repeatedly or continuously, often implying a slight annoyance or persistence. It emphasizes that the sound isn't just a one-off.
--- word ---
term: 꼬르륵
type: onomatopoeia
breakdown: 
meaning: gurgle, rumble (sound)
notes: 꼬르륵 (kkoreureuk) is an onomatopoeic word that mimics the sound of a stomach rumbling or gurgling, often when one is hungry. Korean uses many such expressive sound words.
--- word ---
term: 소리나
type: noun + verb (contracted)
breakdown: 소리 + 나다
meaning: sound comes out / makes a sound
notes: 소리 (sori) means 'sound.' 나다 (nada) means 'to come out,' 'to occur,' or 'to happen.' The combination 소리가 나다 (soriga nada) means 'a sound comes out' or 'to make a sound.' In informal speech, the subject particle -가 (ga) is often omitted, making it 소리나 (sorina). This is a common way to express that a sound is being made.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N-에서
meaning: A particle used to indicate the location where an action occurs, or the starting point 'from' which something originates.

level: Beginner

meaning: The phrase 소리가 나다 (to make a sound, a sound comes out) is often contracted to 소리나 in informal spoken Korean by omitting the subject particle -가 (ga). This is a common feature of casual speech.
example: 이상한 소리나 (isanghan sorina) - A strange sound is coming out.

example: 맛있네! (It's delicious, I see!)

=== [34:18] 아 근데 아침에 딱 일어나면 ===

=== [34:20] 커피 한 잔이 그렇게 힐링이란 말이지 ===
